The planned product is a Windows GUI that injects an owned 3DS game into a playable Wii U WUP. Current releases are original diagnostics; commercial games and the GUI remain unfinished. This guide records evidence at its source version; Project #2 owns current tracking and acceptance. Reading and search work offline. External source/issue links need internet and may require repository access. Source commit: 0b5e01cd0abcb9fe2db1c00408b6f6584151cabb.

CTR2WUP¶

CI results

Documentation · Releases · Project · Contributing

First visit? Take the repository tour.

CTR2WUP is an experimental 3DS-to-Wii U converter. Its goal is a Windows GUI that translates a 3DS game into an individual WUP title, with the top screen on the TV and the bottom screen, touch and controls on the GamePad.

Early development: commercial games, playable-game WUP conversion and the Windows GUI are not available yet. Current releases provide developer tools and original homebrew diagnostics. Super Mario 3D Land is the first planned compatibility target.

Approach¶

The PC translates ARM game code ahead of time into C, which is compiled for Wii U PowerPC. A shared native runtime supplies the required 3DS services, graphics, input, audio and saves. The intended workflow is:

Select game → Configure title and artwork → Inject → Install WUP → Play

Support will expand as the shared runtime implements more game requirements. See architecture and compatibility for the supported subsets and current limits.

Current capabilities¶

  • Bounded 3DS/CXI/CIA inspection, optional local decryption with supplied keys, RomFS integrity checks and private asset preparation.
  • ARM/Thumb/VFP translation foundations, checked memory, guest threads and scoped 3DS services.
  • Original compiled homebrew with two-screen framebuffer output, input, SD save/reload and read-only assets, including reviewed Wii U test results.
  • Texture decoding, GX memory/display transfers, PICA configuration and first original software triangle scenes, now driven by a compiled guest through standard GSP. Broader PICA state, GPU shader translation and 3DS guest audio remain unfinished.
  • Reproducible RPX/WUHB diagnostics and original packaging fixtures. Actual WUP installation and full Windows conversion still require acceptance.

Progress¶

Live Project progress

The chart is generated from live Project fields and refreshed hourly. It shows task status separately from full milestone acceptance. Click it for the interactive dashboard and update time; GitHub may cache the embedded image.

Development Project tracks the seven milestones and their implementation/evidence tasks. Completing a task does not accept a full milestone. See the roadmap and current status for scope and verification details.

Development tracking explains the native milestone → task → feature → PR relationships and the contributor workflow.

Getting started¶

Download a matching development release and check its SHA256SUMS.txt. For Wii U diagnostics, copy the kit's sd-card/ contents to your SD root and launch the WUHB in Aroma. Follow its prompts; plain-text results are saved to sd:/ctr2wup/. Original diagnostics need no ROM or key. See getting started and console testing.

For development, use Python 3.12+, GNU Make 4.2+ and a C compiler. Host checks run without the SDK. Its first setup also requires Docker. From the repository root:

python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
python3 -m pip install -e '.[packaging,decryption]'
make help
python3 -m host doctor
make check
bash tools/install-wiiu-sdk.sh
python3 -m host build --target prototype --output "artifacts/my-diagnostic"

This builds an original RPX/WUHB diagnostic. Build instructions, Windows notes and compiled homebrew cover other workflows. Generated files stay under ignored artifacts/; local ROMs, keys and derived game data belong under ignored private/.

Contributing¶

Focused fixes, original reference fixtures, documentation and reproducible console reports are welcome. Start with Contributing, open work items and the Project. Report bugs with build identity and relevant test results; keep game data and keys out of issues. Security reports follow SECURITY.md.

License and acknowledgments¶

CTR2WUP source is MIT licensed. Dependencies retain their own licenses; see THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES and the dependency audit. Builds use devkitPro, WUT and libctru. The planned injector workflow is inspired by UWUVCI AIO.

Maintained source: README.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

First visit to the repository¶

CTR2WUP aims to create a playable per-game Wii U WUP through a Windows injector GUI. Today the repository provides reusable runtime foundations, developer tools and original homebrew diagnostics. Commercial gameplay, full WUP installation acceptance and the final GUI remain open.

Source map¶

Location Responsibility
host/ Shared CLI, intake, build stages, package checks and diagnostic workflows
translator/ Original translation fixtures and the checked external lifter adapter
runtime/core/ Portable checked guest memory, CPU, kernel, services and software renderer
runtime/recomp/ Generated-code ABI, dispatch and observable execution boundary
runtime/wiiu/ WUT platform, GX2 display copies, VPAD/AX and original native diagnostics
tests/ Original host/C fixtures; tests/homebrew/ contains normal-CRT ARM programs
tools/ Verified SDK/dependency setup, validators, documentation and trusted GitHub management
.github/ Required CI, publication, issue/PR templates and repository policy
docs/ Current contracts, plans, acceptance gates and recorded evidence
artifacts/, private/ Ignored generated output and private inputs; never application source

runtime/core/sources.list is the canonical portable guest runtime manifest. The test Makefiles, native build and Python build/validation adapters consume it; add a shared runtime module there once. Consumer-specific fixtures and platform helpers remain in their own recipes. Standalone component suites keep narrow lists.

First host check¶

Use Python 3.12+, GNU Make 4.2+ and a C compiler. The SDK and external lifter are optional for this first check:

python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
python3 -m pip install -e '.[packaging,decryption]'
make help
python3 -m host doctor
make check

For an original native diagnostic, follow the SDK setup, then run make prototype. For a compiled original ARM program, follow compiled homebrew; its validator requires the pinned libctru source, devkitARM and external lifter. These checks use no commercial ROM.

What counts as progress¶

The repository milestone is the gate, its task is a substantial work area, and a scoped feature records one reviewable change. Native parent and PR links connect them in GitHub. A merged feature can be Done while its task and milestone remain open. Project status, checked source provenance and explicit evidence limits make that distinction visible without relying on a chat transcript.

Maintained source: docs/repository-guide.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Guided Wii U tests and TXT results (0.14.6)¶

This page preserves the original 0.14.6 controls and reporting contract. The current physical results and focused follow-up are in console-evidence.md and console-retest.md. Save/RomFS, launch audio and bounded guest draws have reviewed scoped results; no long guide repetition is requested solely for a version bump.

Launch¶

For Aroma, copy the kit's sd-card/ contents to the SD root. Apps belong in sd:/wiiu/apps/. An RPX-capable launcher can use the matching rpx/ files. Keep the existing sd:/ctr2wup/ folder and saves. Use your current launcher.

Each app displays BUILD 0.14.6, RESULT VERIFIED and its new filename. VERIFIED means the current 4 KiB TXT was closed and compared byte for byte after reopening. It does not prove survival of sudden power loss. The separate TRACE SAVING / FAILED status describes the longer trace, not the result file. A trace failure is captured in the result. RESULT STORAGE FAILED / SD ERROR pauses the guest; exit with Minus and report the operation/number and any files.

Prompts¶

  • Button/stick/touch inputs, guest PASS/exit and save reload are checked automatically.
  • R confirms the displayed picture, alignment, sound or stability observation; L marks a problem. These are labeled user evidence.
  • ZL skips a step as NOT TESTED; ZR marks a problem and advances.
  • Minus ends the test and writes the available summary. Unfinished steps stay NOT TESTED. Results are also written at startup and throughout execution.
  • Use Plus when prompted for guest restart/save reload. Y/unscheduled Plus remain reserved while a guide is active.

Only Native plays the platform tone on A. Compiled apps use A as guest input and implement no guest audio. The interactive A prompt waits for a fresh decoded green frame before allowing R and records latency separately from confirmation. Native and interactive full guides include a ten-minute observation interval; its completion is separate from the user's final stability confirmation.

Compiled Save preserves the existing diagnostic snapshot, checks its own guest save/reload and asks for Plus. It may request one exit/relaunch for a fresh-process check. Compiled RomFS automatically checks original asset reads and exits after one step. No photo is needed to report these software checks.

Files to return¶

All reports are under sd:/ctr2wup/:

result-native-0.14.6-<ticks>-<suffix>.txt
result-compiled-interactive-0.14.6-<ticks>-<suffix>.txt
result-compiled-guarded-0.14.6-<ticks>-<suffix>.txt
result-compiled-save-0.14.6-<ticks>-<suffix>.txt
result-compiled-romfs-0.14.6-<ticks>-<suffix>.txt

These fixed-size plain-text results contain named PASS / FAIL / NOT TESTED guide outcomes and their evidence, build/session identity, embedded-check status, guest instructions/PC/fault/fallback, input counters, green-response latency, diagnostic frame timing, save load/commit status and full-trace errors. Trailing space padding is intentional. Later updates verify the reserved file identity; missing or replaced reports are not silently recreated. Per-launch exclusive names preserve older results.

The separate longer traces use native-*.txt, compiled-interactive-*.txt, compiled-guarded-*.txt, compiled-save-*.txt and compiled-romfs-*.txt. Their successful commits sync/close/flush the SD volume. Neither a cleanup marker nor a saved result proves that the launcher finished returning; add that observation.

Upload the new result TXT and any matching full trace in this chat, directly or in a ZIP below 32 MiB. For save testing, include the snapshot and any .pending / .previous siblings, preserving the originals. If nothing exists, say so and include the short on-screen storage error. See console-retest.md for this round. The console environment, launcher version, SD format/capacity and TV resolution help when diagnosing failures; do not resend already supplied details.

Local tests verify report write/read-back, failed/missing/replaced-file handling, interrupted process evidence, guide outcomes and lifecycle ordering. Builds use the actual Wii U SDK. v0.14.6 result persistence and save updates are awaiting physical evidence. These diagnostics do not verify PICA, guest audio, retail saves, Mario gameplay or WUP installation.

Maintained source: docs/guided-testing.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Architecture and implementation boundaries¶

The attached development brief is the project requirements. The repository was empty at the start of this implementation. See foundation-audit.md for pinned candidate source, measured builds, and licensing/PowerPC constraints.

The host CLI is Python 3.12; inspection/runtime tooling uses the standard library and encrypted packaging optionally uses cryptography. It uses the same argument-list subprocess interface on Windows and Linux. Container parsing, inspection, build reports, and package inspection are separate from execution. Executable artifacts are the original native diagnostic and controlled guest RPXs, plus the separate .NET CIA intake console utility cross-published for Windows x64. An RPX is not an installable WUP directory.

The portable vertex module resolves explicitly supplied array streams into shader-input vectors. Its contiguous and indexed entry points share layout/range/value validation and commit output only after the complete request is valid. The indexed path reads unsigned 8-bit or little-endian 16-bit elements, preserving order and duplicates without reading unreferenced vertices. It does not follow guest addresses or implement PICA loader state, fixed attributes, float24/shader arithmetic, primitive assembly or GX2 submission.

The separate original scene boundary now adapts bounded PICA loader/index/fixed/uniform/output state to that decoder and an original MOV/NOP/END shader executor. It stages owned registers/resources and a complete linear target, then rasterizes complete triangles with exact supported numeric values and constant opaque colors. A late error rolls back all state and pixels. The focused native Scene Check presents these CPU-produced scenes with existing GX2 surface copies. Guest GSP draws and general GPU shader translation remain unsupported; explicit resource binding is not guest physical-address resolution.

The CIA intake path uses bounded signature-layout/TMD/bitmap/content checks and selects index-0 application NCCH with checked absolute file offsets. Its derived CXI view preserves ExeFS/exheader code identity while explicitly removing RomFS and header signature. The standalone .NET helper validates stored hashes; the shared Python engine also validates BLZ/decompressed revision. This private view supports analysis through a 32 MiB attachment constraint, not game execution or asset staging. See cia-intake.md for supported profile and limitations.

The portable C core stores guest addresses as 32-bit integers and accesses bytes through checked mappings. Guest memory is explicitly little-endian on every host. The initial interpreter and direct generated-C backend are bounded CPU subsets with explicit unsupported exits. host/loader.py extracts inspected NCCH segments; the portable loader allocates checked permissions/stack/TLS/BSS. A single-guest runtime implements bounded SVC sleep/events/handles/ticks/debug/exit. Version 0.6.0 adds user read-only CP15 TLS access, typed/refcounted session handles, checked TLS IPC transport and restricted SRV/CFG startup commands. Its original ARM probe reads configured region/model/language through all three execution paths; see shared-services.md. This is shared runtime code, not a game patch. Version 0.7.0 adds owned heap/linear CPU allocation, atomic partial free/protection and queries; guest-memory.md defines the bounded contract. Ordinary heap changes preserve AOT snapshots; executable writes/aliases and execute protection still invalidate them. GPU/DSP physical memory remains pending. The original ARM fixture writes both framebuffers and responds to input through an explicit harness contract. Neither implements complete ARM11/VFP, a full 3DS kernel, general IPC or game HLE. The pinned external lifter now generates C through an original host driver. An original ABI 4 implementation statically executes that code using checked little-endian callbacks and per-instruction state; see upstream-integration.md. Its desktop runtime is not a Wii U dependency.

The native WUT backend provides independent TV and GamePad diagnostic output, input, sound, process lifecycle, timing, and CPU self-test instrumentation. v0.14.6 reserves a separate fixed-size 4 KiB result TXT independently of WHB trace handlers. Every update checks the reserved identity, writes, closes and reads back all bytes; named guide outcomes persist in the result even if the full trace fails. First errors are sticky; result-storage failure pauses guest input/execution, while full-trace failure is reported independently. Result writes happen at startup/self-tests/guest/checkpoint/guide/save/periodic/cleanup boundaries. Guest state is captured before close so cleanup cannot erase its final stats. Final result writes precede system transition.

The longer trace retains v0.14.5 fflush/fsync, checked close, mounted-volume flush and existing-file reopen. Handler registration is now checked. WUT owns the SD mount; the diagnostic releases its extra FSA client before transition. Neither exact read-back nor desktop abrupt-exit tests establish SD power-loss durability. The user reports successful v0.14.5 Native/Guarded/Compiled runs but no new logs; the uploaded text reports are older. Cause is unknown. See console-evidence.md.

v0.14.4 caches bounded nearest-neighbor coordinate maps and reuses scaled rows, with byte-order-independent RGBA output, checked source/output capacity and black borders/padding. The native backend flushes/invalidates the produced GX2 surfaces. The guide waits for a fresh decoded green frame after input before offering R, and records A-to-green delay separately from user visual confirmation. AOT execution returns a missing-block fault after 64 consecutive dispatches with no guest instruction progress, so empty/unwinding entries cannot consume unlimited host time without spending their budget. This guard cannot recover GPU/platform hangs and has not been attributed to the console freeze. Varied-clock live-input replay checks original compiled red/green transitions and exit on desktop. v0.14.3 polls ProcUI directly, suspends input/rendering outside foreground and releases guest/owned AX/GX2/report resources before ProcUIShutdown. On Minus it cleans up, clears stale graphics callbacks while retaining save acknowledgement, requests Menu and polls to EXITING; HBL relaunch follows cleanup/shutdown. WUT's devoptab owns SD mounting. Checked TXT records include lifecycle states and bounded input/guest/render checkpoints; end-of-report does not establish menu return. Native AX ownership is explicit; compiled apps do not use AX. The guarded AOT comparison uses a call limit of two instead of the standard 64, with the existing unwind/dispatcher. Exact desktop frames/input/exit match; depth, unwinds and frame-boundary stack headroom are diagnostic evidence, not proof of a console stack fault. See console-retest.md. All diagnostic builds also contain the original TLS/SRV/CFG startup self-test with a visible result and SD log. Unsupported cache operations fail visibly instead of inheriting the external emitter's no-op behavior. All builds also embed the original ARM heap lifecycle self-test and its SD log. Version 0.8.0 adds shared/mutex/semaphore objects and wait-any/all, with mapping/ wait ownership independent of handles. kernel-objects.md defines its single-thread scope. Its original ARM self-test is also embedded. Version 0.9.0 exposes standard read-only HID pad/touch rings and events. An independent original ARM consumer in each Wii U build reads live VPAD input through that service; hid-input.md defines its bounds and absent motion support. Version 0.10.0 adds checked direct GSP framebuffer submission and portable rotated RGBA8/BGR8/RGB565/RGB5A1/RGBA4 conversion. A separate original ARM guest reads HID, writes RGB565 and displays through this interface in all Wii U builds; see gsp-display.md. Version 0.11.0 adds one local GSP relay/shared framebuffer page, diagnostic VBlank and atomic queued presentation; see gsp-queues.md. The standard display guest uses this queue. Version 0.17 adds scoped synchronous GX commands; see gx-transfers.md. Version 0.23 connects bounded compiled guest PICA draws with owned physical resources, canonical tiled targets and atomic completion; software execution remains separate from native GPU shader translation. Its guest mode presents completed ARM-written raw framebuffers through the same GX2 copying path. It is separate from PICA/GSP emulation; see guest-fixture.md. Version 0.12 adds checked user-mode ARM/Thumb multiple-register/stack transfers and BLX calls, with an embedded original nested-call self-test and independent ARM1176 reference checks; see cpu-stack.md. Compilation proves toolchain integration. Only physical console results can prove rendering, input, sound, guest semantics on PowerPC, or performance.

Version 0.13 adds eight single-core guest contexts with strict priorities, instruction quanta, CPU/TLS/raw VFP/FPSCR isolation and retained wait/stack ownership. Thread joins, owner-aware locks, address arbiters and local quota queries support normal compiled libctru threads/startup. BSS shares the final DATA page correctly; a read-only synthetic kernel configuration page supplies explicit HLE policy. See guest-threads.md for supported SVCs and limits. compiled-homebrew.md records normal devkitARM CRT/newlib/libctru applications, checked three-frame CPU display, standard input and a fourth interactive Wii U build. The v0.13 path left APT/FS startup requests visibly unavailable; v0.14 adds explicit standalone APT and platform-owned private FS volumes with checked archive/file sessions and snapshot commits. Guest filenames never address the host filesystem. The original compiled test proves save/read/fresh-process reload; see filesystem.md for supported commands and remaining commercial boundaries. It does not execute PICA or supply commercial compatibility.

v0.14.6 adds an explicit borrowed 64-bit read-only current-process Level-3 RomFS view. Checked FSFILE transport exposes bytes; unmodified libctru resolves nested and Unicode names/directories in guest code. Lifetime and read publication are checked, with no host path exposure. v0.16 adds canonical NCCH/IVFC verification and asset preparation; signature authentication and commercial asset integration remain open; see romfs-assets.md.

Save publication reserves .pending, moves a validated primary to absent .previous, publishes into the vacant primary name, then removes the backup. Failed publish attempts rollback; absent-primary load can recover a validated backup. Corruption and ambiguous siblings remain visible failures. This avoids requiring FSA rename replacement; it is not a concurrent-writer/power-loss guarantee.

A read-only shared time page at 1ff81000 uses two banks and a published sequence, explicit synthetic Unix-zero epoch, guest-clock progression and matching 268111856 Hz ticks. It is separate from kernel policy/TLS and counts as an additional 4 KiB backing allocation. ARM single-data byte/halfword/word accesses use checked little-endian U=1/A=0 unaligned semantics; other instruction classes stay strictly aligned. Defined lowest-base STM writeback is supported. Reference and generated original/external AOT checks are recorded in cpu-stack.md.

renderer/ contains bounded command inspection and small format/presentation references. Capturing PICA register writes does not execute a draw. An explicit exception reports the absent shader/texture/TEV/GX2 translation path.

packaging/ inspects original/supplied package structure and title identities. Content structure, cryptographic authenticity, and installer acceptance are different checks. There is no bundled common key and no unlicensed packer. Original stage-title metadata/artwork is a packaging prerequisite. Its output is explicitly not an installable package. Version 0.4.0 implements scoped encrypted FST/content/TMD/ticket construction with user-local inputs, and a separate streaming decrypting verifier. The original four-content profile has no authentic signatures and awaits installer/console acceptance. No key or certificate is bundled; offline proof uses temporary synthetic inputs only.

Generated artifacts are ignored by Git. User ROMs, saves, extracted assets, translated commercial code, keys, and packages must stay under ignored private directories. Synthetic fixtures are original instructions/data generated from source; no commercial assets are distributed.

Execution gates¶

  1. Native RPX compile and desktop tests can be completed in this environment.
  2. Physical RPX screen/input/audio/exit tests and PowerPC CPU results are pending.
  3. Checked loading and bounded guest-thread/kernel services now execute original compiled libctru CPU/input/framebuffer applications. Console stability, physical interface evidence and wider APT/FS/GX service semantics are needed.
  4. PICA rendering, required services/modules, filesystem/save/audio semantics, and exact-ROM revision profiles must precede a commercial conversion.
  5. License-cleared packing plus locally configured keys and actual install/menu launch tests must precede a WUP conversion claim.
  6. The Windows GUI follows a demonstrated execution pipeline; it is deferred.

The interpreter implements ARMv6 SXTB/SXTH/UXTB/UXTH register extensions, ROR

0/8/16/24, conditional execution and destination/source aliasing without flag¶

changes. PC operands and adding/paired forms remain unsupported. Unit/sanitizer checks and 2,048 Unicorn ARM1176 cases validate this scope. Full compiled libctru interpreter startup still stops on STRD, so no interpreter comparison is shipped. For the optional reference, install unicorn==2.1.4 in a developer Python environment, then run python3 tools/reference-extend.py --output NEW_DIRECTORY.

v0.15.0 adds input preparation ahead of container inspection. A bounded local key provider loads selected 16-byte file references in-process, with redacted failures/repr. Plaintext support-file imports create a separate explicitly private store. CIA AES-CBC main-content hashes are checked before inner NCCH decryption; NCCH v2 primary/secondary CTR, seed/key derivation and extents are checked before publishing a derived CXI. Output includes the main RomFS, but not other contents, tickets or key files. Input/output identities and lack of signature authentication are explicit; v0.16 verifies the full canonical IVFC tree when RomFS is present. Read-only sources, bounded chunks, input-change checks and atomic non-overwriting publication reuse the existing host boundaries. The validated decrypted CXI enters the unchanged loader/lifter pipeline; key values never reach generated C, runtime binaries or WUP staging. See encrypted-input.md for profiles and pending hardware/Windows checks.

v0.16 separates validated asset preparation from execution. The host verifies NCCH's anchored RomFS superblock, the complete canonical IVFC tree, every file hash and the bounded Level-3 directory/hash metadata. It atomically exports one Level-3 romfs.bin plus a manifest, never host paths derived from guest names. The file provider streams SHA-256 once at open, checks the configured expected hash and lends a read-only view to existing FS transport. It uses standard-C fseek/ftell, so the Wii U/32-bit limit is LONG_MAX, and requires immutable backing during execution. It owns no extra SD mount/client. Guest teardown releases mounts/references before provider close and system transition.

The portable texture decoder converts bounded base-level PICA tiles to explicit RGBA bytes without native guest-word loads. It preflights compressed blocks, lengths and aliases before publication. The Python/reference tools and emulated PPC checks establish decoder behavior, not GX2 upload/sampling or draw execution. See romfs-assets.md and renderer-status.md for the supported contracts.

v0.17.0 adds a standard client-0 GSP GX ring dispatcher and synchronous CPU-backed operations. DMA/fill/display conversion/raw copy stage source/outputs and validate all spans before command mutation; output cannot touch retained relay backing through any guest alias. Complete-range bulk memory APIs copy adjacent mappings, reject host/guest backing alias and update exclusive/code generations correctly. Successful commands advance and signal only enqueued completions; errors preserve the failing entry and later work while retaining an already committed prefix. PICA command lists, physical/VRAM translation and asynchronous GPU coherency are outside that contract. gx-transfers.md records accepted wire flags/dimensions.

GitHub now separates source-reviewed runtime evidence from hosted CI and physical testing. Managed milestone issue sections derive from checked-in status data; PR/check/artifact/release records never automatically close hardware acceptance. Repository instructions and agent profiles preserve the same boundaries.

Maintained source: docs/architecture.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Tracking development from the repository¶

The Development Project is the working board. The seven milestone checklists define full product acceptance. Every implementation has a scoped feature issue, a native parent, a repository milestone and an associated pull request.

Follow an implementation¶

  1. Open a milestone overview, then its native sub-issues.
  2. Open the relevant work task, then its scoped feature children.
  3. Read the feature's scope, acceptance criteria and evidence limits.
  4. Open Development / Linked pull requests to inspect the actual change, checks, commits and branch. PRs also appear in the Project's Pull requests view.

Text references alone do not establish native links. The management workflow creates and verifies GitHub parent relationships and closing PR references. Historical PRs have retrospective feature issues; those issues describe bounded delivered checkpoints rather than implying the whole parent task is complete.

Contributor workflow¶

Create a scoped feature under an existing task before starting implementation. Assign its M1–M7 repository milestone and add it to Project #2. Give it concrete scope, reproducible acceptance checks and explicit limits. Add its stable identity and PR relationship to the reviewed feature catalog, docs/features.json.

Work on a focused branch, commit attributable checkpoints and push a draft PR early. Link the scoped issue with Closes #...; reference broader unfinished tasks with Refs #.... Keep the PR in the same milestone and Project. After required checks and review, merge and delete both remote and local topic branches.

Run the trusted-main management dry-run and apply after opening the draft. For an already planned feature, the tool can discover its new PR through the native closing reference even while the catalog edit is on the topic branch. It reads GitHub relationships and never executes that branch. An ambiguous relationship, unknown feature or active PR extending a closed feature fails before writes.

Done means the scoped acceptance criteria were met. It does not mean a parent task, console route or full milestone was accepted. Full gates need their entire documented checklist and reviewed evidence. Physical testing remains pending when only host checks or RPX/WUHB builds exist.

Reviewed management and audit¶

Run Development traceability on main with apply=false for a read-only preflight, then apply=true for the reviewed catalog. Both runs use an immutable trusted-main checkout; the management credential remains an Actions secret. No arbitrary branch, issue body or workflow input is executed with that credential.

The tool backfills native parents for the 35 established tasks, creates bounded feature issues, links PRs, assigns milestones and ensures Project membership. It fails before writes on conflicting native parents or a historical feature whose PR has not merged. Its private artifact records verified live relationships and any partial operation failure; a failed run must be reviewed before retrying.

The initial status migration only replaces old empty/Todo values. Later manual Project status edits remain authoritative. Merged scoped features become Done; no full gate becomes Accepted through this workflow. The hourly Project tracking workflow publishes safe aggregates separately and does not expose private issue titles, bodies or feature identifiers in the public dashboard.

Next renderer features¶

Feature Parent task Acceptance focus
Compiled original guest draws #27 Standard GSP queue, exact targets, rollback and retained state
Vertex shader arithmetic #28 Original numeric references and unsupported-state rejection
TEV fragment combinations #29 Independent fragment reference for supported operations
Texture sampling #29 Checked owned descriptors, wrap/filter rules and exact scenes
Resource coherency #30 Memory generations, invalidation and readback correctness
GX2 shader translation #28 Native ABI, explicit shader misses and comparison route
Physical scene comparison #31 Exact-build Wii U results, repeat launch/exit and measured costs

These are independently reviewable features. Completing a software draw path does not establish general PICA compatibility or native GPU translation.

The v0.23 command review completes only task #27 after its two criteria are verified against merged PR #48/#57 and original reference traces. Its review marker is recorded once; later maintainer reopen/status changes are preserved. No full milestone or other unreviewed task can complete through that rule.

Maintained source: docs/development-tracking.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Contributing to CTR2WUP¶

CTR2WUP targets a Windows GUI that converts an owned 3DS game into a playable Wii U WUP. Current builds are development tools and original diagnostics; the commercial execution pipeline and GUI are still being implemented. Read STATUS.md, the milestones and AGENTS.md before proposing compatibility or acceptance changes.

Choose verifiable work¶

Check existing issues and the milestone checklist. For a bug, provide a minimal reproduction with original data. For a feature, describe the resulting behavior, its milestone and acceptance evidence. The issue forms also accept Wii U test results, including missing reports, failed storage checks and hangs.

Use development tracking for native milestone → task → scoped feature → PR relationships. Assign the repository milestone and Project membership, give the feature a native parent, and record its PR in docs/features.json. Broad unfinished tasks remain open when a feature merges.

Do not upload ROMs, key databases, personal keys, extracted game assets, saves, translated commercial code, game packages or credentials. Keep them under ignored private/ directories locally. Original diagnostic TXT files are useful after reviewing them for private paths or data. Use SECURITY.md for vulnerability reports instead of a normal issue.

Build and check¶

Use Python 3.12+, GNU Make 4.2+ and a C compiler. Host checks do not need the Wii U SDK; start with make help and make check. Install the verified Wii U SDK with the repository script; the first installation requires Docker. Optional encrypted input/packaging tests use the cryptography dependency. The target SDK uses devkitPro's devkitPPC, WUT and Wii U tools; the host compiler runs desktop tests.

python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
python3 -m pip install -e '.[packaging,decryption]'
export CTR2WUP_SDK_ROOT="$PWD/../ctr2wup-sdk"
bash tools/install-wiiu-sdk.sh
export DEVKITPRO="$CTR2WUP_SDK_ROOT"
export DEVKITPPC="$DEVKITPRO/devkitPPC"
make check
make prototype

make check runs Python and portable C suites with sanitizer checks. make prototype builds RPX/WUHB diagnostics. Neither command proves physical Wii U behavior or WUP installation. An existing official devkitPro installation can be used by setting DEVKITPRO and DEVKITPPC to its SDK paths. For Windows, use devkitPro's MSYS2 environment and signed wiiu-dev packages; the extracted Linux SDK cannot run natively on Windows. Consult Windows build notes, upstream integration and the focused test suites for requirements specific to your change. SDK installation must preserve checksum, signature and TLS verification.

Console evidence and diagnostic releases¶

Use the versioned original diagnostic build and instructions for the requested test round. Record its release tag or commit, displayed application version and artifact checksum where available. A newer source version does not establish that an older console result tested the new behavior.

For Aroma, copy a diagnostic kit's sd-card/ contents to the SD root; WUHB apps belong under sd:/wiiu/apps/. Matching RPX files require an RPX-capable launcher. These files are homebrew applications, not WUP installation directories. Follow guided testing for controls and the expected sd:/ctr2wup/ TXT results. Preserve failed-run reports and explicitly report when no new file appeared. Include exact firmware, Aroma and launcher versions when known, plus observed exit/Menu behavior.

Published diagnostic releases and CI build artifacts demonstrate only their documented original fixture/build checks. Commercial games, broader PICA rendering, guest audio and installed playable WUP output still have separate acceptance gates in the milestone checklist.

Submit a pull request¶

Keep the change focused and explain the concrete before/after behavior. Link its issue or milestone, record relevant checks and identify unavailable prerequisites. Use the pull request template. Update affected status, architecture, compatibility and build documentation when behavior or evidence changes; do not claim console execution from compilation. Record evidence in versioned reports where the existing workflow calls for them.

Develop on a separate topic branch and push a draft PR early. Link its scoped feature using Closes #..., then keep the PR and issue visible in the same milestone and Project. Merge only after required checks pass. Delete and verify the completed remote and local topic branches after every merge.

New fixtures must be original and reproducible from source. Guest memory is little-endian even on big-endian PowerPC; avoid native guest-word loads and keep unsupported states explicit. Review licenses and notices before adding a dependency or redistributing an executable. Generated builds belong under ignored artifacts/; inspect release contents for private data before publication.

Agent-assisted work¶

Repository Copilot instructions and specialist profiles under .github/agents provide CPU/runtime, host-pipeline, graphics and console-evidence context. Supported clients can select them when Copilot access is available. These files do not turn on paid services or autonomous jobs. Agent changes receive the same review and validation as any contribution; their summaries must identify pending physical and commercial acceptance checks.

Codex commit attribution¶

For work generated by Codex, install the opt-in hook in this checkout:

python3 tools/codex-attribution.py install
git codex-commit -m "Describe the concrete change"

The wrapper records Codex as author using the verified OpenAI-owned ChatGPT Codex Connector bot identity. The hook adds OpenAI's published trailer exactly once: Co-authored-by: Codex <noreply@openai.com>. The published Codex attribution source records the commit and PR formats used here. Add Generated with [Codex](https://openai.com/codex/). to the associated PR body.

Ordinary git commit retains your identity and message. Installation is local to this repository, preserves global configuration and refuses conflicting hooks or aliases. Use python if that is your Windows Python command. The public bot email identifies commit authorship; it supplies no bot login, signature or access. Pushes, PR creation and merges still use the connected account. Existing history is preserved; GitHub decides how bot authors appear in contributor statistics.

Maintained source: CONTRIBUTING.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

CTR2WUP documentation¶

CTR2WUP is developing an UWUVCI-style Windows GUI that turns an owned 3DS game into an individual playable Wii U WUP. Current releases contain original homebrew diagnostics and developer tooling. Commercial games and the injector GUI are not supported yet.

The single HTML guide in development releases can be downloaded and opened directly in a browser, with offline search, navigation and printing. The source guides below also remain readable in GitHub. See documentation hosting for the free docs-only site and build receipts.

Start here¶

  • Repository tour: reader routes, source map and first host check.

  • Development tracking: native feature/task/milestone/PR workflow.

  • Getting started: downloads, Aroma launch, SD layout and reports.

  • Troubleshooting: missing reports, storage failures and hangs.
  • Testing: which checks establish desktop, Windows and console evidence.
  • Current status and progress assessment.
  • Live development Project for authoritative task status, blockers and reviewed milestone acceptance.
  • GitHub development: CI, releases, issues, agents and permissions.
  • Contributing and security reports.

Build and development¶

  • Build on Windows and environment/toolchain audit.
  • Architecture, product plan and GUI workflow.
  • Milestone acceptance and remaining inputs.
  • CPU/AOT integration, compiled homebrew, threads, memory and kernel objects.

Runtime and preparation contracts¶

  • Renderer status, GX transfers, framebuffer display, shared GSP/VBlank and input.
  • Compiled guest draws: standard GSP queue, exact two-screen captures, owned resources and transactional completion.
  • First software scenes: checked loader/MOV shader/raster boundary, independent exact images and focused Scene Check console guide.
  • Filesystem/saves, RomFS assets and application lifecycle.
  • Local encrypted input, decryptor investigation, CIA intake and title staging/packaging.

Evidence and history¶

Compatibility describes supported boundaries. Console evidence separates supplied logs from verbal reports. Original fresh-launch save/results/assets are verified; the focused launch-audio regression remains open. Development history preserves earlier implementation notes and receipts; its old pending statements are historical.

No desktop pass, CI badge or RPX build establishes commercial gameplay, hardware performance or successful WUP installation. Keep ROMs, keys, saves, game traces and extracted commercial assets private.

Maintained source: docs/README.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Getting started¶

CTR2WUP currently runs original diagnostic applications, not commercial 3DS games. The final Windows GUI and playable-game WUP conversion are still being developed. Choose a diagnostic release only when you want to help test the runtime. Native .wuhb/.rpx diagnostics are different from installable WUPs.

Download a matching release¶

Use the repository's development releases. Read its release notes and SHA256SUMS.txt; keep the kit, source version and instructions together. CI builds are available from each successful Actions run, with the source commit in their artifact names. Hosted builds are structural/test evidence.

The focused 0.20.0 kit contains Audio Check and GX Transfers original diagnostics. See the short launch-music check for its purpose and prompts. Earlier 0.14.6 contains the Save/RomFS follow-up kit; 0.16.0 adds Asset Files. Use the kit requested by the relevant testing guide. Do not replace/delete save snapshots or old reports while investigating a failure.

Wii U prerequisites¶

The diagnostic launch route targets a homebrew Wii U with Aroma and an SD card. The current tester reports a U.S. Wii U using latest firmware/Aroma/WUP Installer GX2; exact version numbers have not been recorded. This is a reported environment, not a promise for every setup. Follow your homebrew environment's own installation instructions; CTR2WUP does not install or update console firmware. WUP Installer is not needed to launch the diagnostic WUHB files.

  1. Extract the kit and copy the contents of sd-card/ to the SD root.
  2. WUHBs go under sd:/wiiu/apps/. Launch the named app from Aroma.
  3. Follow the app's controller prompts. Minus saves the current result and requests exit; tell us if the launcher/Menu return fails.
  4. Collect results from sd:/ctr2wup/. ZIP the named result-*.txt files and any full traces requested by the guide, then attach the ZIP to a console-result issue or the current development conversation.

An RPX launcher can use the kit's rpx/ files instead. RPX launch and Aroma WUHB launch can follow different lifecycle paths; record which you used.

For GX Transfers 0.17.0, see the two-step guide. It needs no ROM, key or extra asset file. For Asset Files 0.16.0, the original blob must also be present at sd:/ctr2wup/romfs-test.bin. Save/result testing follows console-retest.md. Completed long input guides need not be repeated merely because a newer source version exists.

If a result is missing or the app hangs, use troubleshooting.

Build from source¶

See CONTRIBUTING.md for Python, host compiler and verified devkitPro/WUT setup. make check runs the regression suites; make prototype produces original RPX/WUHB diagnostics. Windows has a separate native toolchain route and CI baseline; Linux SDK executables do not run on Windows.

Your owned game input¶

The future GUI will accept decrypted dumps or locally prepared encrypted inputs. Current engineering commands can prepare supported inputs locally and export a small private executable intake. Their success does not enable game execution. Do not attach keys, full ROMs, saves or commercial asset blobs to GitHub issues. The first selected game is Super Mario 3D Land; its actual revision and requirements still await private analysis.

Maintained source: docs/getting-started.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Testing and evidence¶

Tests must establish the behavior named in their result. Use original fixtures for shared development; keep commercial files and credentials under ignored private directories. Unsupported CPU/services/GPU states must fail visibly.

Desktop regression checks¶

make check
make prototype

make check runs Python and portable C suites, including ASan/UBSan. Separate CI targets are make check-python, make check-c and normal-only make check-c-portable. The latter is appropriate for native Windows GCC, where the Linux sanitizer runtime is not assumed available.

Targeted graphics checks include:

make -C tests/gx check sanitize
python3 tools/validate-transfers.py --output artifacts/my-transfer-matrix
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application transfer --wiiu \
  --output artifacts/my-compiled-transfer

The compiled test uses pinned external libctru and the audited AOT toolchain. Optional external color/layout references use a clean pinned Zakuro checkout; that code remains test-only. Full console rendering is not inferred from it.

Continuous integration¶

Automatic CI runs Python 3.12/3.14, GCC/Clang normal/sanitizer fixtures, native Windows host/portable-C checks, workflow lint and an immutable-SDK Wii U build. Required CI summarizes those results; a failed dependency cannot become a successful aggregate. Artifacts include logs and original diagnostic binaries, with source commits, hashes and notices. Read the actual run status and logs.

Manual Windows development validation adds the external AOT execution and WPF SDK probe. WPF tooling readiness is not a finished GUI. Optional manual CodeQL requires existing repository scanning eligibility; it does not enable a paid plan and is not part of the required regression gate.

Wii U evidence¶

Each diagnostic guides its needed inputs and records a TXT result independently of the full trace. Collect the release/commit, app version, launch route, console firmware/Aroma/installer versions where available, observed output, exit behavior and named report files. The console-result issue form accepts this evidence.

Keep automatic observations, user confirmations, failures, skips and unrun checks distinct. A saved result does not prove successful Menu return. A valid visit-1 snapshot does not establish later update/reload. A reported pass can guide continued development while hard logs remain pending.

No new long test is requested while the current tester is away. The existing Save/RomFS follow-up, optional Asset Files and new GX Transfers round await availability. See getting started and each kit's guide.

Acceptance¶

A full milestone closes only when its complete criteria have recorded evidence. Commercial acceptance requires the exact selected revision, boot/menu/gameplay/input/audio/save/reload/stability, actual WUP install/Menu launch, useful Wii U timings and the final Windows GUI conversion workflow. Passing unit tests and creating more diagnostics are partial implementation.

Maintained source: docs/testing.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Diagnostic troubleshooting¶

Use the application and guide from the same release. Record the displayed build version, WUHB or RPX launch route, and firmware/Aroma/launcher versions when known. Keep existing reports and snapshots while investigating a failure.

Symptom What to check and return
App missing from Aroma Copy the kit's sd-card/ contents to SD root. WUHB files belong in sd:/wiiu/apps/; a source ZIP or RPX is a different download.
No new TXT result Check the displayed build and RESULT VERIFIED / RESULT STORAGE FAILED footer. Return any displayed SD operation/error number, plus available matching files. A missing report is useful evidence.
Save/reload failure Preserve filesystem-test.snapshot, .pending and .previous. Follow the versioned Save/result check; do not delete the files to force a pass.
Wii U Menu splash never finishes Record which app/version and launch route were used, and whether music continued. Report the observed return behavior separately from saved test results.
System freezes during a guide Record the last prompt/action and approximate delay. After recovery, return any files already written. Do not repeat a known failing run solely to obtain a screenshot or log.
GX picture differs or never appears Mark the picture step with L and return the GX result. A desktop reference image cannot substitute for what the console displayed.
Console guide passes but injector rejects a game Commercial execution and the final GUI remain unfinished. Read compatibility and current status for the supported boundary.
Desktop build cannot find devkitPPC/WUT Follow Contributing or Windows setup. Linux SDK executables require Linux; select the native Windows toolchain on Windows.

Diagnostic results are under sd:/ctr2wup/. ZIP the new result-*.txt files and requested original diagnostic traces, then attach them to the console-result issue form or the current development conversation. Include a brief observation of the picture and exit behavior. Check files for private paths/data before posting. Share commercial dumps, keys or saves only through the separately agreed private intake process; they do not belong in GitHub issues.

The older Native/Guarded/Compiled successes are already recorded. The pending 0.14.6 Save/RomFS round and optional 0.16 Asset Files / 0.17 GX round have different purposes; testing explains which evidence each provides.

Maintained source: docs/troubleshooting.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Any commercial revision Unsupported/unverified New reportedly decrypted Mario CCI exceeds transfer limit; smaller private executable intake remains needed; required HLE/GPU/audio/save implementation and Wii U gameplay evidence remain absent
