Stable inspection identity uses the 3DS program ID for save identity. Executable revision includes code and extended-header hashes, separate from stable identity. This is preparation for future caches/profiles, not a currently usable save system.

Only ctr2wup-framebuffer-fixture-v1, containing original code and an exact layout, is enabled by the guest CLI. The separate original startup probe is executed by developer tests and embedded diagnostics, alongside the heap/shared/HID/GSP probes. No commercial title profile is enabled. Profiles must match an exact executable/module revision and recorded console evidence. Upstream authors' desktop compatibility reports never set CTR2WUP's console-verified status.

The October 11 native report provides scoped physical evidence for its embedded CPU/services/heap/objects/HID/GSP/relay/stack/thread self-tests. It does not establish full live guest graphics, save update/reload, full lifecycle acceptance or commercial compatibility. Full console acceptance remains open; see console-retest.md.

Deferred: New 3DS exclusives, stereo output, online services, cameras, microphone, DLC/update merging, save import/export, arbitrary-ROM compatibility and Windows GUI. Native output copying is not a working PICA renderer.

ARMv6 interpreter SXTB/SXTH/UXTB/UXTH rotations/conditions/aliases pass 2,048 independent reference cases. This is a bounded CPU extension; full compiled interpreter startup still rejects STRD. The delivered guarded app uses AOT.

October 12 reported console environment is U.S. firmware/Aroma/WUP Installer GX2, each described by the user as latest. Exact versions are not yet captured. Earlier v0.14.5 Native/Guarded/Compiled successes are retained as reported evidence while the user is away. They do not establish v0.15 decryption, v0.14.6 SD/RomFS, retail compatibility or actual WUP installation. No completed milestone is added.

Maintained source: docs/compatibility.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Project completion assessment¶

Updated October 13, 2026, against version 0.20.0 and the product plan.

The live Project owns current task states, waiting resources and reviewed acceptance. This article is a dated engineering assessment and evidence record. Its rough implementation estimate is independent of the Project's task counts.

Estimated implementation: approximately 20%¶

The completion target is a Windows GUI that converts one verified commercial 3DS revision into a WUP, followed by successful Wii U installation, Menu launch, interactive gameplay, audio, and save/reload. Clean Windows operation, rebuild/save continuity, stability, and measured useful performance are part of acceptance. Broad game compatibility is a later goal.

About 20% is a rough engineering judgment about implementation toward that target. It is not calculated from tests, commits, version numbers, or subsystem counts. The remaining work includes large, uncertain areas, so this estimate does not predict a release date or mean that 80% of the time remains.

The earlier estimate was roughly 15% at v0.12. Subsequent work added normal compiler-built homebrew execution, guest thread/context support, standard input and CPU-framebuffer applications, private filesystem save/reload, and standalone application lifecycle support. These advance a reusable runtime needed by the injector. They do not yet demonstrate commercial game execution.

Full milestone acceptance: 0 of 7. This separate measure tracks complete requirements and required hardware evidence. Partial implementation can exist while every full acceptance checkbox remains open. See the milestone checklist for each verified portion and missing gate.

What supports the estimate¶

Area Verified implementation Limits for the finished product
Host preparation Container inspection, optional local key/seed import and decryption, executable loading, private intake, external code generation, static builds, original metadata/artwork staging Original encrypted execution fixture passes; no complete commercial conversion, actual user-key import or clean Windows execution
CPU and shared runtime Bounded ARM/Thumb/VFP support, checked AOT boundary, memory, eight single-core guest thread contexts, scoped services Incomplete CPU/HLE coverage; actual game and Wii U timing requirements unknown
Homebrew execution Eight original normal-CRT/libctru applications, input/exact CPU-written frames, save/reload, standalone lifecycle and read-only RomFS assets Desktop/sanitizer/emulated PPC checks plus physical original fresh-launch save, embedded/SD assets and GX results; broader runtime acceptance remains open
Packaging Original encrypted writer and independent decrypt/hash/FST/file verification, including real diagnostic RPX payloads Synthetic credentials; no installer acceptance or retained installable package
Wii U platform Named TXT read-back, fresh-launch save reload, current pictures/Minus return and earlier controls evidence Launch-music regression, exact platform versions, recovery and installed-WUP acceptance remain open

v0.14.6 passes 184 Python tests and fourteen C suites normally and with ASan/UBSan, including 1,892 FS and 574 report/guide assertions in each mode. Fourteen external AOT probes pass 28 compile/execute checks; 944 independent ARM1176 cases verify the scoped CPU additions. The new RomFS program passes normally, under sanitizers and on emulated big-endian PowerPC with zero fallback. The compiled save/interactive regressions and guarded/live-input replays pass. These are supporting checks, not a measurement of product completion.

Current reports are under artifacts/reports/console-results-v0146/; the five-app kit and matching source are under artifacts/delivery/console-results-0.14.6/. Private uploaded logs/snapshots are excluded. STATUS.md records validation and the remaining gates. These local artifacts are not a published playable-game release.

Largest unfinished work¶

  • PICA-to-GX2 renderer: actual game draws, shaders, textures, transfers, and coherency. Displaying CPU-written pixels does not implement this renderer.
  • Commercial runtime integration: Retail asset integration, required services/modules, guest DSP/audio, retail saves, and any CPU behavior exercised by the first game.
  • Selected-title proof: exact Mario revision analysis, boot, menu, gameplay, audio, save/reload, stability, and useful Wii U performance.
  • Finished injection engine: commercial end-to-end orchestration, caching, cancellation/rebuild behavior, native Windows execution, and real WUP installation.
  • Windows GUI: the specified UWUVCI-style workflow is designed but not implemented. Its Inject action will use the proven conversion engine.

Evidence and inputs still needed¶

The supplied Mario CIA has not been inspected: it exceeds the attachment tool's 32 MiB transfer limit. The CIA intake helper can export a small private executable view for revision and code analysis when the user's computer is available. An encrypted executable can now be prepared locally with the new optional decryption stage, using locally obtained material. Real console export and native Windows acceptance remain pending. That export excludes game assets; commercial asset integration will require access to those separately.

The native guide and later Minus exit have user-provided console evidence. The user now reports Native/Guarded/Compiled completed without errors on 0.14.5. No new logs were saved; supplied logs have older build headers. Save failed, though a valid two-record visit-1 snapshot was received. v0.14.6 supplies an independent named TXT result with closed-file read-back and portable save publication/rollback/recovery. The exact prior console failure causes remain unconfirmed. The follow-up requests Save, then a quick original RomFS test, with no long native/input repetition.

Actual WUP installation also needs the documented console environment and local packaging prerequisites. Native Windows tests remain pending.

Missing inputs block those acceptance checks and title-specific decisions. Further reusable runtime work remains possible; it does not substitute for the missing evidence. Reassess progress after title boot/rendering, physical diagnostic tests, successful installation, and GUI-driven conversion provide new evidence. The next steps are maintained in current-roadblock.md.

v0.15.0 advances the same approximately-20% foundation: local encrypted main-content preparation and support-file import have original fixture/known-vector checks and an actual AOT/RPX/WUHB pipeline proof. The reported U.S. latest-firmware/Aroma/ WUP Installer GX2 setup and earlier successes allow continued independent work; exact versions/hard logs remain pending. Commercial rendering/runtime, installed WUP gameplay and GUI proof still dominate remaining work, so no full acceptance gate or percentage increase follows from the decryption increment.

v0.16.0 adds full canonical RomFS IVFC/metadata verification, private asset blob preparation, a SHA-256 checked file provider and all 14 base texture formats. These advance reusable M3/M4/M6 foundations while the user is away. They do not establish game rendering, Mario gameplay, Wii U file I/O acceptance or installed WUP behavior. The approximately-20% estimate and 0/7 full gates remain unchanged. Current validation/delivery: artifacts/reports/assets-textures-v016/ and artifacts/delivery/assets-textures-0.16.0/.

v0.17.0 adds standard CPU-backed GX queue operations and a stock-libctru transfer application with exact frame checks. GitHub now exposes seven tracked milestone issues, CI/artifacts, review templates and agent guidance. These improve shared runtime foundations and development visibility. They do not supply the missing PICA draws, actual Mario/console/Windows/installed-WUP/GUI acceptance, so the approximately-20% estimate and 0/7 full gates remain unchanged.

v0.18.0 adds a bounded PICA command front end, transactional raw viewport/scissor configuration and standard queue/P3D completion, verified by original pinned libctru command construction and a compiled guest. Draw/shader/GX2 scene work and console acceptance remain open, so the rough estimate remains 20% and 0/7 full gates. The owner supplied Project #2; automated population still requires Projects access, and the tool cannot create a duplicate after an access failure.

v0.20.0 records reviewed physical TXT collection, original save fresh-launch reload, embedded/SD assets and GX transfer/configuration evidence. The looping launch music remains an open regression with a short Audio Check/GX handoff. Project #2 is populated through Actions; its task fields remain authoritative. These scoped successes do not accept a full gate or materially change the approximately-20% implementation estimate.

Maintained source: docs/progress.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Milestones and remaining work¶

Updated October 13, 2026 for v0.23.0. The product is an UWUVCI-style Windows GUI: Select owned 3DS ROM → configure title/artwork → Inject → install its WUP → play from the Wii U Menu. The CLI is the shared engine. Super Mario 3D Land is the first candidate; its exact revision remains unverified.

Approximately 20% implemented; 0/7 full milestones accepted. The estimate is a rough engineering judgment, not a percentage of issues, tests or commits. These 35 gate work items make the work actionable; creating or closing an issue does not automatically accept a milestone or raise the completion estimate.

The project brief defines requirements, the product plan defines execution order, and STATUS.md records current results. Historical version details and receipts remain in development history.

How to use the roadmap¶

The existing seven GitHub milestone issues remain overview trackers. Each work item below is a separate implementation or evidence task feeding its milestone. Project #2 is the authority for status, blockers and reviewed full acceptance. milestone-status.json records versioned scope/evidence and initial seed values; it cannot overwrite existing Project values. The states below are a dated evidence snapshot. Manual issue notes and acceptance checkmarks remain reviewable. Native feature/PR links and the one-time empty/Todo status migration are documented in development tracking; later owner edits remain authoritative.

State Meaning
Planned Defined remaining work; implementation has not begun.
In progress Partial implementation exists; acceptance remains open.
Awaiting evidence The scoped implementation/test artifact exists, but the required physical or user evidence is missing.
Blocked An identified input or acceptance prerequisite prevents completing this task.

needs-console, needs-input and needs-windows identify required evidence or resources, independently of the work state. Dependencies describe prerequisites for accepting a task; independent original fixtures, interface review and preparation may proceed while prerequisites remain open. A task is complete only when its stated criteria have evidence. Milestone closure requires its full exit criteria and reviewed console/Windows/title evidence, never an automatic checklist.

Commercial ROMs, code, assets, saves, keys, traces and packages stay in the documented private workflow. GitHub issue evidence uses original fixtures or redacted reports; never attach a game or key. Desktop/sanitizer/emulated-PowerPC passes establish their stated boundaries, not physical-console execution.

Milestone overview¶

Milestone State Remaining acceptance
M1: Native Wii U platform Awaiting evidence Platform lifecycle/recovery, stability and installed original WUP evidence
M2: CPU translation and performance In progress Exercised CPU/ABI/fallback coverage and actual Espresso timing
M3: Homebrew execution runtime In progress Stable physical compiled guest plus broader services/modules/audio
M4: PICA to GX2 rendering In progress Real PICA/GX2 draws/shaders/TEV/coherency and measured scenes
M5: Super Mario 3D Land revision Blocked Owned exact-revision intake and Mario gameplay/audio/save/performance
M6: Automated ROM to WUP conversion In progress Full Windows conversion, installer acceptance and rebuild/save continuity
M7: Windows GUI injector Planned Responsive packaged Windows GUI Inject-to-playable-WUP workflow

The implementation sequence is P1–P5 in the product plan: resolve portable translation and original WUP packaging early; identify the selected revision; implement its execution/renderer requirements; prove Windows conversion; then implement the GUI. Milestones track acceptance boundaries, so independent work can overlap without claiming downstream completion.

M1: Native Wii U platform¶

State: Awaiting evidence. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: Versioned original Native/guest diagnostics build with WUT/devkitPPC, and the tester can run them under the documented Wii U environment. A diagnostic RPX/WUHB is distinct from an installed WUP.

Deliverables: A reproducible native platform build, checked result/save paths, a short guided console kit, an installed original diagnostic and a versioned platform acceptance record.

Exit criteria: Physical evidence establishes both screens, required controls/touch, native audio, reliable launch/exit, SD persistence/recovery and a documented stability session. An original WUP installs and launches/exits from the Wii U Menu.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: Older native reports and the confirmed Minus exit are retained. The user reports Native/Guarded/Compiled completed without errors on v0.14.5; supplied text logs have older headers. A valid visit-1 snapshot proves initial persistence, while a later Save update failed and no new reports appeared. Current independent TXT read-back and save publication/recovery have desktop/sanitizer checks, not new console acceptance.

Reviewed physical follow-up: Named TXT collection, original fresh-launch save reload, embedded/SD assets and GX transfers pass their scoped checks; pictures and Minus return have user confirmation. October 13 Audio Check/GX reports and user confirmation verify the launch-music regression; full lifecycle/recovery and installed-title acceptance remain open.

Still needs external evidence/input: Focused audio regression, exact firmware/Aroma/installer versions and native/installed lifecycle/recovery evidence require the Wii U. No game dump is needed for the original diagnostics.

References: Console evidence · Short follow-up · Guided tests · Console guide.

Actionable work items¶

m1-results: Verify current TXT result collection on Wii U¶

Current Save/RomFS/SD-asset/GX named reports are physically verified, including fresh-launch save reload. Retain older failed/missing reports separately; their exact original cause remains unknown. Project review records this scoped collection task independently of M1 acceptance.

State: Awaiting evidence · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: none.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] A fresh launch creates a uniquely named, closed-file read-back-verified TXT report in the documented SD results directory; its header identifies the tested build and diagnostic.
  • [ ] Upload the requested TXT files and preserved snapshot/siblings through the private intake route; retain automatic observations, user confirmations, failures and skips separately.

m1-lifecycle: Verify repeat launch, exit and SD publication recovery¶

Exercise the native application lifecycle and result/save persistence without discarding the earlier Minus-exit confirmation.

State: Awaiting evidence · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m1-results.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] A documented repeat-launch and Minus/Home/APT exit sequence returns to the Wii U Menu without a hang; record exact firmware, Aroma, launch route and build.
  • [ ] Save/relaunch retains the expected bytes, and any observed publication/recovery failure preserves readable previous data or reports an actionable error; record SD files while retaining the original failure evidence.

m1-controls-audio: Record native display, controls and audio acceptance¶

Retain older native/control evidence and fill only the missing checks needed for current platform acceptance.

State: Awaiting evidence · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: none.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] The native guide records TV/GamePad mapping, buttons, sticks, touch and the original native tone with observed results and build identity.
  • [ ] Any changed display, input, audio or lifecycle path has a focused console regression result; earlier successful checks are cited instead of repeating a long guide solely for a version bump.

m1-package-launch: Verify an original diagnostic as an installed Wii U title¶

Use the validated original-homebrew WUP smoke package to prove installation and Wii U Menu launch rather than inferring them from RPX/WUHB launch.

State: Blocked · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m6-original-wup.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] The original diagnostic installs with the recorded homebrew/installer environment and launches from the Wii U Menu with its expected title identity and screens.
  • [ ] The installed diagnostic exits normally and can be relaunched; retain the versioned build/package report and installation observations without exposing local packaging material.

m1-platform-acceptance: Review and accept the native Wii U platform gate¶

Collect the scoped native platform results into one reproducible acceptance record.

State: Blocked · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m1-results, m1-lifecycle, m1-controls-audio, m1-package-launch.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] The current result, lifecycle, controls/audio and installed-diagnostic tasks have reviewable evidence and no unexplained hang or persistence failure.
  • [ ] A versioned platform report states the tested firmware/Aroma/installer, supported launch routes and stability session; M1 remains open until this evidence is reviewed.

M2: CPU translation and performance¶

State: In progress. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: The audited external lifter generates C through a checked portable ABI; original interpreter/AOT/reference fixtures and an emulated big-endian PowerPC correctness route are available.

Deliverables: Documented exercised ARM/Thumb/VFP support, checked generated-code/memory behavior, observable fallback and private trace handling, plus actual Espresso timing/memory reports.

Exit criteria: Original exercised workloads have independently supported state/fault parity, explicit unsupported behavior and reviewable real Wii U performance limits. The selected title must separately demonstrate useful gameplay performance in M5.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: Current original CPU/reference and external AOT checks cover a bounded subset, eight single-core contexts, scoped synchronization and independent ARM1176 cases. GCC/Clang sanitizers and emulated big-endian PowerPC are supporting correctness evidence. No actual Espresso benchmark or full ARM/VFP coverage is claimed.

Still needs external evidence/input: Original benchmarks can proceed without a ROM. Real timing requires the Wii U; title-driven coverage and fallback analysis need the private exact-revision executable/trace.

References: CPU subset · Stack semantics · External lifter ABI · Threads.

Actionable work items¶

m2-instruction-coverage: Complete exercised ARM, Thumb and VFP correctness¶

Close concrete CPU gaps with independent references and original fixtures, preserving visible errors for unsupported behavior.

State: In progress · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: none.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Every supported new instruction/exception/exclusive/VFP case has independently checked register, flags, memory and fault results through the relevant interpreter/AOT paths.
  • [ ] Supported scope, remaining gaps and reference-model limits are documented; no unsupported instruction is accepted by a fabricated success result.

m2-abi-memory: Complete the generated-code ABI and checked memory boundary¶

Measure and tighten generated C integration, little-endian guest access, resume state and code/data ownership on the PowerPC target.

State: In progress · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: none.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Original generated-code fixtures match interpreter/reference state across calls, SVC/resume, threading and partial-fault boundaries on host and emulated big-endian PowerPC.
  • [ ] Release-build code size, memory/RSS, build cost and checked-access cost are recorded; optimizations preserve bounds, permissions and executable-store invalidation.

m2-fallback-tracing: Implement observable fallback and missing-block trace handling¶

Provide a correct, bounded recovery route for supported AOT misses and an actionable stop for unavailable execution states.

State: In progress · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: m2-instruction-coverage, m2-abi-memory.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] An original missed-block/dynamic-dispatch fixture either executes through a verified supported fallback with state parity or fails explicitly before corrupting state.
  • [ ] Reports separate AOT instructions, fallback time/counts and unsupported locations; trace import validates input identity and stays private for user-derived code.

m2-espresso-benchmarks: Measure real Espresso execution and memory costs¶

Run representative original CPU/AOT workloads on physical Wii U before promising useful game performance.

State: Planned · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m2-abi-memory, m2-fallback-tracing.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] A versioned console benchmark records actual CPU/AOT/fallback, checked-memory and scheduling costs, build flags, memory peak and workload identity.
  • [ ] The report distinguishes emulated PowerPC correctness from actual Espresso timing and identifies measured bottlenecks for targeted optimization.

m2-cpu-acceptance: Review CPU correctness, fallback and performance acceptance¶

Close the CPU milestone only against the exercised execution requirements and physical timing evidence.

State: Blocked · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m2-instruction-coverage, m2-abi-memory, m2-fallback-tracing, m2-espresso-benchmarks.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Instruction/ABI/fallback evidence establishes the documented supported workload without unexplained state mismatches or silent unsupported execution.
  • [ ] Actual Wii U timing and memory reports support a stated feasibility limit; useful selected-title gameplay performance is subsequently confirmed by the title gate.

M3: Homebrew execution runtime¶

State: In progress. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: Original normal-CRT/devkitARM/libctru applications execute through the portable runtime on desktop. Their required guest memory, scheduler, service and display contracts are defined.

Deliverables: Stable compiled guest execution with checked kernel/IPC/lifecycle/display/input/filesystem contracts; focused module and guest-audio fixtures feed later selected-title integration.

Exit criteria: A real compiled guest executes on Wii U, updates both screens, responds to required GamePad input/touch and exits or completes a documented stability session. Exercised save/assets behavior is verified. This fixture gate does not require claiming all retail services or PICA draw support.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: Nine original compiled applications cover boot, threads, input, graphics, interactive execution, save/reload, RomFS, GX transfers and bounded PICA draws. Desktop/sanitizer and selected emulated PowerPC checks support exact pixels/state/files. Reviewed physical Save/results/assets/GX evidence is retained; October 13 compiled draw and launch-audio results are reviewed, with a 57.33-second draw-scene delay and premature visual-prompt guard requiring follow-up; a native tone does not establish guest DSP/audio.

Still needs external evidence/input: Original guest modules, audio probes and shared service work can proceed independently. Hardware validates display/input/audio/lifecycle/assets; observed retail service/module/save requirements need M5 intake.

References: Compiled applications · Services · Filesystem · RomFS assets.

Actionable work items¶

m3-homebrew-console: Verify the normal-CRT homebrew suite on Wii U¶

Collect focused physical evidence for the original compiled applications, especially Save, RomFS/Asset Files and GX Transfers.

State: Awaiting evidence · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m1-results.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Current compiled guests update both screens, respond to the required input/touch, complete their documented exit and retain their versioned observations.
  • [ ] Save fresh-process reload, nested/Unicode read-only assets and GX transfer pictures/results match the documented original fixtures; unresolved errors are investigated instead of marked passed.

m3-service-contracts: Extend exercised kernel, IPC and application contracts¶

Advance the shared scheduler/memory/SRV/APT/CFG/HID/GSP/FS contracts using original applications and later observed title requirements.

State: In progress · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: none.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Each added service or kernel behavior has checked request shapes, ownership, scheduling/teardown and failure cases backed by an original fixture.
  • [ ] Unsupported applets, cross-process semantics and privileged/optional services stay explicit; generic success is used only where its observable behavior is justified.

m3-module-loading: Implement checked dynamic-module loading and dispatch¶

Add the portable CRO/CRS loading, relocation and translated-module boundary required for executable integration.

State: Planned · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: m2-abi-memory, m3-service-contracts.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] An original module fixture loads, relocates, calls and unloads through interpreter/AOT with checked ranges, symbols and code ownership; malformed relocation inputs fail visibly.
  • [ ] Supported module forms and dispatch/ownership limits are documented; unsupported forms fail visibly, while actual title module requirements are subsequently established in the private revision matrix.

m3-guest-audio: Build the guest DSP/audio path with original probes¶

Separate native diagnostic tone evidence from the unimplemented guest audio protocol and Wii U output pipeline.

State: Planned · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m3-service-contracts.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Original guest audio fixtures exercise the implemented DSP/service path with checked buffers/voices and independently verified sample/timing results.
  • [ ] Wii U output, synchronization, pause/exit and resource cleanup have focused console observations; unsupported audio commands fail visibly.

m3-runtime-acceptance: Accept a stable compiled guest runtime on Wii U¶

Prove the milestone fixture really executes guest code with the documented kernel/services, display and input on physical hardware.

State: Blocked · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m3-homebrew-console, m3-service-contracts, m1-lifecycle.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] A versioned normal-CRT guest demonstrates guest execution, two-screen updates, input/touch, filesystem/assets as exercised and clean exit or a recorded stability session on Wii U.
  • [ ] Its CPU/service/fallback requirements and known limits are documented; passing the fixture does not claim retail services, PICA draws or Mario compatibility.

M4: PICA to GX2 rendering¶

State: In progress. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: Guest framebuffer presentation and bounded GSP/GX operations work on original fixtures. Independent original scenes/reference primitives are available for each new PICA feature.

Deliverables: Real PICA draw/state decoding, vertex/shader and fragment translation, checked GPU resources/coherency, explicit shader/state cache behavior and original reference scene/timing evidence.

Exit criteria: Exercised fixture scenes compare to a trusted reference using stated tolerances and visual checks. Physical Wii U measurements include CPU submission, GPU work, transfers, waits and memory. Unsupported renderer features remain visible.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: Five rotated framebuffer formats, all 14 base texture formats and scoped DMA/fill/display conversion/raw-copy/cache operations have original/reference checks. The stock-libctru transfer fixture has exact desktop frames and zero fallback. These CPU-backed transfers and decoder checks do not implement PICA draws or prove GX2 scene correctness.

Still needs external evidence/input: Original PICA/reference scenes and license review can progress without a ROM. Real GX2 images/timing need a Wii U; the selected revision determines additional lighting/LUT/geometry/procedural or readback requirements.

References: Renderer status · GX transfer contract · Display · GSP queues.

Actionable work items¶

m4-pica-commands: Decode PICA state and submit bounded draw commands¶

The standard guest path handles configuration and bounded owned-resource draws through canonical tiled targets, retained shader/state and transactional completion. An original compiled guest verifies exact software scenes; broader pipeline state and native GPU translation remain open.

State: Done (bounded command acceptance reviewed) · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: none.

Verified foundation: PICA command contract, 353 original builder/reference cases, transactional native state and the compiled standard command-queue fixture. No draws or full task acceptance are claimed.

v0.23.0 connects compiled guest draws to the standard GSP queue with owned resources, exact target/display checks and preserved failure state. October 13 physical own PASS/exit and user picture confirmation are reviewed; rendered presentation at 57.33 seconds and premature guide timing need a focused follow-up.

v0.22.0 adds owned software scenes with complete-list state/pixel rollback, explicit resource binding and independent exact pixels. These execute draws in the experimental boundary; the guest queue remains configuration-only. Full task acceptance remains open.

Acceptance:

  • [x] Original command streams establish register/state changes, draw inputs and validated resource ranges; malformed or unsupported state fails visibly before modifying output.
  • [x] The decoder feeds a documented renderer boundary, with reference traces that distinguish CPU-written framebuffer presentation from actual PICA draws.

Reviewed implementation: PR #48 and PR #57. Their original configuration/draw traces and exact output satisfy both criteria. Task #27 closes through the recorded Actions review; broader rendering and full M4 remain open.

m4-vertex-shaders: Implement vertex formats, shader execution and GX2 translation¶

Choose a license-cleared Latte shader route and validate exercised vertex attributes, uniforms and shader instructions.

State: In progress · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: m4-pica-commands.

Verified foundation: resolved vertex decoding now covers both contiguous streams and checked 8-bit/16-bit indexed gathers. The original suite covers every index value and transactional failure behavior. The first owned scenes now exercise loaders/fixed state, uniforms, MOV/NOP/END shaders, output maps and opaque triangles. Independent analytic pixels and 12,290 pinned encoding comparisons pass, and a focused Wii U diagnostic builds. Broader shader behavior, hardware/reference raster evidence, physical measured costs and a license-cleared GX2 shader route remain open; this checkpoint does not accept the task.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Original vertex/shader scenes compare independently checked outputs across the reference and chosen GX2/software correctness path using stated tolerances.
  • [ ] Shader tooling licenses, generated-code ownership, supported instructions and miss behavior are documented; a software correctness path has measured costs rather than an assumed final speed.

m4-fragment-pipeline: Implement textures, samplers, TEV and raster state¶

Connect the verified base texture decoders to real draws and the fragment behavior required by reference scenes and the selected title.

State: Planned · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: m4-vertex-shaders.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Original scenes cover exercised texture layouts/samplers, TEV stages, blend, depth, stencil and alpha tests with reference images and stated comparison tolerances.
  • [ ] Required lighting/LUT/geometry/procedural behavior is added from observed requirements or remains explicitly unsupported; texture-decoder tests alone do not claim renderer acceptance.

m4-coherency-cache: Implement GPU resource coherency and versioned shader/state caches¶

Handle render-to-texture, aliases, completion/readback and CPU/GPU ownership while making shader/cache misses explicit.

State: Planned · Needs: none for original-fixture development.

Depends on: m4-pica-commands, m4-fragment-pipeline.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Original resource scenes prove guest-visible writes/readbacks, alias invalidation and synchronization ordering without stale data or unchecked GPU access.
  • [ ] Cache identity includes relevant code/runtime/renderer/settings inputs; missing or invalid state follows a supported translation/fallback or diagnostic failure, never silent incorrect rendering.

m4-console-scenes: Compare rendered scenes and measure Wii U graphics costs¶

Retain reviewed bounded compiled draw results; correct premature visual gating and measure execution/scaling/GPU/wait/memory costs separately before full graphics or title acceptance.

State: Blocked · Needs: needs-console.

Depends on: m4-vertex-shaders, m4-fragment-pipeline, m4-coherency-cache.

Acceptance:

  • [ ] Physical Wii U images match the defined scene tolerances and required visual checks; build/reference identity and unsupported features accompany the results.
  • [ ] CPU submission, GPU work, transfers, waits, memory and frame-time distribution are measured on Wii U; selected-title scenes are later validated through M5.

M5: Super Mario 3D Land revision¶

State: Blocked. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: The user supplies a privately prepared executable and required assets for an owned exact Old 3DS revision. Super Mario 3D Land is the chosen candidate; its region/version remain unverified until inspected.

Deliverables: An exact-hash revision profile, observed feature/test-route matrix, required shared runtime/renderer/audio/retail-save integration and a measured supported-title console acceptance report.

Exit criteria: That exact revision cold-boots, reaches its menu, plays the agreed course with required input/touch and synchronized audio, saves/reloads, and completes a recorded stability session at useful intended-speed performance on Wii U.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: The original CIA attachment exceeds the workspace attachment transfer limit. The received small intake reports encrypted executable content and contains no executable; it supplies no observed game behavior. Local preparation and original-fixture proofs establish tooling only. Upstream source observations are leads, not reproduced CTR2WUP compatibility.

Still needs external evidence/input: This milestone is blocked on the owned executable/assets and exact revision. Keys remain local; no commercial code, assets or traces are posted in GitHub issues or public artifacts. Actual Wii U gameplay evidence follows implementation.

References: First-title intake · CIA helper · Local encrypted input · Compatibility.

M6: Automated ROM to WUP conversion¶

State: In progress. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: Original AOT/Wii U staging and structural packaging work; the selected-game runtime and required assets become available through M5. Resolve real packaging/installer requirements early, independently of commercial execution.

Deliverables: One checked shared ROM-to-WUP engine with safe stable title IDs, local preparation, actual translation/build/assets/cache/staging/packing, atomic output, cancellation/rebuild/trace support and a clean-Windows acceptance report.

Exit criteria: A clean native Windows machine converts the supported revision without manual source edits into a WUP that installs and Menu-launches on Wii U. Rebuild/update preserves saves, titles keep separate identities, and user-directed uninstall behavior is documented and verified.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: Original encrypted input reaches actual external AOT and native cross-built diagnostics. The scoped writer and independent decrypt/FST/hash/file verifier pass with synthetic credentials. Hosted Windows host/portable-C and deep real AOT/Unicode/.NET 10 WPF tooling checks passed. They do not prove actual local credential provenance, installer acceptance or full commercial native Windows conversion.

Still needs external evidence/input: Real locally managed packaging prerequisites and console installation are still required. Commercial stage integration needs M5 code/assets; clean native Windows full-conversion and continuity checks are separate from the already passed tooling CI.

References: Packaging boundaries · Windows build · Encrypted preparation · Host architecture.

Actionable work items¶

M7: Windows GUI injector¶

State: Planned. Full acceptance: open.

Entry: The selected title has a proven execution path and the shared engine has demonstrated real Windows conversion. Specify/validate interfaces now; do not implement the GUI before the execution/conversion pipeline works.

Deliverables: Packaged C# WPF CTR2WUP.exe with the reviewed UWUVCI-style Inject page, prerequisite/local-key setup, responsive real progress/cancellation, saved projects/rebuild and redacted reports.

Exit criteria: On clean Windows, the user selects the supported owned ROM, configures title/artwork/output and clicks Inject to obtain its validated WUP without undocumented tools or manual compiler steps. That package passes installation/Menu gameplay/audio/save/reload/stability and rebuild continuity on Wii U.

Verified desktop/earlier evidence: The GUI workflow and encrypted-input amendment are documented, and supported WPF tooling passed hosted Windows validation. No finished GUI exists. A mockup, diagnostic launcher or CLI alone cannot close this final product milestone.

Still needs external evidence/input: Implementation follows the proven M5/M6 pipeline. Packaging/usability needs clean Windows; local-key and selected-game tests require owned inputs; final installation/gameplay evidence needs the Wii U.

References: GUI workflow · Windows build · Product execution plan · Encrypted-input amendment.

Acceptance review and deferred scope¶

Close a work issue only with its scoped result and links to evidence. Update the versioned source state when implementation/evidence changes. The overview tracker remains open until the milestone exit criteria are reviewed; closing its child issues by itself does not prove physical behavior or complete the product.

The first MVP completes only when M7 demonstrates GUI conversion of the exact supported revision into an installed playable per-game WUP, including the agreed gameplay/audio/save/reload/stability and rebuild tests. Record any measured performance limit before broadening compatibility.

New 3DS exclusives, stereoscopic TV, online services, cameras, microphone, DLC/update merging, save import/export and broad arbitrary-ROM compatibility remain deferred. Titles that require them need separate implementation/evidence.

Public documentation delivery is separate from gameplay acceptance. The private source repository remains private: private Pages is unavailable on the user’s free plan, while the generated offline HTML and public docs-only export support free publication. The user declines private-repository branch protection; topic branches, reviewable PRs and passing CI remain the development convention. No paid subscription or administrative setting is assumed enabled. See GitHub development for actual access limits.

GSP command queue and CPU-backed GX transfers (0.18.0)¶

This M3/M4 foundation lets ordinary libctru GX calls clear/copy/convert buffers through the standard shared command ring. It supplies no PICA draw renderer or native GX2 transfer acceleration. No game patch or commercial data is involved.

Supported boundary¶

Client ID0 uses the retained 4 KiB relay page, 15 32-byte command slots at 0x820 and the header at 0x800. TriggerCmdReqQueue requires the owning registered GSP session and GPU right. Commands commit synchronously in order, advancing index/ count only after successful execution. A failed command and its later entries remain pending, with a visible guest fault; any successful prefix stays committed. Documented stop/should-stop states are retained, not silently erased.

Command Supported behavior Current limits
DMA (0) Staged byte copy and DMA completion Nonempty, at most 4 MiB
Memory fill (2) 16/24/32-bit LE repeating pattern, either/both banks Trigger required; 8-byte endpoints; whole patterns; at most 4 MiB per bank
Display transfer (3) All five display formats, linear/tiled/same layout, vertical flip Equal dimensions in 1..1024; tiled multiples of 8; no scaling/crop/raw-display copy
Texture copy (4) Raw copy with independent 16-byte-unit line widths/gaps Flags8, lengths in whole 16-byte units; bounded spans; gaps preserved
Cache regions (5) Checked coherent CPU-backed ranges and barrier No asynchronous GPU/DSP cache model
PICA command list (1) Finalized raw viewport/scissor configuration and P3D completion All draws, shaders/uploads and other register state remain unsupported; see the contract

DMA accepts the virtual addresses defined by the GSP command wire. This is not a general physical/VRAM translator. All source/destination spans, permissions, alignment, arithmetic and allocations are checked before each command changes bytes. Staging makes guest overlap safe. Device output may not alias relay backing, including through another mapping. Bulk byte APIs validate complete ranges across adjacent mappings and update exclusive/code generations, including executable storage aliases; caller-owned host buffers cannot alias guest storage.

PSC0/PSC1/PPF/DMA completions use the standard relay interrupt ring. A two-bank fill sends one PSC0, following the pinned Azahar behavior; Zakuro's two-IRQ variant is not assumed equivalent. Full rings retain drop/error accounting and do not signal an event for an interrupt that was not enqueued. CPU-backed timing is synchronous; actual hardware latency, cache/coherency and scheduling accuracy remain unverified.

Original application and verification¶

tests/homebrew/transfer.c uses stock CRT/libctru GX APIs. It checks both/single fill banks, raw copy with asymmetric gaps and untouched gap bytes, cache regions, DMA bytes, tiled RGBA8 -> linear RGB565 conversion, and green/red/green top with blue bottom images. It waits through standard PSC/PPF/DMA/VBlank events, then unmounts/exits through ordinary library code.

Its checked AOT run exits after 1,489,172 instructions, zero fallback, 17 GX operations (3 DMA, 8 fill, 3 display, 1 texture copy, 1 cache, 1 PICA setup), and exact three-frame pixel checks. Three deliberately unavailable startup services remain recorded. C normal/sanitizer checks cover failure atomicity, permissions, aliases, generations, queue wrap/stop/prefix behavior, malformed headers and full interrupts. The image matrix compares 400 original vectors across C/Python and pinned external color/Morton primitives; emulated big-endian PPC hashes match. The external full transfer engine and physical Wii U were not executed by those reference comparisons. Required checks and WUT builds are recorded separately.

Optional console round¶

The 0.18.0 kit places ctr2wup-libctru-transfer.wuhb in sd-card/wiiu/apps/. Copy the contents of sd-card to SD root and launch CTR2WUP GX Transfers 0.18.0 in Aroma. This test needs no ROM, key or extra blob.

  1. Watch the TV change green/red/green with a blue GamePad image. The guide asks R for the observed picture or L for a problem. Do not confirm a picture you did not observe; faults/skips stay distinct.
  2. The app checks the guest's own GX PASS and exit automatically. Press Minus when complete and separately report whether it returned to Menu/launcher.

Results are under sd:/ctr2wup/, named result-compiled-gx-transfer-0.18.0-*.txt, plus any full trace. They include operation counts and the last GX command/fault. ZIP those TXT files together and attach the ZIP to a console-result issue or the development conversation. Preserve existing saves/reports. Completed long native/input guides need not be repeated. Actual SD/lifecycle/pictures and console timings remain pending until that round.

The v0.18 fixture also submits an original libctru-built no-draw PICA configuration list and waits for P3D. Its TXT report includes PICA list/write/finalizer counts and parser diagnostics. Picture and launcher confirmations remain separate; these pictures still come from fills/transfers, not a PICA draw renderer.

Maintained source: docs/gx-transfers.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Current-process read-only RomFS assets (0.16.0)¶

This shared M3 foundation lets ordinary libctru romfsInit and newlib code read an explicitly supplied decrypted Level-3 RomFS view. No game patch or host path exposure is involved. The original fixture contains three tiny files and no commercial content.

Implemented boundary¶

The platform initializes a zeroed ctr_fs_romfs with a borrowed read callback, context and 64-bit byte length, then mounts it before guest execution. It owns the provider until all mounts and retained file handles close; active views cannot be reinitialized/closed. The callback must read the requested range or return failure. The runtime stages reads before publishing guest bytes so a failed/partial provider cannot leak partially written output.

FSUSER OpenFileDirectly (0x08030204) recognizes archive 3, the empty archive path, 12-byte all-zero binary current-process file selector and read-only flags. It validates static descriptors and guest buffers. FSFILE supports Read, GetSize, Flush and Close. Writes/resizes are denied. Handles retain ownership across copies; whole-buffer permissions, IPC physical aliases, EOF and overflowing 64-bit offsets are checked. Requests are bounded to 1 MiB, while the provider's file size may exceed 4 GiB. Unmounted/unknown archives fail visibly.

Unmodified libctru resolves directories, filenames and Unicode inside guest code by reading Level-3 metadata. This is not a new implementation of generic FSUSER directory commands. tools/generate-romfs-fixture.py creates the original 322-byte image, SHA-256 C header, manifest and synthetic IVFC wrapper. Hash consistency does not establish signature authenticity.

Original application and checks¶

tests/homebrew/romfs.c runs normal CRT/APT/FS/newlib startup and checks:

  • root file bytes;
  • nested binary reads, seek and EOF;
  • a Japanese filename;
  • directory listing through opendir/readdir;
  • rejection of a write-open, then ordinary unmount and process exit.

It emits CTR2WUP compiled RomFS PASS only after all checks. Desktop and ASan/UBSan runs exit after 49,550 instructions with zero interpreter fallback. The same original generated program also passes in QEMU emulated big-endian PPC Linux. The Compiled RomFS RPX/WUHB is built for Wii U; physical execution is pending. The portable FS suite adds lifetime, copied-handle, sparse >4 GiB, malformed-descriptor, read-failure atomicity and permission/alias checks.

source /workspace/ctr2wup-tools/environment/activate.sh
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application romfs --wiiu \
  --output artifacts/reports/my-romfs
make -C tests/filesystem check sanitize

Normal libctru filename/time handling exposed three shared prerequisites: a read-only shared time page, the defined ARM STM writeback form with its base lowest in the list, and ARMv6 U=1/A=0 unaligned single-data accesses. These were implemented in the runtime and checked against ARM1176 references, without changing the program's library implementation. Time starts at an explicitly synthetic Unix-zero epoch and advances with the guest clock; no real console RTC or timezone accuracy is claimed. See cpu-stack.md.

Host asset preparation¶

prepare-rom now verifies the complete canonical three-level IVFC tree whenever RomFS exists. prepare-assets performs the same full verification after checked decrypted container inspection, validates Level-3 metadata, and exports a private blob and manifest:

python3 -m host prepare-assets "private/prepared/game.cxi" \
  --output "private/mario-assets"

The output contains romfs.bin (Level-3 bytes) and asset-manifest.json, including source/code revision, full-tree integrity, blob SHA-256 and virtual file offsets, sizes and hashes. Directory parents/siblings/hash chains, Unicode components, cycles/orphans, duplicate names, ranges, alignment and bounded budgets are checked. Virtual names never become host extraction paths. Unsupported IVFC layouts and corruption outside the old NCCH superblock check fail explicitly. All declared physical blocks are checked, including zero final-block padding. NCCH's checked superblock must cover the complete master table. This establishes hash consistency, not authentic signatures or commercial compatibility. Output publication is atomic and refuses replacement; sources remain unchanged.

File-backed provider and optional Wii U test¶

ctr_romfs_file opens an explicitly configured immutable blob, streams its SHA-256 and requires the expected manifest hash before mounting. It lends the same read-only callback contract to guest FS; libctru still resolves filenames. It closes after guest mounts/handles release and creates no extra SD mount/client. The current standard-C fseek/ftell provider is limited to LONG_MAX on the target (2 GiB minus one byte on 32-bit PPC), despite the abstract provider's 64-bit/sparse-file support. Backing must remain immutable while mounted; this is an open-time hash check, not per-read authenticated storage. Actual SD startup cost, file access and lifecycle behavior still require console evidence.

Build the optional original Asset Files diagnostic with:

python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application romfs --wiiu --romfs-file \
  --output artifacts/reports/my-romfs-file

The validator checks identical complete guest results for RAM/file providers and rejects a corrupted blob before execution. tools/validate-asset-pipeline.py reuses its romfs/ directory to prove original encrypted CIA -> local preparation -> full IVFC -> private asset blob -> actual compiled libctru file access, with transient synthetic keys and zero interpreter fallback.

The delivery ZIP places its WUHB in sd-card/wiiu/apps/ and the tiny original blob in sd-card/ctr2wup/romfs-test.bin. Copy the contents of sd-card to SD root when available, launch CTR2WUP Asset Files 0.16.0 in Aroma, and follow its short existing RomFS guide. It reads root/nested/Unicode files, lists/seeks, denies writes and exits. This test needs no ROM or key. A missing/changed blob fails visibly and is preserved.

Reports appear under sd:/ctr2wup/, using result-compiled-romfs-file-0.16.0-*.txt plus any full trace. Return those TXT files together in a ZIP; include the observed exit result if it was not recorded. No new physical success is claimed, and the earlier Save/RomFS result round in console-retest.md remains pending. Completed long native/ input guides do not need to be repeated for this asset test.

Remaining work¶

Actual Mario asset/revision integration, other cartridge/archive selectors, extdata/retail saves, console SD behavior and Windows conversion integration remain unfinished. Signature authenticity is unverified. This diagnostic does not enable a retail profile, render PICA scenes or establish console speed.

Maintained source: docs/romfs-assets.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Local encrypted-ROM preparation (0.16.0)¶

On October 12, 2026 the user expanded the intended injector to accept either already-decrypted dumps or encrypted dumps with locally supplied material from their own 3DS. This amends the original brief's initial decrypted-only input scope; the original brief is preserved. The final product remains a Windows GUI that converts a supported revision into a playable per-game Wii U WUP.

What is implemented¶

The shared Python engine now supplies prepare-rom and import-rom-keys. They are developer commands; the Windows GUI and on-console extraction helper are not implemented. Decryption is local preparation before the existing checked loader/translator. No platform key values, console dumps or commercial assets are bundled. Already-decrypted input requires no keys or crypto dependency.

Input layer Implemented behavior
CIA outer content AES-CBC with a local plaintext title key, or a local normal common key plus the ticket's wrapped title key; content-index IV and full main-content TMD SHA-256 check
NCCH v2 Exheader/access descriptor, ExeFS header/files/padding and RomFS AES-CTR; primary and secondary key selection, unaligned stream offsets and 1 MiB bounded chunks
NCCH crypto methods Standard KeyX slot 2c, and methods 1 / 0a / 0b using slots 25 / 18 / 1b; seeded secondary KeyY derivation and seed check
NCSD cartridge Bounds/overlap checks and extraction of main partition 0; other partitions are not exported or fully inspected
Local key imports Plaintext AES key database retail NCCH KeyX records; seeddb base-title seeds; explicitly declared decrypted title-key database base-title records

The prepared private output contains game.cxi and preparation-report.json. It preserves the main executable and RomFS bytes; CIA auxiliary contents, tickets and key files are not copied into that output. The existing inspector validates exheader/section/file hashes and code decompression before atomic publication. A small executable-only upload can then be made with prepare-intake. Source files and existing destinations remain unchanged; failed staging is removed.

Wrong/missing keys or seeds, damaged content, malformed ranges, overlaps and unsupported modes fail visibly. Integrity checks do not authenticate Nintendo signatures. RomFS superblock and canonical three-level IVFC verification are implemented. All declared hash/data blocks, final padding and bounded Level-3 metadata are checked before preparation is published. Unsupported layouts fail explicitly; commercial asset/runtime integration remains unfinished. A derived CXI is not a playable Wii U game or a complete reconstruction of every cartridge/CIA content.

A key set, rather than one personal master key¶

CIA wrapping and NCCH encryption are different layers. The necessary material can include platform KeyX components, a CIA common/title key and a title-specific seed. Some platform keys are shared across consoles. A console-specific SD key such as movable.sed is not sufficient by itself for these standalone game files. The key scrambler is an implemented public algorithm, not a bundled platform key. 3DS input keys and Wii U packaging inputs are separate configurations.

Exporting data requires work on a homebrew-enabled 3DS. A normal Windows USB connection to a stock console does not provide access to its keys. Existing GodMode9 can decrypt game images on-console and export support data; its documentation covers support-file creation and bootrom access. CTR2WUP imports already obtained files. It does not install a console exploit, dump boot9/OTP/NAND, fetch key databases or currently supply a companion.

The local AES-key importer requires decrypted aeskeydb-format records and imports only standard retail NCCH KeyX slots with empty identifiers. It rejects encrypted databases, ambiguous duplicates and malformed metadata; unrelated and devkit-only records are ignored. It does not extract keys from boot9.bin or aes_keys.txt. seeddb uses little-endian title IDs; decrypted title-key records store title IDs in the ticket's big-endian byte order. Database plaintext declaration is not cryptographically authenticated: the recovered CIA must still match its TMD.

A console-bound/personalized ticket needs an already recovered local title key; EC ticket depersonalization is not implemented. Fixed-key NCCH, v1 counters, unknown crypto methods, raw encrypted SD/NAND, updates and DLC are unsupported. Actual user-console exports and native Windows behavior remain untested.

Developer workflow¶

Keep all real dumps, keys, prepared games and derived code under private local storage. The import output is a key store and must remain on the user's PC; do not upload it. Only file paths are accepted as key configuration; reports, repr and commands never contain key values. Keys are read in-process and are not passed to compilers or included in Wii U output. The current private store uses local files; Windows protected credential storage is a later GUI requirement.

python -m pip install '.[decryption]'
python -m host import-rom-keys --aeskeydb "private/aeskeydb.bin" \
  --seeddb "private/seeddb.bin" --decrypted-titlekeys "private/decTitleKeys.bin" \
  --output "private/3ds-key-store"
python -m host prepare-rom "private/owned-game.cia" \
  --keys-config "private/3ds-key-store/keys.json" --output "private/prepared-game"
python -m host prepare-intake "private/prepared-game/game.cxi" \
  --output "private/small-intake"

Each optional database argument can be omitted. Missing material is named by role/title rather than silently substituted. Advanced schema-1 JSON supports ncch_key_x_files, cia_common_key_files, cia_title_key_files and title_seed_files; map slot/index/title identity to a local 16-byte binary file path. A decrypted input can go directly through prepare-rom without --keys-config.

Verification and remaining acceptance¶

Original synthetic fixtures cover all four NCCH methods, seed/no-seed, both CIA layers, mixed ExeFS keys, padding, compressed code, Unicode paths, chunk boundaries, wrong inputs and non-overwriting publication. NIST AES CBC/CTR vectors and a separate bit-string/byte-carry scrambler reference check the crypto primitives. No user key or game is used in these tests.

The original encrypted execution fixture runs through decrypt → inspect → small intake → external AOT → desktop execution → Wii U RPX/WUHB cross-build. The original guest completes its frames and exits; this is a pipeline check, not commercial execution or console acceptance. Run:

python3 tools/validate-rom-preparation.py --wiiu \
  --output artifacts/reports/my-encrypted-pipeline

Binary-format facts were reviewed in GodMode9 commit f3b9ec7a50b8c8459f62b87bfd7b7214b6f6bda2 (ncch, keydb, ticket, ticketdb and seedsave sources) and the existing audited loader sources. No GodMode9 code, bundled key database or executable was incorporated. An actual locally exported key set and owned encrypted dump, clean Windows execution and broader integrity profiles remain acceptance work. PICA, guest audio, retail saves and installable commercial WUP conversion remain separate unfinished milestones.

Use prepare-assets on the recovered CXI to prepare a private checked Level-3 blob and manifest. Guest filenames remain virtual; no archive filenames are extracted onto host paths. See romfs-assets.md.

Batch CIA 3DS Decryptor investigation compares its methods, licenses and input/output handoff. Supported decrypted .cci (NCSD) can enter the same checked pipeline; no batch executable is bundled or executed.

Maintained source: docs/encrypted-input.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

CIA intake and the attachment transfer limit¶

October 13 current input: the user supplied a CCI described as decrypted, but it exceeds the executor's 32 MiB transfer limit. No byte from that attachment has been inspected; filename identity and decryption remain unverified. On the Windows 11 PC with Python 3.12+, install the released CTR2WUP Python wheel, then:

py -m host prepare-rom "C:\path\owned-game.cci" --output "C:\path\new-Mario-prepared"
py -m host prepare-intake "C:\path\new-Mario-prepared\game.cxi" --output "C:\path\new-Mario-intake"

Return new-Mario-intake\CTR2WUP-intake.zip and new-Mario-prepared\preparation-report.json privately in this chat. The output directory must be new. NCSD cartridge input uses the same content-based Python route as .3ds; renaming or uploading the full CCI again is unnecessary. The original file remains unchanged and stays on the PC for later RomFS/module work. The archive contains a receipt and, for supported decrypted input, a derived executable-only CXI below 30 MiB; it excludes RomFS, tickets, certificates and keys. A report-only result still requires resolving its stated blocking error. The standalone .NET CIA helper below does not accept CCI.

The user's direct 0.20.0 export reported unsupported_ncch_version for version 0. CFA data partitions use version 0, while supported application CXIs use version 2. The two-step route selects and validates the supported main partition without inspecting unsupported auxiliary layouts. An original v2-executable/v0-data fixture verifies this route; which partition caused the user's error remains unknown until their small intake arrives. A separate planned feature addresses strict decrypted CFA inspection. Do not alter the original header or infer commercial compatibility from successful intake.

October 11 update: the user returned a valid report-only intake ZIP. The helper reports encrypted_cia_content; no executable view was exported. This identifies the next input as a decrypted executable/CIA copy. The receipt is retained privately, and the original CIA bytes have not been independently inspected.

October 12: v0.15.0 now has a local Python engine prepare-rom stage and supported key/seed database importer. With locally obtained material it can prepare a checked main CXI, then feed that into the existing small export. See encrypted-input.md. No real console export has been tested, and the standalone .NET helper remains unchanged/decrypted-only. Do not upload key files or a key store; preparation runs on the user's PC.

Updated October 7, 2026 (America/New_York). The user supplied 0004000000054000 Super Mario 3D Land (CTR-P-AREE) (v0.3.0) (U).legit.cia. Its transfer into this execution workspace failed because the attachment exceeds the tool's 32 MiB limit. Its bytes, hashes, encryption status, region and revision have not been inspected. The filename suggests the selected USA title; it is not verification of content, authenticity or decryption.

CIA is an installation container. A supported, fully decrypted base-application CIA can supply the same executable NCCH/ExeFS data as a CXI. Version 0.5.0 adds strict bounded CIA metadata/content inspection, checked executable selection and small private executable export. The pinned external lifter already accepts CIA. Commercial compatibility remains disabled.

Smallest local step on Windows¶

The separate CTR2WUP-CIA-Intake console utility is an engineering intake helper; it does not inject, install or run a game. The Windows x64 distribution includes its .NET runtime, licenses and third-party notices. It requires no Python, SDK, separately installed .NET, donor title or encryption key.

  1. Extract CTR2WUP-CIA-Intake-Windows-x64-0.5.0.zip into a folder.
  2. Drag your CIA onto CTR2WUP-CIA-Intake.exe. Alternatively, open the EXE and paste the CIA's local path when prompted.
  3. Upload the resulting CTR2WUP-intake.zip, found in the new <original CIA filename>.ctr2wup-intake folder beside the CIA.

The helper reads the original without modifying it and refuses existing outputs. It hashes the source and validates CIA bounds/bitmap/TMD groups/content digests, ticket-title agreement and executable NCCH/ExeFS stored hashes. It exports a derived executable-only CXI and a JSON receipt into an archive smaller than 32 MiB. It omits RomFS/plain/logo assets, the ticket, certificates and keys. ExeFS contains the private executable and may include game icon/banner material; keep this archive private. The derived header is unsigned and explicitly has no RomFS. It is an analysis view, not a complete or playable ROM.

If outer CIA or inner NCCH encryption is declared, the archive contains only the small report with the specific blocking layer. Do not infer encryption from .legit or any filename. A report-only export does not unlock executable analysis; fully decrypted executable data will then be required. No decryption or credential collection is performed. Malformed/unsupported files fail visibly. The executable view limit is 30 MiB; metadata allocations are bounded and content hashing is streamed. The full original file remains needed locally for later RomFS assets/module investigation and eventual conversion.

The helper's .NET logic passes original-fixture tests on Linux. Its Windows x64 self-contained EXE is cross-published; native Windows execution remains unverified. No user game byte or credential is included in the utility bundle.

Shared Python engine¶

For developers with Python 3.12+:

python -m host inspect "private/game.cia"
python -m host prepare-intake "private/game.cia" --output "private/game-intake"
python -m host analyze-title "private/game-intake/executable-view.cxi" --lifter --output "private/game-analysis"

The engine supports standard 0x2020 type-0/version-0 CIA, SHA-256 signed-header layouts, version-1 TMD tables, sparse content bitmaps, exact ticket/TMD identity, and content-index-0 executable selection. Update/DLC/system titles are outside the first-title route. CIA metadata inspection can report declared encryption; full executable intake rejects encrypted content. TMD/section hashes establish integrity, not authentic signatures or support for the game.

The Python export performs BLZ/code-revision inspection and verifies the derived CXI preserves the original executable revision. The .NET exporter validates stored ExeFS bytes; cloud host intake performs the subsequent decompression, segment and revision checks. Both exporters produce identical derived CXI bytes for the original tested fixtures. Neither exports credentials or RomFS payloads.

Reproduction and evidence¶

tests/test_cia.py covers 16 original cases: decrypted/sparse contents, both encryption layers, damaged hashes/bitmap/bounds, identity mismatch, missing executable, update refusal, output preservation and RomFS-free export. tools/validate-cia-helper.py executes 13 .NET cases, including a >32 MiB original RomFS placeholder and compressed code, and checks byte-for-byte agreement with the Python engine. No commercial test material is used.

Build the helper with .NET 10.0.401, using intermediate/output paths outside the source tree. Windows publishing is pinned to the .NET 10.0.12 runtime:

dotnet build tools/CiaIntake/CiaIntake.csproj -c Release -p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath=/absolute/artifacts/cia-obj/ -p:OutputPath=/absolute/artifacts/cia-linux/
python tools/validate-cia-helper.py --helper /absolute/artifacts/cia-linux/CTR2WUP-CIA-Intake.dll --output artifacts/cia-helper-report
dotnet publish tools/CiaIntake/CiaIntake.csproj -c Release -r win-x64 --self-contained true -p:PublishSingleFile=true -p:EnableCompressionInSingleFile=true -p:IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true -p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath=/absolute/artifacts/cia-win-obj/ -p:PublishDir=/absolute/artifacts/cia-windows/

The .NET Windows runtime is MIT with additional preserved third-party notices, copied from the exact restored runtime package. The helper archive includes LICENSE-DOTNET.txt and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES-DOTNET.txt; the SDK is not bundled. CIA layout facts were checked against the pinned lifter's src/rom.rs and documented container offsets. The reader/exporter code is original.

Maintained source: docs/cia-intake.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Original encrypted homebrew packaging¶

Version 0.4.0 implements an original, scoped encrypted writer and a separate decrypting verifier. No package has been installed or launched on a physical Wii U. The offline test uses invented test certificates and a synthetic key; it cannot produce an installable title. Commercial conversion remains disabled.

Implemented profile¶

host.package_writer packs original code/content/meta staging into four encrypted 0x2001 contents: FST, code, content and meta. It supports application IDs beginning 00050000, explicit version/group/OS fields, UTF-8 names, nested and empty directories, empty files and at most 1 GiB per content group. Files start at 32-byte boundaries; contents are padded with zeros to 32 KiB.

AES-128-CBC uses the content index followed by zero bytes as its IV. A fresh random title key is wrapped with the locally supplied common key using the title ID as IV. TMD content records contain SHA-1 of padded plaintext plus 12 zero bytes; SHA-256 protects the content-record group and content-info tables. The ticket contains one content-access bitmap. Common-key index 0, unpersonalized tickets, RSA-2048/SHA-256 header layouts and absent signatures are the only implemented profile. Hashed 0x2003/H3 writing, disc/update/shared contents, authentic signatures and retail-console installation are unsupported.

The writer checks RPX structure and XML identity, rejects symlinks and unsafe or case-colliding paths, refuses existing outputs, detects changed inputs, checks disk space and keeps unfinished output in a temporary directory. It streams content with 1 MiB buffers; bounded FST/name metadata is held in memory. FST logical cluster placement, ownership, COS permissions/resources and metadata/ artwork are research settings awaiting installer/console evidence.

host.package_integrity independently reads the result without calling the writer's FST, inventory or serialization functions. It unwraps the key, verifies every padded plaintext hash, bounds/tree/parents/names/content assignments/file ranges and zero padding, checks decrypted XML against TMD identity and launch entry, and optionally compares every file hash and directory with source staging. Certificate layout and CP/XS issuer agreement are checked; a trusted root and signature authentication are not established. The existing structural validator still works without a key, with an explicitly narrower result.

Local inputs and commands¶

Install the licensed crypto dependency in your Python environment:

python -m pip install '.[packaging]'

Copy packaging/local-inputs.example.json into an ignored private configuration directory. Its paths resolve relative to the configuration file:

{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "signature_policy": "homebrew_unsigned",
  "common_key_file": "common.key",
  "certificate_file": "title.cert"
}

The common-key file must contain exactly 16 raw bytes. The certificate file is a user-managed binary chain with its parents and one RSA-2048 CP and XS signer. Establish its provenance locally. No key, certificate or donor is downloaded or provided by CTR2WUP. Keep these files outside the staged folders; their paths and values are omitted from packaging reports. Do not paste keys into command-line arguments or conversations. The explicit policy acknowledges unsigned homebrew; it does not establish which patched installer/environment will accept the result.

python -m host stage-title PATH.rpx --title-id YOUR_APPLICATION_ID --name "My title" --output private/my-staging
python -m host pack-title private/my-staging --config private/packaging/local-inputs.json --output private/my-package
python -m host verify-package private/my-package --common-key-file private/packaging/common.key --expected-staging private/my-staging
python -m host validate-package private/my-package

stage-title creates original XML/checker artwork and an empty content/; it produces staging, not a WUP. pack-title publishes the .app, TMD, ticket, certificate and report only after successful independent decrypted/file checks. Its installable_wup field explicitly awaits installer and console validation. The report separates decrypted integrity, unauthenticated signatures and unrun installation. It never claims an authentic or proven installable title.

Title identity¶

IDs are explicit; no globally safe homebrew range is assumed. Staging/packing does not reserve an ID or inspect installed console titles. Check your console inventory and local projects before using an ID. The existing explicit local registry command remains available after structural validation:

python packaging/validate.py PATH --registry private/title-registry.json --reserve "my project identity"

The registry covers only this user's local allocations. Same-owner rebuilds may update the package revision while retaining the title ID; a different owner cannot take it. Serialize concurrent registry writes. TMD digests and randomized ciphertext are package revisions; title/save identity remains the explicit ID. Rebuild/save continuity, installation, Menu launch, exit and uninstall await physical-console tests. No installation is automated.

Evidence and dependency audit¶

20 new offline tests exercise streaming across chunk boundaries, empty/Unicode paths, OpenSSL independently decrypting the ticket and all contents, wrong keys, ciphertext corruption, malicious FSTs with repaired hashes, source mutation, unsafe inputs, cancellation and output preservation. tools/validate-packaging.py also packages the real WUT-built RPX, verifies all staged files and exercises both CLI paths. Its synthetic inputs/package exist only in a temporary directory; the retained report records that they cannot authenticate a console title.

The encryption dependency is cryptography 50.0.0, Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause; CTR2WUP selects the BSD-3-Clause grant and preserves its notice in docs/licenses/cryptography-LICENSE.BSD. The library and its native dependencies remain external Python packages; no crypto binary is bundled here.

Binary layout/alignment/hash facts were reviewed against makefst's fst.h, tmd.h, tik.h, certs.h at e363897e29109e6f1125d60376822c3768d3c2d4 and the previously audited Java/C# packer format descriptions. The writer/reader are original implementations; no source, binary, XML template, certificate or key from those packers is adopted. This is a scoped research profile, not a full Wii U package-format implementation.

The MIT-root makefst candidate bundles GPLv2-or-later PolarSSL, MIT ezxml and Unlicense miniz. Its plaintext 0x2000 route, test signing and production certificate output did not supply the required encrypted profile or certificate provenance; it was not incorporated or executed. The nuspacker/CNUS_Packer checkouts have no identified license grant and remain research references only. See foundation-audit.md for pins and licensing constraints.

Maintained source: docs/packaging.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Compiler-built homebrew evidence¶

The optional v0.14 developer workflow builds original C applications with the normal devkitARM 3DSX CRT/newlib and clean unmodified libctru source pinned at 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425. It does not replace __appInit, library startup, locks, threads or input functions with test stubs. The trusted original ELF segments are placed in an original synthetic NCCH for the existing checked loader; this is not a general ELF or 3DSX importer.

source /workspace/ctr2wup-tools/environment/activate.sh
bash tools/install-arm-fixtures.sh
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --output artifacts/reports/libctru-local
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application interactive --wiiu \
  --output artifacts/reports/libctru-console
python3 tools/validate.py --compiled-homebrew

The source path defaults to ../ctr2wup-deps/libctru; use --libctru-source and --devkitarm elsewhere. The tool verifies a clean pin, builds a temporary library. install-arm-fixtures.sh extracts devkitARM from the same immutable official SDK image, verifies its compiler hash and checks out the clean library pin without replacing existing dependencies. CTR2WUP_SDK_ROOT and CTR2WUP_LIBCTRU_SOURCE can select other setup paths. The validator builds a temporary library, and preserves compiler logs, hashes, generated AOT and guest results under the requested new output directory. It also supports --sanitize, --cc-prefix, --run-prefix and emulated PPC Linux --static. Dependencies stay external. Console compilation stages generated sources under controlled relative paths so chosen output and checkout paths can contain spaces/Unicode; RPX/WUHB are checked before copying them to the requested location. A future native Windows toolchain setup needs its own validation.

The applications test:

  • boot: normal CRT/library startup, reaching main and orderly process exit.
  • threaded: real threadCreate/threadJoin/threadFree, LightLock, a 1,024-count shared result and initialized C __thread data isolated between contexts.
  • graphics: real libctru graphics/event thread, CPU-linear double buffers, checked cache flush, shared GSP queue and VBlank waits. Three full RGB565 frames must decode to green/red/green on top and blue below; six PPMs are preserved.
  • input: real hidScanInput, held/down/up edges, signed Circle Pad and touch coordinates, including release, through three standard shared-ring updates.
