  • filesystem: normal savedata mount and newlib file I/O, Japanese UTF-16 path, delete, explicit commit, fresh-process reload and independently checked snapshot bytes. Corrupt snapshots are rejected and preserved. Its optional RPX/WUHB writes only an original private diagnostic save.

  • interactive: standard input drives three exact top-screen colors, bottom touch pixels at (17,23)/(319,239), release and the guest's own B-button exit. Its optional RPX/WUHB uses live VPAD input and the same checked AOT/GSP runtime.

  • romfs: ordinary root/nested/Unicode file reads, seek/EOF, directory listing, write denial and unmount through unmodified libctru's Level-3 parser. Its current-process view contains only original assets; see romfs-assets.md.

  • transfer: standard GX DMA, fill, tiled display transfer, raw texture copy and cache commands with checked pixels, padding and event waits; see GX transfers.

  • draw: standard GX/PICA lists, owned tiled targets, retained shader/state, exact target/transfer/display assertions and own PASS/exit. See compiled guest draws for the bounded fragment contract.

The default validator now runs all nine applications. The current console kit includes Native, interactive, guarded interactive, Save and RomFS variants. The RomFS guest also passes under ASan/UBSan and emulated big-endian PPC Linux; this does not establish physical Wii U execution.

All require a real guest PASS, ExitProcess and zero interpreter fallback. Pixel checks inspect every pixel, not only a debug message. Input tests use the public HID producer; no host writes a private application result or skips guest code. The interactive program includes a 1 ms sleep per frame to expose boundaries and avoid polling between presentation and the next input sample.

v0.14 explicitly enables standalone APT and mounts an empty read-only private virtual SDMC for these applications. Normal APT initialization, wakeup/event thread, Old-family model query, FS initialization/archive mount and APT exit now run successfully. The boot checks its active state and aptMainLoop. Use --application boot --trace-startup to capture every request. The remaining startup lookup failures are APT:S, APT:A and optional ptm:sysm. Privileged/system services and general applets are not supplied. See filesystem.md for exact state, ownership, persistence and limits. No NCCH/IVFC authentication or retail save integration, DSP/audio, CRO, general PICA commands/physical mappings or commercial compatibility is claimed; the bounded GX/draw profiles are documented separately. Native diagnostic tone remains platform audio; v0.14.5 interactive/guarded completion is user-reported. Reviewed named results, fresh-launch saves and RomFS evidence are retained; new draw/launch-audio physical results and full Windows conversion remain pending. Do not enable a commercial profile from these homebrew passes.

The original source is under tests/homebrew. Optional locally generated guest images link external libctru/newlib/compiler support. The library's full license is preserved at licenses/libctru.txt; retain this and the existing runtime notices when distributing the compiled interactive artifact. The MPL 2.0 3DSX CRT's corresponding unmodified assembly/linker/spec sources, full license and section/relocation audit are supplied under licenses/devkitarm-crtls/. No ROM, game assets, keys, certificates or proprietary SDK is part of this workflow.

Maintained source: docs/compiled-homebrew.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Controlled NCCH guest execution (0.2.0)¶

This original, redistributable ARM fixture is the first executable guest pipeline: NCCH inspection and hash checks → checked segment extraction → original generated C → portable guest loader/SVC runtime → raw TV/GamePad framebuffer presentation. Desktop execution and Wii U cross-compilation pass. Physical Wii U execution is awaiting testing. This fixture uses an explicit harness I/O contract; it is not a stock 3DS graphical application and does not implement HID, GSP or PICA.

Reproduce from source¶

Use Python 3.12+, native GCC for desktop execution, and the installed official devkitPPC/WUT SDK for console builds. Run from the complete source root:

python3 -m host make-fixture --output "artifacts/original guest"
python3 -m host run-fixture "artifacts/original guest/ctr2wup-guest.cxi" --output "artifacts/guest AOT" --frames 2 --buttons 1 --touch --touch-x 100 --touch-y 80
python3 -m host run-fixture "artifacts/original guest/ctr2wup-guest.cxi" --output "artifacts/guest interpreted" --frames 2 --buttons 1 --touch --touch-x 100 --touch-y 80 --mode interpreter
python3 -m host build "artifacts/original guest/ctr2wup-guest.cxi" --target guest-fixture --output "artifacts/guest console"

Each output directory must be new. Failure preserves compiler/execution logs and removes incomplete staging. run-fixture writes real guest-generated top.ppm (400×240), bottom.ppm (320×240), generated C, the native runner, logs and a JSON report. The installed wheel includes the bounded translator, but C compilation requires the full source; set CTR2WUP_PROJECT_ROOT to that source root.

tools/generate-guest.py deliberately regenerates the tracked original fixture sources after fixture changes. Console builds compare these sources against the inspected input and reject stale results. Setup does not regenerate source. Only the exact original code, segment layout and program ID are execution-enabled. Unknown commercial/homebrew profiles fail before publishing an output.

Guest loading and I/O contract¶

The unsigned, decrypted NCCH v2 has program ID 000400000ff00f02, 340 bytes of ARM text at 0x00100000, one 4 KiB data page at 0x08000000, and 691,200 bytes of BSS for framebuffers. Text is read/execute, rodata is read-only, data/BSS and stack/TLS are read/write. Padding/BSS, the 16 KiB stack below 0x10000000, and the TLS page at 0x1ff82000 are zeroed. Initial SP is 0x10000000; entry uses user ARM mode. No guest pointer becomes a host pointer. Allocation is bounded to 64 MiB; page alignment, descriptor sizes, permissions, overlaps and wraparound are checked. Non-page-aligned data/BSS layouts are explicitly rejected for now. The TLS page exists; v0.6.0 implements its user read-only CP15 access and a separate original SRV/CFG startup probe. See shared-services.md; the framebuffer contract below still uses harness input and is not HID/GSP.

Data-page offset Harness/guest meaning
0 Host supplies buttons; bit0 changes the top framebuffer color
4, 8, 12 Host supplies touch X, Y and active flag; guest checks bounds
16 Guest increments completed-frame count after writing both buffers
20 Host requests exit; guest calls ExitProcess at its next loop entry
32 Original debug message for OutputDebugString

Top pixels begin at 0x08001000; bottom pixels follow 400×240 RGBA8 pixels. The ARM program fills both images with STR loops, writes a white touch pixel, increments its frame count, sleeps for 1 ms and repeats. Presentation reads little-endian pixels through checked mappings and converts them to the host's RGBA representation. These addresses are a fixture contract, not emulated 3DS shared-memory/HID/GSP addresses. No GPU draw commands are executed.

Implemented SVC subset¶

One guest CPU/thread runs under instruction quotas; callers supply monotonic virtual time in nanoseconds. No host thread is created for a guest thread.

SVC Implemented behavior/register contract
03 ExitProcess Guest enters exited state
0a SleepThread r0:r1 nanoseconds; zero yields, positive sleeps; negative/overflow faults
17 CreateEvent Reset type r1: OneShot0 or Sticky1; result r0, handle r1; Pulse unsupported
18 SignalEvent Handle r0, result r0; wakes the controlled waiter
19 ClearEvent Handle r0, result r0
23 CloseHandle Handle r0, result r0; retains objects owned by other handles/waiter
24 WaitSynchronization1 Handle r0, timeout r2:r3; zero polls, positive times out, negative waits indefinitely
27 DuplicateHandle Input handle r1, result r0, new handle r1
28 GetSystemTick Virtual time scaled to 268,111,856 Hz; low/high r0:r1
2d ConnectToPort Bounded srv: name at r1; result/typed session handle r0/r1
32 SendSyncRequest Checked TLS IPC; restricted SRV registration/CFG configuration reads
3d OutputDebugString Checked pointer r0, length r1, maximum 256 bytes; success result r0=0

There are 32 event objects and 64 generation-tagged handles. Stale/closed handles, timeouts and exhaustion return explicit results. Unknown SVCs fault visibly. v0.6.0 adds 16 session objects sharing the handle table; event/session operations are type checked. Multi-thread scheduling, timers, memory-control SVCs, general IPC and FS/APT/HID/GSP remain unimplemented. Only the CFG reads in shared-services.md are implemented. Interface facts were checked against audited research sources and the public libctru SVC header; their implementation was not copied.

Console controls and expected evidence¶

Launch ctr2wup-guest-fixture.wuhb with a compatible Aroma setup or the RPX with an RPX-capable launcher. It starts in guest mode. The native diagnostic also contains this guest; Y toggles modes. Plus restarts the guest; B asks the guest to exit; Minus exits the host application. A changes the next guest top color and plays the native test tone. A calibrated touch changes the bottom color and draws one white guest pixel. Hold A/touch through a completed guest frame.

The TV has a small host status overlay; the remaining image and GamePad image come from guest ARM memory writes. Quotas allow 50,000 guest instructions per display iteration, so a full guest frame spans multiple display iterations. This deliberately bounded correctness test does not measure game performance. Record both outputs, increasing guest frame count, successful color/touch/exit/ restart, and sd:/ctr2wup/native-*.log; follow console-testing.md.

Verified desktop evidence¶

Two AOT frames with button1 and touch(100,80) execute 1,382,534 guest instructions, zero fallback instructions, four SVCs and one debug message, then exit normally. Top RGB is 10 bb 22; bottom RGB is 77 22 bb, with a white touch pixel. Interpreter and AOT frames have identical SHA-256 values:

top.ppm     267878f6f7665b30bf43465c48d9b55094e8fe7d8fc694585e1afe74c30bae81
bottom.ppm  6cadca972cf65a4842d8d8a73bd6a4b806bf1706a2c5b826cd136d21a371d06d

The original fixture SHA-256 is 004d03a5a0eca2679f2efbf6f887590fbf51ed984649056e78702c43af968210. The separate executable revision is 38820d9091dcf0eb863b5c74c04536c9e765aaf39f4719f017205298015a3472. Kernel/loader tests cover faults, events, sleeps, ticks and actual interpreter/AOT guest state/memory matches, both normally and under ASan/UBSan. All hardware, stock 3DS homebrew, commercial gameplay, saves and WUP claims remain open.

The separate v0.7.0 original heap lifecycle probe exercises real memory SVCs without extending this framebuffer harness contract. All three RPXs embed its original interpreter/AOT self-test; external AOT is also verified on desktop and emulated PPC. See guest-memory.md and collect the additional native-mode result per console-testing.md. GPU/DSP physical memory and broader boot remain pending.

Maintained source: docs/guest-fixture.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Build on Windows¶

Windows development tooling is verified. The finished injector GUI, native Wii U conversion and playable WUP output remain under development.

The hosted baseline passes Python fixtures and all 17 normal C suites on Windows Server 2022, including Unicode guest compilation/execution. Three filesystem/privilege-specific Python tests are explicitly skipped; their platform behavior remains unverified. The deep Windows run also passes real pinned external ARM/Thumb/VFP generation, 14 execution probes, runtime API checks, Unicode output and a .NET 10.0.401 WPF tooling build. The WPF probe verifies the SDK; it is not CTR2WUP's final application.

Prerequisites¶

  • Python 3.12 or newer and Git.
  • MSYS2 UCRT64 with native GCC and Make for host-generated C and portable tests.
  • Official devkitPro Windows tooling with devkitPPC/WUT for Wii U RPX/WUHB builds.
  • Rust 1.95.0 for the optional audited external lifter.
  • .NET SDK 10.0.401 for the separate CIA intake helper and WPF tooling probe.

The Linux Docker SDK installer does not produce native Windows dependencies. Use the official Windows SDK route and preserve download/signature verification. Native gcc executes host fixtures; the PowerPC cross-compiler cannot run them. Keep the two compilers distinct in PATH.

Source CLI and original fixtures¶

Run these commands from the repository root, using python or your local Python launcher. Install the declared crypto extras before exercising encrypted inputs:

python -m pip install '.[packaging,decryption]'
python -m host --help
make check-python PYTHON=python
make check-c-portable
python -m host make-fixture --output "artifacts/Windows fixture 日本語"
python -m host run-fixture "artifacts/Windows fixture 日本語/ctr2wup-guest.cxi" --output "artifacts/Windows frames 日本語"

Run Make commands in UCRT64 with native GCC available. make check-c-portable runs normal C suites; Linux ASan/UBSan results are separate evidence. The vertex suite includes every unsigned 8-bit/16-bit index value and original multi-stream/interleaved gathers; it uses portable byte loads rather than native unaligned index casts. These checks do not prove Wii U rendering or conversion. Generated fixtures are original and require no ROM, console key or commercial asset. See encrypted inputs for private local key handling.

The software scene suite also runs under portable Windows GCC. It checks PICA-format loader/MOV shader/raster behavior independently of a Wii U build. With the verified native SDK installed, python -m host build --target scene-check --output artifacts/scene-check builds the focused original console diagnostic. See software scenes for the accepted subset and evidence limits.

The Python wheel contains the CLI, translator and preparation modules. It does not bundle the SDK, GUI or native runtime/build recipes. Extract the matching source distribution for Wii U builds; CTR2WUP_PROJECT_ROOT can point an installed CLI to that distribution.

Optional external generation¶

In PowerShell, use tools/install-translator-windows.ps1. It builds the audited source pin with Rust 1.95.0 and writes a provenance receipt. Then use the native GCC/Make environment:

python tools/validate-upstream.py --service-api --heap-api --kernel-api --hid-api --gsp-api --relay-api --fs-api --apt-api --thread-api --multiple-api --output artifacts/windows-upstream
python -m host run-fixture "artifacts/Windows fixture 日本語/ctr2wup-guest.cxi" --backend upstream --output "artifacts/Windows AOT frames 日本語"

The manual Windows development validation workflow reproduces these checks and the WPF SDK probe, preserving logs as Actions artifacts. Upstream integration records ABI, pin and notice limits. Optional Unicorn/Zakuro/reference comparisons require their own pinned tools; the Windows baseline is not evidence that those optional comparisons ran there.

CIA intake helper¶

The v0.5.0 CTR2WUP-CIA-Intake console utility has a separate self-contained Windows x64 bundle. It needs no Python/SDK/runtime installation and exports private analysis data. See CIA intake for controls, build pins and its user-machine acceptance check. It is separate from the planned injector GUI.

Remaining Windows acceptance¶

Full native devkitPPC conversion, personal decrypted/keyed inputs, untested filesystem/privilege behavior, installer acceptance and the complete GUI need their own evidence. The end-to-end product must convert the selected title, install its WUP, launch from Wii U Menu and support gameplay/audio/save/reload. Original synthetic packing checks and development SDK probes do not close these gates. Follow milestones and GitHub development for the current record.

Maintained source: docs/build-windows.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Portable CPU and AOT evidence¶

This is original C11 code, independent of desktop threads, a flat 4 GiB host reservation, SIMD, or host byte order. Addresses remain guest uint32_t values. Mapped read/write/execute permissions, end-of-address-space overflow, alignment, aliasing, capacity and overlap faults are explicit. Writes invalidate code snapshots through executable aliases. The exclusive model supports one deterministically scheduled guest CPU, and invalidates its monitor on writes; it is not a multicore ARM memory-model implementation.

Interpreter subset: ARM conditions, data-processing ALU/shifts/flags, branch and link/exchange, MUL/MLA, immediate/shifted-register byte/word transfers, unsigned immediate preindexed LDRH/STRH without writeback (v0.10), word LDREX/STREX, user read-only CP15 TLS MRC (Rd other than PC) and observable SVC exits; Thumb16 shifts/ALU, high-register operations, branch/exchange, selected loads/stores/SP-relative operations and SVC exits. Version 0.12 adds checked user-mode ARM LDM/STM, Thumb16 PUSH/POP/LDMIA/STMIA and original-AOT register BLX calls; see cpu-stack.md for scope and reference evidence. Unaligned transfers fault deliberately. Exception-return/general VFP arithmetic, Thumb long calls, ARM user-bank/exception-return multiple forms, other halfword transfers and privileged instructions are unsupported. The supported subset is research code, not a complete ARM11.

translator/aot.py specializes a smaller flat-executable subset into direct C instruction blocks. It fails with guest PC/opcode context for unsupported code. Version 0.11.0 also specializes word LDREX/STREX directly, with the same bounded monitor/generation/fault behavior as the interpreter. The queued GSP probe checks successful updates and a device publication forcing retry across execution paths. Each block is instruction-budgeted. The dispatcher keys address plus ARM/Thumb state, records missing blocks with module hash and PC, and permits explicit interpreter fallback. Invalidated code cannot execute a stale translated block. The dispatcher binary-searches strictly increasing block-address tables and retains linear lookup for unsorted or duplicate-address tables.

make -C tests/cpu check sanitize exercises explicit golden values, randomized integer carry/overflow against widened arithmetic, permissions/faults/aliases, exclusive monitoring, branching, condition skips, SVC state/logging, budget, fallback and invalidation. ASan/UBSan are enabled for a separate run. Python tests verify generator reproducibility and rejection behavior. The original translator also specializes this TLS read. v0.6.0's separate 89-instruction original startup probe matches interpreter/original/external AOT state and memory through actual SRV/CFG SVCs; see shared-services.md. v0.13 adds user CP15 ARMv6 DMB/DSB ordering, UADD8/USUB8/SEL with GE flags, SMLAxy with signed halfwords and sticky Q, FPSCR transport and checked scalar VLDR/VSTR word transport. See guest-threads.md. Other CP15/cache operations remain unsupported, including emitter no-ops. General interpreter VFP arithmetic remains absent; external arithmetic rejects unsupported controls. v0.7.0's 151-instruction heap lifecycle probe executes allocation, permissions, partial free and queries through all three backends with zero fallback. Data mapping changes preserve AOT; executable mapping/protection and alias writes still invalidate it. Protected/unmapped stores preserve the exact fault PC across backends; see guest-memory.md.

The original fixture has ten ARM and three Thumb instructions. A loop computes 10, stores/reloads it through guest memory, branches to Thumb, computes 8 and exits on SVC127. Interpreter and generated execution take 40 guest instructions, and compare registers, flags, memory and instruction counts. Both paths are linked into the RPX; it logs the result on actual console launch.

The separate 85-instruction NCCH guest exercises a checked executable loader, SVC handling, long ARM framebuffer loops, input and orderly process exit. It matches interpreter/AOT registers, flags and full data/BSS memory after two frames. See guest-fixture.md; this adds single-guest sleep/event scheduling, not full kernel/service emulation. v0.13 separately adds bounded single-core multi-thread scheduling and conventional compiled libctru evidence.

Desktop timing is reported separately from console results. Its combined tiny self-test time includes setup/interpreter/generated execution and cannot predict game performance. Actual PowerPC correctness/timings, VFP/reference differential testing, interrupt/wait scheduling, complete exclusive behavior and broader arithmetic/memory/branch/VFP/dispatch/fallback benchmarks are outstanding.

translator.upstream adds validated-container, pinned-source external analysis using the audited lifter. It does not use its native-endian desktop ABI as a PowerPC backend and does not distribute that tool or its incomplete notices.

Maintained source: docs/cpu-subset.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

ARM/Thumb stack and register transfers (0.14.6)¶

The shared CPU runtime now executes ordinary function stack frames and transfers of multiple registers. This is ROM-independent work toward M2/M3/P3; it does not enable a commercial profile or the GUI Inject-to-WUP pipeline. Guest threading is implemented separately in guest-threads.md.

Supported instructions:

  • User-mode ARM LDM/STM in IA, IB, DA and DB addressing modes, with checked optional base writeback and ascending register/address order.
  • ARM LDM of PC, including stack returns, with ARM/Thumb interworking.
  • Thumb16 PUSH/POP of low registers and optional LR/PC; POP PC interworks.
  • Thumb16 LDMIA/STMIA. LDM with its base in the list suppresses writeback; STM with its base in the list is supported only when the base is first.
  • Register BLX calls in the bounded original translator, matching existing interpreter support. The target is captured before LR changes, including BLX LR, and Thumb callers receive an odd return address.

The interpreter decodes these forms into a checked word-transfer helper. Original AOT emits constant decoded parameters and never calls the interpreter. Condition skips preserve registers/memory while consuming one instruction. Transfers preserve flags except the execution-state bit on a loaded-PC return. Address arithmetic wraps at 32 bits; every word still enforces mapping, alignment and read/write permissions. No guest address becomes a host pointer.

An access fault keeps the faulting instruction PC and the original base; earlier successful register loads or memory stores remain visible. These CPU operations are not atomic service publications. The external emitter writes an ARM LDM base early; the checked ABI captures and restores that base on a memory fault. It clears that abort bookkeeping at subsequent instruction/execution boundaries. CPU logs contain the failed address/opcode; guest fault detail retains the PC.

v0.13 supports ARM STM's PC source as PC+8, checked independently against ARM1176. Empty lists, PC as base, Thumb stores of PC, user register banks/SPSR returns and ARM loads with writeback and base-in-list, or stores with a lower listed register, are rejected before effects. v0.14.6 permits the defined ARM STM writeback form when its base is the lowest listed register: it stores the original base then writes back, including GCC __aeabi_ldivmod stack entry. Thumb STM with a non-first base and BLX PC are rejected. The external instruction guard rejects the same empty/unpredictable transfer forms, including empty Thumb operations that the emitter otherwise turns into successful no-ops. This is an explicit safe subset, not a claim to implement every ARM11 abort/unpredictable behavior. Thumb long-immediate calls and broader ARM/VFP/privileged behavior remain open.

The original checked NCCH (host/stack_fixture.py) calls an ARM function, then a Thumb function that calls ARM again. It saves/restores register frames, stores and reloads register groups, skips one conditional transfer from an unmapped base, returns to ARM, checks a restored stack and exits. Its 47 instructions and one SVC produce independent golden data, with zero fallback and matching CPU/ mapped bytes across interpreter, original AOT and the real external lifter at uneven budgets. Separate external cases compare six partial-word fault paths and reject six empty/unpredictable Thumb forms without effects.

make -C tests/multiple check sanitize checks golden modes, interworking, partial word faults, permission/alignment failures, unsupported forms, wrapped addresses and the interpreter/original-AOT nested call self-test. python3 tools/validate-upstream.py --multiple-api --output artifacts/my-stack runs eleven external probes plus this API (24 checks).

tools/reference-multiple.py --output artifacts/my-reference independently compares 944 instruction cases through interpreter and original AOT with pinned Unicorn 2.1.4 configured as ARM1176. It checks all registers, CPSR and all 4096 data bytes, then verifies the nested program's 47-instruction trace, golden output and restored stack. Unicorn and a native GCC/shared-library toolchain are optional developer requirements; production has no Unicorn dependency. In the managed environment, set PYTHONPATH=/workspace/ctr2wup-tools/reference-python for this optional tool. The reference report is artifacts/reports/console-results-v0146/arm1176-data-validated/report.json. The reference uses ARM1176 SCTLR U=1/A=0. Checked bytewise unaligned halfword/word single-data accesses are supported across fully validated ranges; failed ranges produce no partial stores. Instruction fetch, multiple-register, exclusive and FP accesses retain strict alignment. The checked external ABI selects this policy per instruction; raw span shortcuts remain disabled. Preindexed ARM register-offset halfword forms used by unmodified romfsUnmount are supported. This is a fixed userspace policy, not a privileged SCTLR implementation.

All Wii U builds embed ARM/THUMB STACK CALLS and stack_selftest. Compilation, reference checks and emulated PPC prove only the stated checks. Workload speed, full physical PowerPC acceptance, commercial startup and the user's selected Mario revision remain unverified. Scoped normal compiled homebrew has separate desktop/sanitizer/emulated-PPC evidence.

Maintained source: docs/cpu-stack.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Checked external AOT integration¶

Version 0.3.0 uses the pinned external lifter's C generation, then statically links the translated executable with an original CTR2WUP ABI/runtime on desktop and Wii U. It no longer stops at analysis. The commercial conversion command remains gated; this integration does not enable arbitrary game execution.

Reproduction¶

bash tools/install-translator.sh
python3 -m host make-fixture --output artifacts/original-input
python3 -m host translate artifacts/original-input/ctr2wup-guest.cxi --output artifacts/lifted-code
python3 -m host run-fixture artifacts/original-input/ctr2wup-guest.cxi --backend upstream --output artifacts/lifted-frames
python3 -m host build artifacts/original-input/ctr2wup-guest.cxi --target guest-fixture --backend upstream --output artifacts/lifted-console
make upstream-check
python3 tools/validate-upstream.py --sanitize --output artifacts/my-upstream-sanitizers

On Windows use tools/install-translator-windows.ps1 after installing official Rust/rustup and Git; native GCC is required for execution. The generation receipt binds the clean audited source pin, current original driver and binary hashes. Source/header/driver changes require rebuilding or regeneration. Configure CTR2WUP_GENERATOR only with a corresponding receipt; this is local build provenance checking, not cryptographic authentication of an untrusted tool. For a source archive extracted elsewhere, CTR2WUP_LIFTER_SOURCE selects the clean pinned checkout and CTR2WUP_GENERATOR selects the existing external driver. For external analysis from that location, CTR2WUP_LIFTER selects the audited 3dsrecomp executable.

The driver calls upstream public discovery/generation APIs and exposes every discovered instruction as a resume point. It discards the emitted upstream recomp.h. The Python adapter removes register-local/page-table/budget optimizations from the pinned emitter's C and inserts an original instruction boundary. Unexpected grammar fails the build. The replacement header is an original implementation of interface facts, under this project's MIT license. The lifter executable/source are external and are not bundled. Its missing complete permission/copyright notice still blocks bundling its implementation.

Runtime contract¶

v0.13 additionally preserves CPU/TLS/raw VFP/FPSCR contexts across real guest threads, implements checked user ARMv6 CP15 DMB/DSB, byte media/SMLAxy and scalar FP word transport, and allows integer work with nondefault FPSCR controls. Unsupported FP arithmetic/control modes still fail before effects. Normal devkitARM CRT/libctru/newlib applications exercise actual thread/C TLS/LightLock, standard HID and shared-GSP CPU framebuffers; see compiled-homebrew.md. The original three-thread, FP-context and media probes bring the external suite to 14 probes. The independent ARM1176 reference has 870 cases and a nested-call trace. Emulated PPC Linux is not a Wii U or performance acceptance result.

v0.6.0 refreshes user read-only TLS from the CPU context on every execution entry. Its original 89-instruction SRV/CFG startup probe matches interpreter/original/ external AOT state and memory (297 boundary assertions). It also rejects unsupported CP15 cache operations and ARMv7 DMB/DSB/ISB, including emitter no-ops; 35 dedicated assertions cover executed faults and condition skips. --service-api adds the 1,001-assertion malformed IPC/handle/resource suite to a compiler/target run. All four integration probes pass on GCC/sanitizers, Clang and emulated big-endian PPC Linux. See shared-services.md for exact interfaces and limits; this does not provide a complete guest kernel or commercial profile.

v0.7.0 adds the original 151-instruction heap lifecycle probe. All three backends match CPU/mapping/backing state at 1/7/23 instruction budgets, with zero fallback and unchanged executable generations. Guest stores fault identically on protected/freed pages. The 1,778 boundary assertions and 307 heap API assertions pass GCC/sanitizers, Clang and emulated PPC Linux. --heap-api adds the latter suite; combine it with --service-api for a 14-check run of all five probes and both API suites. See guest-memory.md for exact supported memory semantics.

  • Registers and guest addresses are uint32_t; page pointers remain unused. Every memory access takes the checked little-endian callback path; span bypasses are disabled. No 4 GiB host address reservation is required.
  • The attach step checks ABI 4, generation 3, entry ordering and FNV code-piece hashes against executable mappings. CRO/module attachment is rejected.
  • State is visible at every instruction. Exact budgets resume mid-function; SVCs unwind to the existing guest scheduler, which owns sleep/events/exit.
  • Callback failures stop immediately through a bounded host setjmp/longjmp boundary. Fault diagnostics retain the instruction PC and failing address; no following instruction or store executes. Multi-transfer instructions may retain earlier transfers if a later access faults. The external instruction counter includes an attempted faulting instruction; successful execution matches the interpreter's count.
  • Executable mapping/protection changes and code writes, including writable aliases, invalidate the snapshot. Ordinary data mappings preserve it. Guest writes clear the single-guest exclusive monitor. Multi-core/multi-thread exclusive semantics are not implemented.
  • Missing blocks and emitted interpreter requests fail visibly by default. Opt-in C attachment fallback executes only the existing bounded interpreter and records every fallback instruction. It cannot supply general ARM/VFP HLE.
  • Scalar VFP words/double assembly are endian-safe. Only basic default-control arithmetic/moves/compare are currently exercised. Non-default FPSCR controls and vectors fail. Complete NaN/denormal/exception/rounding/conversion behavior is unverified; absent helpers cause compilation failure rather than success stubs. Builds disable contraction/fast math. This is not full VFP support.
  • Native guest call depth is limited to 64; deeper calls unwind to dispatch.

Verified evidence¶

The original 85-instruction framebuffer guest produces two touched/button frames, 1,382,534 executed instructions, four SVCs and an orderly exit with zero fallback. Full registers/flags and all loaded memory regions agree with the interpreter after uneven budgets; framebuffer files match byte-for-byte. The separate original 30-instruction ARM/Thumb probe verifies scalar single and double arithmetic, compare/flags, stack transfer, state switch and one-instruction resume. Its register/VFP results also match Unicorn 2.1.4.

The external integration suite passes 566 framebuffer/boundary assertions and 34 ARM/Thumb/VFP assertions with GCC, ASan/UBSan, Clang and statically linked 32-bit big-endian PowerPC Linux GCC under QEMU's 750 model. This is emulated Linux evidence, not Espresso performance or Wii U execution. Real devkitPPC/WUT RPX/WUHB linking also succeeds; physical input/rendering/exit tests are pending.

For this guest the generated C is about 24 KiB, with one function and 85 resume entries. There is no 4 GiB reservation or million-entry page table. The checked desktop run uses roughly 0.02 seconds of CPU for two frames on this machine; this is a regression measurement, not a game or console speed claim. Code-size, memory, compiler peak RSS and fallback timings for the selected commercial revision remain awaiting its private analysis and hardware measurements.

Versioned reports are under artifacts/reports/upstream-* and the main validation report. Emulated PowerPC/Clang checks can be repeated from the cloud host with:

python3 tools/validate-upstream.py --cc powerpc-linux-gnu-gcc --cc-prefix 'bash /workspace/ctr2wup-tools/environment/dev-container.sh' --run-prefix 'bash /workspace/ctr2wup-tools/environment/dev-container.sh qemu-ppc -cpu 750' --static --output artifacts/my-ppc-check

Source tests/recomp/abi.s generated abi.arm.bin with Clang 19's --target=arm-none-eabi -mcpu=arm1176jzf-s -mfpu=vfp -mfloat-abi=softfp and llvm-objcopy -O binary --only-section=.text; the object has no relocations.

v0.8.0 adds the 148-instruction original shared/sync probe (639 assertions) and --kernel-api (197 assertions). All six probes pass GCC/sanitizers, Clang and emulated PPC; the compiler/target runs include the kernel API (14 checks). See kernel-objects.md for exact lifetime rules; v0.13 adds the separate single-core guest-thread scheduler described in guest-threads.md.

v0.9.0 adds the standard-HID consumer (185 instructions, 18 SVCs, 858 boundary assertions) and --hid-api (336 assertions). The validator runs seven probes; compiler/target runs with HID API contain 16 checks. See hid-input.md for the shared-ring contract and live original-AOT Wii U input consumer.

v0.10.0 adds an original standard-HID/GSP RGB565 graphics probe: 1382865 instructions, 27 SVCs, zero fallback; 3603 assertions including four reference PPMs. --gsp-api adds 362 format/rights/bounds assertions. All eight probes plus that API give 18 compiler/target checks. The original ARM halfword subset is exercised in the graphics program. See gsp-display.md for exact limits.

v0.11.0 adds the ninth external probe, relay: 1,383,167 instructions and 34 SVCs per path, zero fallback, full-state/pixel parity including a VBlank between LDREX/STREX. It exports four relay/frame-{1,2}-{top,bottom}.ppm images. --relay-api adds timing, ring, lifetime, rejection and resource rollback checks. Nine probes plus this API produce 20 compiler/target checks. See gsp-queues.md for the single-client diagnostic timing and remaining stock startup/GX/PICA gaps.

v0.12 adds stack and stack_reject, bringing external probes to eleven. The original nested ARM/Thumb program takes 47 instructions/one SVC; 352 assertions compare normal execution and six partial-word fault paths across external/original AOT and interpreter. The 48-assertion rejection probe checks six empty/unpredictable Thumb forms, including ones the emitter erases. --multiple-api adds the stack/transfer API suite (24 compiler/target checks total). An ARM LDM abort restores an early-emitter writeback; prior successful loads remain visible. See cpu-stack.md for constraints and independent evidence.

Raw VFP memory under CRT controls¶

Version 0.23 keeps VPUSH/VPOP and the exercised VLDM/VSTM forms as raw bit transport under the CRT FPSCR flush/default-NaN controls. Register ranges and word counts are checked before effects; NaN/denormal payload bits and FPSCR are preserved. Unsupported arithmetic modes still reject before execution. This scoped fix enables the original compiled draw program without claiming general VFP control-mode support. The progress suite retains its separate dispatch checks.

Maintained source: docs/upstream-integration.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Shared guest memory management¶

v0.13 places BSS directly after initialized DATA, including its last page's unused tail, and copies no ExeFS padding into BSS. A separate read-only synthetic kernel configuration page at 1ff80000 supplies documented HLE policy; it counts toward backing limits. Thread stacks pin owned allocations until their guest context exits, and eight 512-byte TLS slots share the original TLS page. See guest-threads.md; no retail firmware image or cross-process mapping is supplied.

v0.14.6 adds a separate read-only shared time page at 1ff81000. Its double-bank milliseconds/tick-frequency/tick-offset reference advances with the guest clock from an explicitly synthetic Unix-zero epoch. Ordinary unmodified libctru time queries can run; actual RTC/timezone/full firmware config is not supplied. This page adds 4 KiB to loader backing/quota accounting; the minimal thread fixture now owns 36 KiB rather than 32 KiB. Both host and portable loaders reject segment collisions and include the allocation in their bounds.

Version 0.7.0 advances M3/P3 with ControlMemory (SVC 1) and QueryMemory (SVC 2). It does not enable a commercial profile. This is original C11 code with checked little-endian accesses and no guest-to-host pointer casts. Public interface values/register conventions were checked against libctru 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425 (svc.h/svc.s); the already-audited Azahar source supplied semantic comparisons. No upstream implementation was copied.

Supported contract¶

ControlMemory receives operation in r0, address in r1, source address in r2, page-aligned size in r3 and permissions in r4. It returns Result in r0 and the allocated/affected guest address in r1. Errors clear r1 and leave mappings and owned backing unchanged. Other registers remain intact.

Operation Supported behavior
ALLOC 3 Zero-filled owned heap allocation; address 0 selects the first free page range, or use a specified free address
ALLOC_LINEAR 0x10003 Same checked CPU allocation in the Old-family linear window; reports Continuous state
FREE 1 Remove a fully owned mapped range, including a subrange or adjacent owned allocations; permissions must be 0
PROT 6 Enforce permissions on an owned range, splitting mappings at page boundaries
Application region flag 0x100 Accepted with the supported operations

Heap addresses occupy [0x08000000,0x10000000) and Old-family linear addresses [0x14000000,0x1c000000). Allocation skips existing image/stack mappings. Addresses and sizes must be page aligned; sizes must be nonzero. Source address must be zero. Permissions 0/1/2/3 mean inaccessible/read/write/read-write; execute and other flags return an error. Reads/writes enforce permissions in every backend. FREE/PROT cannot mutate loader-owned image, stack or TLS mappings, even inside the heap window. Partial operations preflight the full range and fragment capacity before publishing a change.

QueryMemory receives address in r2 and returns r0 Result, r1 base, r2 size, r3 permissions, r4 state and r5 page flags. It reports the containing logical region, merging adjacent regions with equal state and permissions. A region may include immutable loader pages and mutable heap pages: its query result does not authorize FREE/PROT of the entire extent. Gaps report Free state 0/permissions 0. Image text/rodata report Code 4, data/heap Private 5, linear Continuous 7, stack/TLS Locked 11. Page flags are 0; physical-page attributes are unavailable. Queries outside [0,0x40000000) return an address error and clear r1-r5. No host pointer is returned.

Misaligned address/size, invalid combination, invalid address/state and resource exhaustion return nonzero Result codes (e0e01bf1, e0e01bf2, e0e01bee, e0e01bf5, e0a01bf5, d86007f3). Exact precedence and unsupported combinations are a scoped HLE contract, not full retail-kernel conformance. Request/failure counts and last operation/address/result remain in ctr_guest for diagnosis.

Capacity, ownership and translation¶

There are 16 owned allocations and 32 mapping fragments, with a combined 64 MiB retained-backing limit including image/BSS/stack/TLS. Partial free retains the original host allocation until its last mapped byte is removed; all its backing remains charged. Removed pages can be allocated again with new zeroed storage. Final free and guest close release ownership. Fragment exhaustion fails atomically. These limits need revisiting against actual title requirements and Wii U costs.

Non-executable mapping changes advance the memory/exclusive generation without invalidating unchanged executable snapshots. Adding/removing executable mappings, changing execute protection and writes through executable aliases still invalidate AOT. Dynamic heap management therefore preserves normal translated execution.

Linear backing is contiguous for checked CPU accesses only. FCRAM physical addresses, GPU/DSP allocation, cache/coherency support and the GX2 memory bridge are absent. MAP/UNMAP aliases, RESERVE, system/base regions, New-family linear windows and unknown flags stop visibly as unsupported. Image reprotection, multi-thread ownership, physical accounting and broader kernel behavior remain unfinished.

Original execution evidence¶

host/heap_fixture.py generates an original NCCH with 151 ARM instructions. It allocates three heap pages, writes little-endian values, queries the region, protects the middle page read-only, reads it, restores permissions and frees it. It queries/reallocates that hole, verifies zeroed memory, allocates/queries linear memory and frees all backing before exit: 14 memory requests plus ExitProcess. Independent expected SVC outputs accompany full state/memory comparisons.

make -C tests/heap check sanitize checks the real ARM lifecycle, guard/write-only permissions, static-page ownership, overlap/overflow, holes, retained-backing budget, slot/fragment exhaustion and atomic failures. tests/recomp/test_heap.c compares actual external AOT, original AOT and interpreter CPU/mapping/backing state at 1/7/23 instruction budgets. It also checks guest-store fault PCs on protected/freed pages, zero fallback and preserved executable generations.

All three Wii U builds embed the original interpreter/AOT self-test. Native mode displays GUEST HEAP LIFECYCLE PASS CODE 0; SD logs record heap_selftest PASS result=0, or an exact FAIL code. The external-AOT heap probe is a desktop/emulated-PPC test, separate from the console guest program. Physical behavior/timings, native Windows and conventional 3DS homebrew boot remain pending. STATUS.md records current report paths.

v0.8.0 pins source allocations used by shared-memory objects; FREE/PROT rejects those allocations until final release. Owned shared backing uses the combined budget. See kernel-objects.md.

Maintained source: docs/guest-memory.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Single-core guest threads¶

Version 0.13 adds eight process-local thread slots, including the initial thread. Each holds ARM registers/CPSR, 32 raw VFP words/FPSCR, a separate 512-byte TLS slot, waits and deadlines. One emulated CPU executes strict priorities 0x18..0x3f; equal priorities rotate after 32 instructions. Host budget changes do not restart the quantum. Context switches invalidate the exclusive monitor. This is not SMP, priority inheritance, complete affinity or a full 3DS kernel.

Implemented thread SVCs are CreateThread (08), ExitThread (09), GetThreadPriority (0b), SetThreadPriority (0c) and GetThreadId (37). Core 0/default -2/any -1 use the single emulated CPU; other explicit cores are rejected. Entry permissions, alignment, stack and capacity are checked before allocation. Heap stack backing is pinned until exit. IDs and handle generations prevent stale slot reuse. Closing a handle cannot kill a running thread. Terminated objects survive retained handles and waiters; waits can join them. ExitProcess terminates every thread.

Mutexes track their owning thread and recursive depth. Wrong-owner releases fail; closing a locked handle retains its object, and owner exit releases its locks. Events, mutexes, semaphores and thread joins support existing wait-one/any/all and timeouts across contexts. Process-local address arbiters (21/22) support signal, signed wait/decrement and timed forms; wait references survive handle closure. No cross-process arbitration or kernel priority inheritance is claimed.

The local process resource-limit object (38/39/3a) exposes the runtime's actual bounded memory/thread/object quotas for IDs 0..8. Query lists are fully checked before any output writes. Timers remain unavailable (quota zero); CPU-time quota and other processes are unsupported. A synthetic read-only kernel configuration page at 1ff80000 supplies compatibility version 2.58.0, application budget and single-system-core fields needed by libctru startup. These values are HLE policy, not measurements or a firmware image. Other configuration fields remain zero.

The checked loader places BSS immediately after initialized DATA, including the unused tail of its last page. ExeFS padding never overwrites BSS. Tests cover non-page-aligned DATA, allocation bounds and conflicts with reserved mappings.

CPU additions required by compiled startup are ARM UADD8/USUB8/SEL and GE flags, SMLAxy with signed halfwords/sticky Q, VMRS/VMSR FPSCR transport, checked scalar VLDR/VSTR for S0..31/D0..15, ARM STM's PC+8 source value and user CP15 ARMv6 DMB/DSB ordering. Interpreter and original AOT transport floating-point words; they do not implement general VFP arithmetic. External AOT preserves FP contexts and rejects unsupported arithmetic/control modes before effects. Cache-maintenance operations and unimplemented forms still fail visibly. Barriers order checked CPU backing; they do not complete PICA work.

make -C tests/threads check sanitize tests context ownership, scheduling across host budgets, joins, failure rollback, stack pins, references, address arbitration and quota output preflight. The original three-thread fixture executes 345 instructions, 22 SVCs and eight switches with three-backend state/memory parity. All diagnostic RPX/WUHB variants embed its interpreter/original-AOT self-test. Separate external AOT probes check media instructions/barriers and two threads' VFP arithmetic/FPSCR isolation. compiled-homebrew.md records real libctru tests. Every physical Wii U result remains pending.

Maintained source: docs/guest-threads.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Shared memory and synchronization¶

Version 0.8.0 adds original C11 objects needed by HID/GSP-style startup. Public SVC register interfaces were checked against pinned libctru svc.h/svc.s; no upstream implementation is copied. The original v0.8 fixture is single-thread; v0.13 extends ownership/waits to eight process-local contexts (guest-threads.md). This remains a single-process subset, not full retail-kernel behavior or a commercial profile.

Interface Implemented behavior
CreateMemoryBlock 1e r0 other rights/r1 source/r2 size/r3 own rights; r0 Result/r1 handle. Zero source creates local zeroed backing; nonzero pins one contiguous owned RW heap allocation
MapMemoryBlock 1f r0 handle/r1 destination/r2 own rights/r3 other rights; checked local aliases in [10000000,14000000)
UnmapMemoryBlock 20 r0 handle/r1 exact mapped address; removes mapping/releases reference
CreateMutex 13 / ReleaseMutex 14 Initially locked/unlocked; recursive acquisition by the sole thread; checked release
CreateSemaphore 15 / ReleaseSemaphore 16 Nonnegative initial count, positive signed maximum, bounded release and old count in r1
WaitSynchronization 24 Event/mutex/semaphore acquisition, consumption, timeout/deferred wait
WaitSynchronizationN 25 Up to 16 checked handles; wait-any index/atomic wait-all. r0 timeout low/r1 array/r2 count/r3 all/r4 timeout high

Typed objects share the existing 64-slot generation-tagged handle table. Pools allow 8 shared objects, 16 mutexes, 16 semaphores and the existing events/sessions. Shared objects allow four mappings each within the 32-fragment memory limit. Owned shared backing shares the combined 64 MiB budget; pinned heap is charged once. Guest close frees owned shared backing and heap storage.

Shared aliases enforce rights: own permissions must be a subset of object rights; other permissions must match policy or DONTCARE 10000000. Only none/read/write/ read-write are supported. Failed mapping changes neither published memory nor references. Mapped objects survive closing their last handle until unmapping or process close. A source allocation cannot be freed/reprotected while an object pins it; final release removes the pin. This conservative rule pins the whole original allocation. Aliases report QueryMemory Shared state 6. Foreign processes, rights transfer and GPU/DSP physical backing remain absent.

Waits retain objects independently of handle closure. Wait-any consumes the first ready object in caller order. Wait-all preflights every object before acquiring any and returns index ffffffff; timeout returns the same index. Duplicate objects in wait-all are rejected explicitly; wait-any duplicates are accepted. Invalid handles/arrays/counts fail without consuming objects. Polling/external event signals resume the sole waiter deterministically. Negative timeout values are infinite; finite deadline overflow is rejected. Mutex ownership belongs to the sole guest thread, so acquisition while owned is recursive. Priority inheritance, other-thread ownership, pulse events, address arbitration and multi-thread scheduling remain unfinished.

host/kernel_fixture.py generates 148 original ARM instructions using 27 SVCs. It allocates/pins heap memory, maps/reads a read-only shared alias, duplicates/ closes handles, acquires/releases a mutex, consumes/releases a semaphore, checks timeout and wait-any indexes, then releases all backing/objects and exits. Expected outputs and interpreter/original/external AOT state/memory are checked. make -C tests/kernel check sanitize covers lifetimes, pins, permissions, wrong/ stale handles, wake/timeout/close while waiting, atomic wait-all and exhaustion/ rollback. External validation adds the real lifter probe; --kernel-api adds the API suite to a compiler/target run.

All three Wii U builds embed the original interpreter/AOT self-test: native mode SHARED/SYNC LIFECYCLE PASS CODE 0, SD log kernel_selftest PASS result=0, or a specific FAIL code. Console/native Windows evidence is still required. See STATUS.md for current reports.

Maintained source: docs/kernel-objects.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Shared TLS and SRV/CFG startup support (0.6.0)¶

This is a reusable runtime foundation for the planned GUI injector. It needs no commercial ROM. An original ARM program exercises standard TLS/SVC/IPC interfaces and reads configuration through the same C runtime used in the Wii U builds. It does not establish conventional homebrew boot or commercial compatibility.

Supported interfaces¶

ARM MRC p15,0,Rd,c13,c0,3 reads the current CPU's user read-only TLS address. The interpreter, original translator and external ABI agree, including condition skips and instruction budgets. Rd=PC, writes, other CP15 registers and cache operations are unsupported. The external adapter refreshes TLS at every execution entry; an attach-time value cannot become stale after the thread context changes. This does not implement thread creation/scheduling or hardware cache maintenance.

Interface Exact request Behavior
SVC ConnectToPort 2d r1: checked NUL-terminated srv: name r0: result; r1: generation-tagged SRV session handle
SVC SendSyncRequest 32 r0: session handle; command at TLS+80 Synchronous validated request/reply; SVC result is separate from service result
SRV RegisterClient Header 00010002, current-PID descriptor 20, one placeholder word Records registration for this session; reply 00010040/result0
SRV GetServiceHandle Header 00050100, name[8], length, flags0/1 cfg:u, v0.9 hid:USER, v0.10 gsp::Gpu; reply 00050042, result, move descriptor 10, handle
CFG SecureInfoGetRegion Header 00020000 Reply 00020080, result, configured region
CFG GetSystemModel Header 00050000 Reply 00050080, result, configured Old-family model
CFG GetModelNintendo2DS Header 00060000 Reply 00060080, result, 0 for Old2DS and 1 otherwise
CFG GetConfigInfoBlk2 Header 00010082, size1, language block 000a0002, mapped descriptor 1c/1e, guest output pointer Writes one language byte; returns 00010042, result, descriptor/pointer

ctr_guest_configure accepts region0..6, language0..11 and Old-family models 0 (Old3DS), 1 (Old3DS XL), 3 (Old2DS) before execution/service access. Diagnostic defaults are USA1, English1, Old3DS0. These are explicit emulated settings; they are not read from the physical Wii U or inferred from an uninspected ROM. Future injection profiles must supply their chosen settings. New-family models 2, 4 and 5 remain outside this execution profile. The libctru enum corrects swapped 2DS/New3DS IDs in a Zakuro comment.

Unavailable service names return a permanent SRV NotFound result d88067fa, without allocating a session. This bounded directory does not wait for services to register asynchronously, even for flags0. It permits library fallback from absent cfg:i/cfg:s to cfg:u; those privileged interfaces are not advertised. Unknown commands, unsupported config blocks and malformed request layouts fault with the endpoint/header recorded. There are no generic-success service stubs. Notifications, subscriptions, user/server IPC, arbitrary descriptors, process identity/permission isolation and cross-process mappings are unimplemented.

Bounds and ownership¶

The transport checks little-endian words, reserved header bits and total word counts within the 64-word (100-byte) command buffer. It preflights read/write permissions for the whole buffer before registration, allocation or replies. Mapped language output is checked before any write; adjacent bytes remain intact. Read/write descriptors require both permissions. Output cannot overlap or alias the command buffer's backing storage, even through a different guest mapping. Unknown handles and event handles cannot be used as sessions. Session handles share the existing 64-slot generation table, with 16 reference-counted session objects alongside the separate event pool. Duplicate/close/stale handles and allocation rollback are tested. Closing SRV does not destroy acquired CFG sessions.

Failures retain IPC endpoint, requested directory target, raw header, result and request/failure counts. Those diagnostics are available in the runtime and SD log. They do not imply that a service in a ROM's allowlist was actually executed. The runtime still owns one CPU/process; this is not a complete 3DS kernel.

Reproduction and original program¶

python3 tools/generate-service-fixture.py
make -C tests/services check sanitize
python3 tools/validate-upstream.py --output artifacts/my-service-integration

The tracked probe is generated from host/service_fixture.py, not a game dump. It reads TLS, connects/registers SRV, opens CFG, reads region/model/language, closes both handles and exits: 89 executed ARM instructions, five IPC requests and nine SVCs. A private synthetic NCCH built from those bytes also passes the normal checked loader and real pinned external lifter. The CLI commercial profile gate and framebuffer fixture profile remain unchanged.

The C service suite covers wire replies, configured values, duplicate/stale/wrong handles, malformed headers/descriptors, permissions, missing services and both session/handle exhaustion. Its interpreter/original-AOT probe matches complete guest state and memory at uneven quotas. External integration compares the same program through all three paths, including a TLS address changed after attach. A separate CP15 probe prevents cache operations from disappearing as emitter NOPs. It also checks unsupported DMB/DSB/ISB barriers instead of silently accepting them. --service-api adds the full malformed-input suite to an external validation run.

All three Wii U diagnostic builds embed the original interpreter/AOT startup self-test. Native mode displays TLS/SRV/CFG STARTUP PASS CODE 0 after success; the SD log records service_selftest. Physical console results remain required. Emulated big-endian PowerPC Linux results do not establish Wii U execution/speed.

Final v0.6.0 reports: validation-v1-20261008T135953Z/report.json (135 Python tests; 27 full checks; 1,001 service API assertions normally and with sanitizers), services-clang-final-v6/report.json, services-ppc-final-v6/report.json (10 checks each, including 566/34/297/35 integration assertions and the service API). All report paths are under artifacts/reports/.

Interface sources¶

No upstream implementation was vendored. Interface facts were checked against:

  • devkitPro/libctru 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425: source/svc.s, source/srv.c, source/services/cfgu.c, include/3ds/ipc.h, include/3ds/services/cfgu.h and thread TLS helpers.
  • The audited Zakuro 76f17bf76cd9ea238a94f37d389fe701ab05a673 kernel IPC/SVC and SRV/CFG interfaces, and the pinned lifter's CP15 emitter.
  • Audited Azahar's documented result-code fields and service-result descriptions; no GPL implementation is incorporated.

Hardware equivalence and broader CFG/Mii/save identity behavior await the selected Mario revision and appropriate reference/console tests. FS/APT/GSP/PICA, multi-thread kernel behavior, audio, saves and CROs remain implementation work. HID pad/touch support is now defined in hid-input.md; motion remains absent. The restricted direct GSP display interface is defined in gsp-display.md; shared framebuffer/relay support is defined in gsp-queues.md. Stock graphics startup and the scoped synchronous GX commands are implemented; see gx-transfers.md. PICA rendering remains absent.

v0.11.0 extends GSP with one local retained relay event/shared page, checked framebuffer queues and diagnostic VBlank/IRQ delivery; see gsp-queues.md. This does not supply stock libctru service threads, address arbitration, barriers or GPU register/command initialization. Those remain explicit unsupported paths.

Maintained source: docs/shared-services.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Standard HID input subset (0.9.0)¶

The reusable runtime advertises hid:USER through SRV. GetHandles request 000a0000 replies with header 000a0047, result0, copy descriptor 14000000 and six typed handles: one shared page and Pad0/Pad1/accelerometer/gyroscope/debug events. The latter three events have no producer. Motion enable/disable and all other HID commands fail visibly. hid:SPVR, IRRST and New-family input are absent.

The device owns six internal handles for its lifetime; requests return independent copies. Initialization and copy exhaustion roll back allocations. The shared page has read-only guest rights; its mapping, copies and waiters obey the checked kernel lifetime rules. Closing a returned handle cannot destroy the device's backing.

ctr_hid_update publishes Old-family physical button bits 0..11, signed Circle Pad axes in -156..156, and valid touch coordinates in 320x240. Circle Pad direction bits 28..31 use a radius40 threshold and diagonal sectors. Touch bit20 is derived by the client from the touch entry, as in the public interface. ZL/ZR, C-stick, accelerometer, gyro, raw analog calibration and slider are unsupported. Invalid buttons, axes, touched coordinates and nonincreasing timestamps cause no update.

The eight-entry little-endian pad ring has index at 10, current buttons at 1c and 16-byte entries from 28: held, added, removed, packed signed XY. The touch index is at b8; eight-byte entries from c8 contain packed unsigned XY and validity. Both indexes advance together. On index0 wrap, current/previous reset timestamps at 0/8 and a8/b0 use the 268111856 Hz guest tick scale. Pad0 and Pad1 one-shot events signal after publication. The single guest runs on the same host thread, so publication has no concurrent partial reader. Multi-thread device timing and physical 3DS equivalence require further evidence.

The original ARM consumer in host/hid_fixture.py reads TLS, registers SRV, acquires HID handles, maps the page read-only, waits for Pad0, reads the standard rings and writes a diagnostic snapshot. Two controlled updates followed by unmap/handle cleanup/exit execute 185 instructions and 18 SVCs. Interpreter, original AOT and actual external AOT match registers, mapped memory, shared bytes, object references and event state at uneven instruction budgets, with zero fallback. The external comparison has 858 assertions; 336 API assertions check wire replies, read-only rights, transitions, wrap timestamps, validation and resource rollback. GCC/sanitizer, Clang and emulated big-endian PPC results do not prove console input.

All three Wii U builds embed hid_selftest and a separate live original-AOT ARM consumer. VPAD buttons map to physical 3DS bits, the left stick scales to ±156, and calibrated touch passes through the checked bottom-screen viewport. In native mode, ARM HID, ARM CIRCLE, ARM TOUCH and sample counts show values read by ARM through shared memory, rather than direct host input. SD logs contain HID live state=... and hid_live_shutdown. Diagnostic Y/Plus/Minus remain host controls; Minus exits before publishing its press. The right stick is displayed by native diagnostics but is not exposed as a New-family C-stick.

Reproduce with make -C tests/hid check sanitize, tools/generate-hid-fixture.py and tools/validate-upstream.py --hid-api --output artifacts/my-hid-checks. Interface facts were checked against devkitPro/libctru commit 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425, services/hid.h, services/hid.c and IPC descriptor definitions. The implementation and fixture are original. This does not claim conventional graphical homebrew boot or Mario compatibility.

Maintained source: docs/hid-input.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Standard direct GSP framebuffer display (0.10.0)¶

The shared runtime advertises gsp::Gpu. This original implementation supports single-process direct framebuffer submission through checked TLS IPC:

Request Checked behavior
AcquireRight 00160042 flags0, one copy descriptor0 and current-process pseudo-handle ffff8001; acquires the local process's display right
ReleaseRight 00170000 Releases the acquired right
SetLcdForceBlack 000b0040 flags0/1; actually controls decoded output
SetBufferSwap 00050200 screen0/1 and seven framebuffer words; validates source range before changing state

Replies have one normal result word and the requested command ID, e.g. 00050040/result0 for SetBufferSwap. Duplicate session handles retain ownership; closing the last GSP session drops the right. Descriptors remain for display after release, until guest close or a later submission. Unsupported commands and malformed/unsupported modes fault visibly, with endpoint/header recorded. Invalid source memory records a precise memory fault before any display-state change. These are scoped HLE policies, not retail error-code/timing conformance.

Framebuffers are fixed 2D top 400x240/bottom 320x240. Active index and display-select must be equal 0/1, the unknown word 0, and the secondary address 0 or equal to primary. Supported mode bits are formats 0..4, top 2D bit 6 and local-memory bit 8. Top requires bit 6; bottom must clear it. Stereo, wide800, VRAM bit 9 and other mode bits fail. Column stride must contain all 240 pixels and be at most 4096 bytes; padding is permitted. The reader uses base + x*stride + (239-y)*bytes_per_pixel and checks the entire source extent, including adjacent mappings, before writing host output. It revalidates on every presentation, so freed/reprotected backing fails safely. No guest address becomes a host pointer and no native-endian pixel cast is used.

The five formats are ABGR-byte RGBA8, BGR8, little-endian RGB565, RGB5A1 and RGBA4. Channels expand by bit replication; alpha is preserved. Host output is numeric RRGGBBAA, which the existing GX2 copy backend writes as explicit RGBA bytes. Force-black output is opaque black. Tests use independent golden channels, four distinct corners, padded strides, split mappings, overflow/permission faults and unchanged output on failed preflight. CPU cache/GX2 surface synchronization remains in the native presentation backend; this is not PICA cache emulation.

The original ARM program in host/gsp_fixture.py acquires SRV/HID/GSP, waits for standard HID input, writes RGB565 CPU framebuffers and submits both screens. A makes the top green, release makes it blue. Valid touch makes the bottom magenta with one white pixel; release makes it cyan. The top's corners are white, red, green and blue (top-left, top-right, bottom-left, bottom-right). Two frames and cleanup execute 1382865 instructions and 27 SVCs, with zero fallback and matching full state/memory through interpreter/original/external AOT. The fixture also checks unsigned ARM LDRH/STRH with immediate preindexing and no writeback; other ARM halfword forms remain unsupported in the bounded original backends.

make -C tests/gsp check sanitize runs 362 API assertions and the original interpreter/AOT display self-test. tools/validate-upstream.py --gsp-api --output artifacts/my-display-checks executes all nine external probes and writes gsp/frame-{1,2}-{top,bottom}.ppm reference images. The external display test has 3603 assertions when writing all four images. Host, sanitizer, Clang and emulated PPC results establish portable behavior, not Wii U execution or usable speed.

All three Wii U builds embed gsp_selftest; native mode displays HID/GSP RGB565 DISPLAY PASS CODE 0. Y cycles native → original custom-I/O ARM fixture → standard HID/GSP fixture. The GSP fixture uses original AOT in all builds; v0.11 replaces the live direct guest with the shared-queue variant described in gsp-queues.md. The existing external-AOT build still uses the actual external lifter for its custom-I/O fixture. External HID/GSP execution is separately tested on desktop/emulated PPC. The GSP status overlay is in the middle of the top image, leaving all four corner markers visible. Plus resets both graphics guests, B requests the current graphics guest's exit and Minus exits the application. Input latency includes bounded 50,000-instruction host slices and complete CPU frame fills. SD logs contain swaps, state, fallback, fault and shutdown outcomes.

Interface facts were checked against libctru 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425, gspgpu.h, gspgpu.c, gfx.c, and the audited Zakuro display/format descriptions. No upstream implementation was copied. Version 0.11 adds interrupt relay registration, shared framebuffer queues and diagnostic VBlank; see gsp-queues.md. GX command queues, DMA/display transfers, VRAM/physical-address translation, PICA registers/draws/shaders/textures/TEV and graphics thread scheduling are absent. Stock libctru graphics initialization needs several of these and is not claimed to boot. This bridge is a reusable display foundation for the injector.

The next planned acceptance gate is physical Wii U input/display/stability and the selected Mario revision's private executable intake. Without that evidence, M3/M4/M5, commercial conversion, installer acceptance and the GUI remain open.

Maintained source: docs/gsp-display.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

GSP shared framebuffer queues and VBlank relay (0.11.0)¶

This shared runtime increment uses standard GSP wire layouts to present the original ARM graphics probe. It supports one local guest/client slot, two screens, CPU framebuffer memory and a deterministic diagnostic clock. It does not enable commercial conversion or stock libctru graphics initialization.

RegisterInterruptRelayQueue (00130042) accepts flags1, copy descriptor0 and a live event handle, after AcquireRight. The successful 00130082 reply contains result0, client ID0, copy descriptor0 and an owned read/write 4096-byte shared-memory handle. Result0 means this HLE display is already configured; the hardware-init result path and GPU register programming are unsupported. Typed resource/stale-handle errors return a service result. Malformed wire layouts and unsupported commands fault visibly. Duplicate registration fails.

The device retains the shared page and event independently of returned handles. Mappings and waits also retain their objects. Closing the last copy of the registering session or UnregisterInterruptRelayQueue (00140000) releases the device references; an existing mapped page stays valid. Another session cannot unregister the owner. Last-GSP-session close releases display rights.

The slot0 interrupt record has index/count/error/reserved bytes at offset0, with 52 interrupt bytes at offset 0x0c. Framebuffer headers are at 0x200 and 0x240: bit0 selects one of two seven-word entries, bit8 marks an update. Queue entry selection is independent of the displayed buffer's active index. The format/range checks and rotated decoders are described in gsp-display.md. Other header bits and unsupported display modes are rejected.

At a due host scheduling entry, both dirty descriptors and readable source extents are checked before either screen changes. Successful publication clears only dirty bits, commits the latest pending descriptors, appends VBlank0/1 IRQs 2/3, and signals the retained event through the normal wait mechanism. Ring wrap preserves pending entries. A full ring sets the error byte and records dropped IRQs; it never overwrites pending interrupts. Invalid headers, source permissions or modes leave the page and display unchanged and fault the guest. Nonzero queued-GX headers at 0x800 fault rather than discard unimplemented work.

The interval is 16,666,667 ns. This is a diagnostic 60 Hz clock, not measured 3DS/Wii U timing. A late host call coalesces missed intervals into one IRQ pair and the latest display update, while recording missed intervals. A repeated call at the same time cannot deliver another pair. Deadline overflow faults. Releasing display rights pauses processing; registration stays owned until unregistered. Device writes invalidate the CPU exclusive monitor and executable aliases.

The original ARM probe (host/relay_fixture.py) maps the page, writes rotated RGB565 screens, uses word LDREX/STREX to publish and drain queues, waits for the relay, reads both interrupt IDs, then unregisters/unmaps/closes/exits. The same instruction stream runs in interpreter, original AOT and the real pinned external recompiler. The comparison deliberately inserts a second VBlank between LDREX and STREX, ensuring a failed store retries without losing interrupts. Two frames plus that interleaving execute 1,383,167 instructions and 34 SVCs per path with zero fallback. The external comparison has 11,541 assertions. All state, mapped/shared bytes and pixel references are compared. Generated original sources reproduce from the checked generator.

make -C tests/relay check sanitize passes 762 API assertions covering timing, atomic two-screen updates, ring wrap/full cases, source failures, resource rollback, session ownership and handle lifetime, alongside the original interpreter/AOT self-test. python3 tools/validate-upstream.py --relay-api --output artifacts/my-relay-check runs nine external probes plus relay API checks and exports four relay/frame-{1,2}-{top,bottom}.ppm reference images. Compiler/target checks include Clang and emulated big-endian PPC Linux; none is physical Wii U evidence.

All Wii U builds embed direct-display and relay self-tests. Y's standard HID/GSP mode now runs the original queued guest. SD logs record relay registration, VBlank, coalesced intervals, dropped IRQs, faults and clean shutdown. See console-testing.md for physical checks and reference images.

Public interface facts were checked against pinned libctru 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425 (gspgpu.h/c, gfx.c). No upstream implementation was copied. Later runtime work supplies the scoped service threads, address arbitration and standalone graphics startup used by the original compiler-built applications. Version 0.17 adds synchronous GX DMA, fills, display transfers and raw texture copies; see gx-transfers.md. Version 0.23 connects compiled guest software draws using tracked owned linear physical ranges and transactional tiled targets. General physical/VRAM mapping, broader PICA/GX2 translation and guest DSP/audio remain open. Private diagnostic saves are described in filesystem.md. Wider shared runtime development can continue without a ROM; exact Mario requirements and physical acceptance still need private intake and console results.

Maintained source: docs/gsp-queues.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Renderer foundation¶

Version 0.23 adds compiled original guest draws through standard GSP, tracked owned resources and canonical tiled targets. The CPU software MOV subset retains shader/state and commits target bytes/completion transactionally. Broader shaders/fragment state, native GX2 translation and physical renderer acceptance remain open. The sections below record earlier foundation boundaries at their named versions.

v0.22.0 executes the first original software scenes through checked PICA-format loader/index state, fixed attributes and uniforms, MOV/NOP/END shaders and a bounded opaque triangle raster path. Both index formats match independently calculated pixels exactly. The focused native Scene Check builds and records timings/automatic results, but physical picture/exit evidence remains pending. Guest GSP draws and general shader/fragment/GX2 translation remain unsupported; full M4 acceptance is open.

v0.21.0 extends the resolved vertex decoder with checked unsigned 8-bit/16-bit little-endian indexed gathers. Duplicates and index order are preserved. Complete referenced ranges, finite float values and full-span aliases are checked before output changes. This supplies array preparation for the future rendering boundary; it enables no PICA loader register, fixed attribute, shader, primitive assembly or draw. Exhaustive index tests use original bytes and explicitly retain physical renderer acceptance as pending.

renderer.pica.decode_commands inspects complete little-endian PICA command records, byte masks, repeated/consecutive register writes, and eight-byte padding. It bounds input, output count, and the 1,024 wire identifiers, and rejects extended/reserved header encodings rather than guessing. Inspection returns records; it does not mark those records supported for execution.

This is original code. The documented basic command layout was checked against the pinned Azahar command-header/processing behavior recorded in the dependency audit; no Azahar implementation is copied or linked.

v0.18 adds the native PICA command front end, explicit raw viewport/scissor configuration and finalized standard queue processing. It is verified against original pinned-libctru command construction and a compiled guest; actual draws, shaders and GX2 submission remain unsupported.

renderer.framebuffer tests little-endian RGB565 decoding, RGB8 PPM export, aspect-preserving viewport calculation, and border touch rejection. The native Wii U backend has its own compiled display/input implementation; Python results do not establish GX2 behavior.

v0.10 implements direct GSP framebuffer submission, rotated portable decoding of all five linear display formats, and an original ARM HID-to-RGB565 display guest in all Wii U builds. See gsp-display.md for scope and desktop reference images. Console behavior remains pending. This does not execute PICA draws.

Pending: asynchronous GPU transfer/coherency, broader vertex and geometry processing/shaders, texture sampling/mips/resource lifetime, lighting, TEV, broader guest draw submission, caching, hardware/reference scene comparisons, and console timings. The Python execute_draw inspection path remains explicitly unsupported; the portable/native guest adapter implements only the documented software subset. Milestone 4 is incomplete.

v0.16.0 adds an original portable base-texture decoder in runtime/core/ctr_texture.c and a Python reference in renderer/texture.py. All 14 PICA format IDs are implemented, including RGBA/RGB packed formats, luminance/alpha, RG8/HILO, ETC1 and ETC1A4. Base dimensions are powers of two in 8..1024; 8×8 Morton tiles decode into explicit row-major RGBA bytes without an implicit display-origin flip. Little-endian source words do not depend on host byte order. Compressed differential blocks and buffer ranges are validated before output changes; invalid ETC2-style modes, truncation and overlap fail. Mip tails, texture filtering/wrapping, caches, GPU allocation and draw submission remain unfinished. No PICA renderer or commercial compatibility is enabled.

Golden layouts/selectors/alpha/clamping and failure atomicity have C normal/ sanitizer and Python checks. tools/validate-textures.py compares 42 original images with the independent Python decoder and 39 with the clean audited Zakuro texture module at 76f17bf76cd9ea238a94f37d389fe701ab05a673. Format 6 follows Azahar's RG8 convention [byte1, byte0, 0, 255]; Zakuro's [0, byte1, byte0, 255] differs and is explicitly excluded from that comparison. External emulator code is test-only in separate checkouts, never copied into or linked with the runtime/delivery. Actual PICA sampling/render scenes and GX2 resource/coherency/performance checks still need Wii U/reference evidence.

v0.17.0 implements a bounded standard GSP GX ring and CPU-backed DMA/fill/display conversion/raw texture copy/cache checks. Equal-sized unscaled transfers support all five display formats, layout/swizzling and flip. Stock libctru produces exact three-frame outputs with zero fallback.
