PICA lists/draws remain unsupported; GPU resource/sampling/TEV/shader/coherency work and actual console scene/timing proof are still required. See gx-transfers.md.

Maintained source: docs/renderer-status.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

PICA command front end¶

The original v0.18 front end processes a small no-draw configuration command list through the standard GSP queue. It stores raw viewport/scissor register values, applies byte masks and recognizes the finalization emitted by libctru. This is a step toward M4. It produces no pixels, runs no shader and submits no GX2 draw.

Version 0.23 also adapts bounded compiled guest draws through the standard queue. The configuration-only API below remains unchanged; the separate scene/guest adapter handles its explicitly defined shader/target subset.

Wire inspection¶

renderer.pica.decode_commands and runtime/core/ctr_pica.c inspect explicit little-endian bytes. A record contains its first parameter, header, extra parameters and padding to an eight-byte boundary. The header contains:

Bits Meaning
0–15 Identifier; this implementation accepts wire IDs 0x000–0x3ff
16–19 Four byte-enable bits
20–27 Extra parameter count; one record has 1–256 parameters
28–30 Unused; unsupported encodings are rejected
31 Increment register for each parameter, otherwise repeat it

Inspection supports the wire range without claiming those registers can execute. Padding is skipped and never becomes a write. Nonzero padding tolerance is our inspection contract; the audited libctru producer writes zero padding. It is not physical hardware evidence. Native/host budgets are at most 4 MiB and 65,536 writes; callers may choose smaller budgets. Range overflow, truncated records, unused header bits and exceeded budgets fail visibly.

Facts were checked against GBATEK's command-list description and clean libctru commit 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425, gpu.h, gpu.c and registers.h. Its builder splits API arrays into records of at most 256 parameters. Its implementation is compiled only into a separate reference test binary; no emulator or external GPU implementation is embedded in the native front end.

Supported execution¶

ctr_pica_execute starts with a software snapshot initialized to zero; this does not model hardware reset values. Its supported ordinary registers are:

IDs Raw configuration
0x41–0x44 Viewport width/inverse width/height/inverse height
0x65–0x67 Scissor mode/position/dimensions
0x68 Viewport origin

The stored words retain byte-mask semantics. Numeric float decoding, clipping and raster behavior are not implemented. A submitted list must have a length divisible by sixteen bytes and end with one or two single-parameter, full-mask FINALIZE (0x10) writes of 0x12345678, matching GPUCMD_Split alignment. Finalization is handled as a completion marker and never stored as an ordinary register. Configuration after a marker, missing/invalid markers, streamed marker parameters and more than two markers fail.

All other registers fail, including with a zero byte mask. Draw triggers, shader/uniform/table uploads, command jumps, caches, textures and render-target state are not treated as generic successful register writes.

The whole list and counters are checked before committing any register change. Input/state/output/report aliases fail before mutation. The commit pass cannot change unread input. This avoids a 4 KiB automatic state copy; the current devkitPPC compiler reports a 176-byte ctr_pica_execute stack frame. That is a compiler observation, not a measurement of the full Wii U call stack.

Standard guest queue¶

GX command 1 stages the checked guest command-buffer bytes before processing. Its address must be eight-byte aligned; length must be nonzero, a multiple of sixteen and within the bounds above. Unimplemented GSP state flags and nonzero reserved words fail. The cache-request bit uses the existing coherent CPU-backed memory contract; asynchronous GPU coherency remains unfinished.

A successful no-draw list emits one synchronous P3D completion (IRQ 5). The counter of tail-marker writes is separate from that command completion. This does not model physical interrupt timing or assert that hardware emits the same interrupt count. Failure preserves the pending queue entry, subsequent work and PICA register snapshot. Successful earlier queue entries remain committed.

The TXT report records completed lists, writes, markers and the last parser's result/register/offset. Guest-memory failures retain the existing memory-fault report. These observations are distinct from picture/launcher confirmations.

Evidence and next work¶

  • make check includes the PICA C suite and integrated GSP rejection/completion tests, normal and with ASan/UBSan. The standalone suite exercises capacity, aliases, counter overflow, the full wire-ID boundary and maximum write budget.
  • tools/validate-pica.py --sanitize --output <new-directory> compares 353 original cases, including real pinned libctru construction, native inspection, the independent Python decoder and checked no-draw state expectations. All 353 vector receipts match an emulated big-endian PowerPC Linux run; this does not establish physical Espresso execution.
  • The original normal-CRT/libctru GX application now sends a builder-produced finalized configuration list and waits for P3D. Its AOT execution exits after 1,489,172 instructions, with zero fallback, 17 GX commands and the expected masked viewport word. Its green/red/green pictures still come from CPU-backed fills/transfers, not PICA draws.

Native/GX RPX and WUHB cross-builds are available. Physical completion/report/exit behavior remains pending. The optional short GX guide is still the same picture test; no completed long input guide must be repeated for this increment.

Actual draw inputs/resources, vertex/shader execution, TEV/raster behavior, GX2 submission and reference scene/performance evidence remain open. Task m4-pica-commands is in progress, and M4 is not accepted.

Maintained source: docs/pica-commands.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Vertex attribute decoding¶

runtime/core/ctr_vertex.c supplies a bounded, original byte-stream decoder for the array-backed vertex formats needed by the PICA rendering pipeline. This is a portable preparation step. No PICA loader register, guest draw, shader or GX2 draw is enabled by adding this module.

Supported contract¶

The caller resolves a layout into at most twelve attributes and twelve streams. Each attribute identifies its stream, offset within a vertex, component count and destination shader-input slot. Destination slots are unique and range from 0 to 15, so the layout expresses an explicit input permutation. A stream supplies an owned byte buffer, its checked byte length, a first-vertex offset and a stride of 1 to 255 bytes. The attribute's entire payload must fit within that stride. Unaligned host buffers and component offsets are handled through byte loads; the caller remains responsible for resolving actual PICA loader alignment and padding rules before providing this explicit layout.

Format ID Source component Conversion
0 Signed 8-bit byte Exact, unnormalized float32 integer value
1 Unsigned 8-bit byte Exact, unnormalized float32 integer value
2 Signed 16-bit little-endian short Exact, unnormalized float32 integer value
3 Little-endian IEEE binary32 Finite bit pattern preserved on the verified host

Each attribute supplies one to four components. Missing components start at (0, 0, 0, 1), so a one-component attribute produces (x, 0, 0, 1) and an explicit fourth component replaces the default W. Unconfigured input slots also contain that deterministic vector but remain absent from the returned enabled mask; they are not claimed as supplied or fixed PICA attributes.

PICA has no unsigned-16 attribute format. IDs outside 0–3, NaN/Infinity encodings and incompatible host float representations fail visibly with unsupported. Float32 is only the interchange representation here: the module does not implement PICA float24 quantization, arithmetic or shader execution. The tests include signed zero, finite limits, subnormals and values that would need separate rounding for PICA float24. These are decoder checks, not hardware numeric conformance evidence.

Bounds and failure behavior¶

A call decodes at most 4,096 vertices; each supplied source span is limited to 4 MiB. The decoder checks every supplied stream, layout, destination capacity, first/last-vertex arithmetic and every finite-float encoding before it changes any output. A malformed later attribute or vertex therefore cannot publish a valid prefix. The decoder allocates no heap memory and never follows a guest address. The caller supplies owned, stable buffers.

Output may not overlap attribute descriptors, stream descriptors or any part of a supplied source span, including an unused tail. Read-only streams may overlap one another. Adjacent input/output spans are permitted. A null output with zero capacity validates the same layout, bytes and float policy without publishing vertices. A zero-vertex range validates the configuration without reading source components or changing output.

Errors distinguish invalid arguments/layout, unsupported formats/values, truncated source ranges, configured limits, insufficient capacity, aliases and arithmetic overflow. The output remains unchanged on every error. This contract does not interpret fixed attributes, retained input state, multi-loader overwrite semantics or implicit padding IDs; those require their own checked PICA-state adapter.

Indexed gathering¶

ctr_vertex_decode_indexed uses the same resolved attribute/stream contract and adds a caller-owned ctr_vertex_indices descriptor. Its format IDs are 0 for unsigned 8-bit elements and 1 for unsigned 16-bit little-endian elements. A byte offset locates the first index in the supplied span; unaligned index addresses are decoded with byte loads. first_index and vertex_count select elements, not byte offsets. Each element selects a source vertex independently in every stream, preserving requested order and duplicates.

For example, indices [2, 0, 2, 1] produce source records 2, 0, 2 and 1, with their input permutation and component defaults intact. Stream offsets resolve the source vertex base. The API does not add first_index to a decoded index, interpret PICA's vertex-offset register, or treat 0xff/0xffff as restart markers. The future state adapter must resolve actual guest addresses and loader rules.

The decoder validates the entire selected index byte range and every referenced attribute range before publication. It checks finite float encodings only in referenced vertices: an unreferenced NaN/Infinity is never read and does not reject an otherwise valid gather. A malformed final index or referenced float leaves all output unchanged. Validation-only calls obey those same rules.

The same 4,096-output and 4 MiB-per-source limits apply to indices. Index descriptors and complete supplied index byte spans, including unselected bytes, must not overlap output. Read-only index and attribute source spans may overlap. Zero output count validates descriptors/layout without reading index elements or source components. Checked first/last-element, width and byte-offset arithmetic rejects overflow before a load. The path allocates no heap memory and enables no primitive assembly, PICA command, shader or draw.

Origin and validation¶

The numeric format IDs and descriptor nibble packing were checked against the pinned libctru enums at 9b55eda44cf80b971503991e0c78f9bc8fe50425: GPU_ATTRIBFMT(i,n,f) packs (n-1) above the two format bits. Register names and the twelve loader banks are in the same revision's register definitions. Published register information is available at 3dbrew. The signed-short, missing-component and loader distinctions were also inspected in Azahar's reference loader at d409087405bc299b88ff35dd5b96b6e2698d5929. No emulator implementation is copied, compiled, linked or distributed with the runtime. The C decoder and all test data are original.

The unsigned byte/short index distinction and indexed-versus-nonindexed vertex offset behavior were inspected in that same pinned reference's pipeline register definitions and draw input selection. This decoder consumes explicit spans rather than implementing those registers.

Run make -C tests/vertex check sanitize. The suite checks every signed-short value, all byte values, all sixteen format/component descriptor combinations, lane defaults, shuffled input slots, twelve-stream layouts, nonzero first vertex, unaligned views, exact finite-float bits, late rejection without partial output, limits, range arithmetic and descriptor/source aliases. Indexed cases exercise every unsigned byte/short value, including 0xff/0xffff, reordered/repeated interleaved and twelve-stream inputs, nonzero first-index/byte offsets, unreferenced nonfinite data, late atomic rejection and full index-span aliases. The combined suite has 364,123 original assertions. Root make check-c and the portable Windows target include this suite, and the native Wii U source list cross-compiles the module. Desktop/sanitizer passes and cross-compilation do not establish Espresso execution or any rendered scene. Milestone M4 remains open.

Maintained source: docs/vertex-attributes.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

First software triangle scenes¶

v0.22.0 establishes the first original scene through a bounded PICA-format loader, index, shader and raster path. Two indexed triangles form a square, red at lower left and green at upper right. Both unsigned byte and unsigned short index formats produce identical pixels. This is a software correctness checkpoint for M4; full renderer acceptance remains open.

Version 0.23 connects this owned renderer to standard compiled guest draws. The focused native check below remains a separate original diagnostic.

Focused Wii U check¶

Build with python -m host build --target scene-check --output artifacts/my-scene-check. The matching CI run also supplies wiiu-scene-check-<source commit>, containing RPX/WUHB, build receipt, checksums, source identity and notices. Use its WUHB from sd:/wiiu/apps/ with the supported homebrew launcher, or its RPX with Homebrew Launcher. This is an original homebrew check, not an installable WUP.

The TV shows the byte-index scene; GamePad shows the short-index scene. Both should show a solid square with red lower left, green upper right and a dark border. The TV guide asks for two observations. Press R for correct or L for a problem; ZL skips and ZR records a failure. Minus closes the application and preserves results. Send the new plain-text files from sd:/ctr2wup/ and report whether Minus returned to the launcher. The automatic pixel self-test and render timings enter the small result and full trace; human display observations remain separate. An automatic failure cannot pass the picture guide.

This check uses the existing native audio initialization and report/exit lifecycle. It does not replace the pending Audio Check/GX Transfers 0.20.0 launch-music regression or repeat the completed Save/RomFS guides.

Execution boundary¶

runtime/core/ctr_scene.c accepts an explicit stable resource span, matching PICA physical base and an explicit owned linear RGBA8 surface. Row zero is the bottom. It never dereferences a guest/physical address. Every command list stages the state and entire target before committing; late errors preserve previous registers, counters and pixels. Failure reports describe an attempted staged prefix, not committed draws. Aliased reports are left untouched.

The bounded path supports:

  • PICA byte masks and finalized command records through the existing inspector; array loader offsets, component formats, alignment, padding, stride and input permutations; fixed attributes; indexed and nonindexed complete triangle lists.
  • Shader code/descriptor uploads, complete packed float24 or float32 uniform uploads, entry point, input mapping and compact output masks/semantic maps. MOV supports swizzle, negation, write masks, initialized temporary registers and uniforms; canonical NOP and END terminate a bounded linear program.
  • Position XYZW and color RGBA outputs; W exactly 1, clip XYZ in the accepted volume, exact 1/16-pixel coordinates, viewport origin/extent, winding cull, include/exclude scissor, degenerate triangles and integer edge evaluation. Each triangle has a constant opaque endpoint color: RGBA lanes are 0 or 1, alpha 1. Fragments replace the target with depth and blend disabled.

Limits are 256 vertices per draw, 32 draws per list, 256 shader instructions, 128 descriptors, 96 float uniforms, 12 attributes/loaders, a 4 MiB resource span and a 1 MiB target with dimensions at most 512×512. No primitive is carried across lists. Unknown pipeline/framebuffer/texture/TEV/depth registers, arithmetic, relative addressing, geometry, clipping, perspective, varying color, interpolation, sampling and blending fail visibly. This is not a general PICA renderer or a GPU shader translator.

runtime/core/ctr_shader.c accepts only finite normal values exactly representable as PICA float24, plus signed zero. NaN/infinity, denormals, out-of-range exponents and values requiring rounding fail. There is no float24 arithmetic or rounding approximation. MOV captures source lanes before writing in-place swizzles and rejects undefined lanes or missing code/END.

The native Scene Check runs this CPU path, flips its owned image rows for display, then uses the established GX2 surface-copy presentation. It does not submit a PICA shader as a Latte shader. The v0.22 standard guest GSP queue continues to use the separate configuration-only PICA executor and rejects draws.

Evidence and reference limits¶

Run make check, make prototype, then:

python tools/validate-scenes.py --output artifacts/scene-evidence \
  --nihstro-dir /path/to/clean/pinned/nihstro

The evidence tool executes both index formats and independently calculates the expected square regions. Exact comparison, with zero tolerance, checks every RGBA byte. Each image has 1,128 red and 1,176 green pixels; two draws execute 18 shader instructions. The validator saves command/resource bytes, raw RGBA, PNG previews and hashed JSON receipts. No commercial input is used. The receipt also records one desktop process CPU-time sample per render, including renderer allocation/validation/commit. These tiny scenes and sanitizer costs do not predict game frame rate or physical Espresso performance.

Original C tests cover exact float24 conversion against mathematical values, all MOV swizzles/negations/write masks, inputs/temporaries/uniforms, packed uploads, loader padding, output maps, cull/scissor, both index sizes, nonindexed winding, aliases, bounds and whole-list rollback. Normal and ASan/UBSan checks are part of GCC/Clang CI; portable checks also run on Windows.

The optional test adapter checks 12,290 encodings against unmodified nihstro headers at f4d8659decbfe5d234f04134b5002b82dc515a44 (BSD-3-Clause, Tony Wasserka). It validates instruction/operand encoding only. Azahar's pinned PICA register/output/loader descriptions provide semantic review; its current float24 model records approximate arithmetic, so it is not claimed as a hardware numeric oracle. No emulator code is copied, linked or bundled with the runtime.

The image below is actual desktop output from the original renderer, flipped to top-first display orientation. It is not Wii U or 3DS hardware evidence.

Remaining acceptance requires physical Wii U picture/timing/exit reports, independent hardware/reference raster observations, broader shader/fragment behavior, guest resource integration and a license-cleared GX2 shader route with measured costs. No commercial compatibility or full milestone PASS is implied.

Compiled original guest PICA draws¶

Version 0.23.0 connects a bounded PICA draw subset to the standard guest GSP queue. An original normal-CRT ARM application uses unmodified pinned libctru to submit two command lists, retain uploaded shader/state between them, render indexed triangles into owned linear allocations and display both screens. This is CPU software rendering followed by GX display transfers and existing native presentation. General PICA behavior and native GX2 shader translation remain open under M4.

Reproduce the checkpoint¶

After the verified SDK and ARM fixture setup, run:

python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application draw --wiiu --sanitize \
  --output artifacts/reports/compiled-draw-local

The output directory must be new. The report records the source commit and dirty state, library pin/hash, generated code identity, exact frame comparisons and RPX/WUHB hashes. The original program executes 17,420,794 ARM instructions with zero fallback, four draws, four triangles, 97,200 covered fragments and 36 vertex shader instructions. Its own target and transferred framebuffer checks must pass before it prints CTR2WUP compiled PICA draws PASS and exits.

The host runner checks every displayed pixel. A separate integer Python image oracle independently compares all 172,800 top/bottom pixels in the captured PPMs. The two images show a dark background and a central region divided diagonally into red and green triangles; top and bottom display sizes are 400×240 and 320×240.

Original host captures; the October 13 physical run has separate user picture confirmation and the guest's own target/readback PASS:

CI executes the sanitized original guest and native build on each PR. Download wiiu-guest-draws-<source commit> for the checked RPX/WUHB, PPM images, report, checksums, source/SDK provenance and notices. This artifact contains original diagnostics; it is not a game WUP or a physical-console result.

Resource and transaction contract¶

  • Commands arrive through the existing standard GSP/GX command-list operation; configuration-only lists retain the previous behavior.
  • Vertex/index resources and targets resolve only to tracked owned Old-family linear allocations: guest VA 0x14000000 maps to PA 0x20000000. Static guest mappings, VRAM and unowned physical addresses are rejected.
  • Permission splits must retain allocation identity. Source spans need read permission; tiled targets need read/write permission. Snapshots are bounded to 4 MiB. Relay memory, command-list/target aliases and source/target feedback fail.
  • One resource base and one target binding are supported per command list. Changing either between draws fails transactionally. State writes after the last draw may prepare the next list; shader/configuration-only uploads persist.
  • Targets are 8×8 Morton tiled RGBA8/ABGR storage, with dimensions that are multiples of eight and no larger than 512. The adapter converts to the renderer's linear RGBA view, then writes the complete target through checked guest memory once.
  • Supported fragment state is explicitly defined: primary-color/pass-through TEV, disabled textures/lighting/depth/stencil/blending, copy logic operation and full color writes. Required registers need fully defined byte masks. Unsupported or incomplete state fails before publishing pixels, shader state or completion.
  • Successful commit updates memory generations, invalidates affected code aliases and exclusive state, commits shader/register/counter state and publishes P3D completion. A failed list preserves its target and scene state. Earlier successful queue entries remain committed; the failing entry stays pending without its IRQ.

The scene contract defines the bounded loader, fixed attribute, uniform and MOV/NOP/END shader subset. Arithmetic shaders, sampling, general TEV, clipping, depth/blend/stencil and native GPU translation require separate scoped features. A raw VFP push/pop guard fix supports exact bit transfers under CRT FPSCR controls; it does not enable unsupported arithmetic modes.

Physical route and reviewed follow-up¶

The October 13 run records both guide steps PASS, own guest exit, zero fallback and clean shutdown. The rendered second presentation takes 57.33 seconds in the trace; the user estimates roughly two minutes. The first frame is startup output, and the visual guide accepted confirmation before the drawn frame. The user's separate picture confirmation is retained; presentation gating and phase timing need a focused follow-up. See the evidence review. This single run does not establish repeat-launch stability or useful game speed.

Copy the artifact's WUHB to sd:/wiiu/apps/ and launch it in Aroma when physical testing is requested for a new build. In 0.23.0 the focused Guest Draws guide requires a guest frame, but startup output also satisfies that guard. Wait for the actual red/green diagonal scene on TV and GamePad, then confirm the guest's own PASS and orderly exit. Minus returns from the native diagnostic. Preserve the named result TXT, source commit and artifact hashes; follow result collection.

The compiled program has three known startup service lookup failures (APT:S, APT:A, ptm:sysm) under the documented standalone startup profile. It still reaches its own main/assertions/exit. This does not claim complete service startup, console picture/timing, WUP installation or commercial compatibility. The earlier focused Audio Check/GX Transfers 0.20.0 launch-music regression has reviewed October 13 physical passes; it does not establish 3DS guest audio.

Tracking¶

Private filesystem and standalone application lifecycle (0.14.6)¶

This is shared runtime implementation, independent of any game patch. The original compiled program uses normal libctru/newlib fopen, fread, fwrite, fflush, fseek, ftell, fclose, unlink and the standard save-archive commit request. It creates a record, rereads it, uses a Japanese filename, deletes a temporary file, commits, exits, then reloads and updates the record in a fresh process. The host independently checks both saved files and their exact second-run bytes. Corrupt snapshots are refused and preserved, rather than replaced with empty saves.

The portable runtime opens fs:USER, initializes its session and supports:

Interface Commands
FSUSER Initialize / InitializeWithSdkVersion; OpenArchive / CloseArchive; OpenFile; CreateFile / DeleteFile; GetFreeBytes; ControlArchive(commit); scoped read-only OpenFileDirectly
FSFILE Read / Write; GetSize / SetSize; Flush / Close
Explicit archives Savedata (4), private virtual SDMC (9), and current-process read-only RomFS view (3), supplied by the platform

No guest path names a host file. The platform owns a volume until guest teardown; mounts and file sessions retain its lifetime. Archive IDs are generation checked and bound to their service session. File sessions survive archive closure and are retained across kernel-handle duplication. Closing a file session invalidates all copies; final kernel-handle close also releases it. Deleting a live file is explicitly refused in this bounded profile. Archive/file/handle exhaustion rolls back ownership, including a create request when a file session cannot be allocated.

Volumes have 16 root files, at most 1 MiB per file and 4 MiB total data. Request buffers are limited to 1 MiB. ASCII and valid UTF-16 paths are decoded to exact, case-sensitive UTF-8 names; traversal, nested directories, device syntax, separators and invalid Unicode are refused. Sparse extensions are zero filled; reads clamp at EOF without truncating 64-bit offsets. Read/write rights and read-only volumes are enforced. Guest buffers are checked across the entire requested range, including physical aliases of IPC storage, before file or guest output changes. Unsupported wire formats and commands fault visibly.

Writes change the buffered volume. Flush and commit succeed for a writable volume only when its platform persistence callback succeeds. A failed flush can leave dirty buffered data but does not claim a successful save. The original snapshot adapter uses explicit little-endian framing, bounded whole-input validation and a CRC32 corruption check. CRC32 provides no authenticity. It publishes through an exclusive sibling .pending file. v0.14.6 validates any existing primary, moves it to an absent .previous, then renames the stage into the vacant primary name. Successful publication removes the old backup. A failed publish attempts rollback; if rollback fails, both siblings remain available. This avoids relying on POSIX replacement semantics: WUT passes rename directly to FSA. The exact console failure stage is unknown; a no-overwrite filesystem model reproduces the assumption and exercises the corrected publisher.

If the primary is missing, a validated .previous can be loaded with the explicit RECOVERED status. A corrupt primary is never masked by a backup or reset. Stale .pending or ambiguous existing backup blocks another update visibly; preserve both for investigation. There is no concurrent-writer or power-loss durability guarantee. Failure/abrupt-process/rollback/recovery cases run under sanitizers.

The user's uploaded snapshot is CRC-valid, with /progress.dat and /記録.dat at visit 1. A private copy advances to visits 2 and 3 in the updated desktop runner while the original remains unchanged. Console update/reload acceptance is pending. The guest now names its failing phase; the independent result TXT records guest message, load status and persistence errno even if full tracing fails.

Read-only Level-3 RomFS assets are implemented separately; ordinary libctru handles nested names and directory listing in guest code. See romfs-assets.md. NCCH/IVFC extraction/authentication, cartridge assets, generic FSUSER directories, rename/subfiles, extdata, secure values, retail save formatting and commercial integration remain unfinished.

Standalone APT¶

The platform explicitly enables one standalone application (APPID_APPLICATION, 0x300). Only APT:U is advertised; privileged APT:S/APT:A remain unavailable. GetLockHandle returns copied handles to a retained recursive mutex. Initialize returns two real retained events; Enable queues one empty wakeup and signals the resume event. NotifyToWait, GlanceParameter and ReceiveParameter run the checked local state machine. Glance preserves the pending parameter, Receive consumes it. Checked static descriptors support the shell-open query and enable-transition unlock. CheckNew3DS returns the configured Old-family policy. PrepareToClose / CloseApplication terminate the process; Finalize unregisters it while copied objects retain their lifetime. This lets the unmodified libctru event thread, LightEvent/arbitration waits, normal initialization and exit code actually run.

The original boot checks aptIsActive, aptShouldClose and aptMainLoop in guest code. A single-instruction trace captures successful APT and FS requests; remaining lookup failures are exactly APT:S, APT:A, and optional ptm:sysm. The compiled runner explicitly mounts an empty read-only diagnostic virtual SDMC. This is not access to the user's SD card. There is no shell lid, HOME menu, sleep, cross-title launch, shared font, applet, CPU-time quota or New 3DS implementation. Unsupported operations fail; complete_service_startup remains false in reports.

Build and console check¶

make -C tests/filesystem check sanitize
make -C tests/apt check sanitize
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application filesystem --wiiu \
  --output artifacts/reports/my-save-test
python3 tools/validate-libctru.py --application boot --trace-startup \
  --output artifacts/reports/my-startup-trace

The additional ctr2wup-libctru-filesystem.rpx / .wuhb executes the same original ARM program through external AOT. Launch with an accessible mounted SD card; the existing diagnostic creates its ctr2wup directory on the WUT-owned SD mount. On a fresh diagnostic save it must display/log SAVE 1 PASS and state=exited. Plus restarts from the snapshot and must display/log RELOAD PASS; exit/relaunch must also reload. The private path is sd:/ctr2wup/filesystem-test.snapshot. Keep the existing file and any .pending / .previous for investigation if the test fails; never replace a failed load with an empty save. Minus exits the host. This is an original diagnostic save and RPX/WUHB, not a Mario save or an installable WUP. The initial snapshot is received; successful physical update/reload is pending. Use the current console retest and return its new result TXT.

Maintained source: docs/filesystem.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

CTR2WUP Development¶

Product: a Windows GUI that turns an owned 3DS game into its own installable Wii U WUP. Select a game, configure its title/artwork, Inject, install, and play from Wii U Menu. Super Mario 3D Land is the first candidate. Current releases are original diagnostics; commercial gameplay, the draw renderer and the GUI remain unfinished.

Tracking authority¶

This Project is the source of truth for task status, blockers and full milestone acceptance. Change Status and Waiting resource here. Issue descriptions hold scope, prerequisites, acceptance criteria and evidence; source files hold versioned implementation receipts. Source JSON is an evidence/initialization record and cannot overwrite an existing Project status.

The dashboard reads this Project and publishes aggregate counts. A publication error must leave the last successful timestamp visible; it never invents a fresh status or turns missing evidence into completion. Task completion is separate from the rough engineering estimate toward a playable injector.

Delivery gates¶

Gate Deliverable and acceptance boundary
M1 · Wii U platform Native displays, controls/audio, repeat launch/exit, verified SD results/save recovery, and an installed original WUP.
M2 · CPU translation Required ARM/Thumb/VFP and ABI/memory behavior, bounded visible fallback, and measured Espresso execution.
M3 · Homebrew runtime Original compiled guests on console, required services/modules/audio, and repeatable save/asset access.
M4 · PICA → GX2 Vertex/shader/fragment execution, textures and coherency, original reference scenes, and console measurements.
M5 · First game Privately identified Mario revision, boot/gameplay/audio/save/reload and measured performance.
M6 · Automated WUP conversion Reproducible Windows conversion and packaging, actual installation/Menu launch, rebuild cache and save continuity.
M7 · Windows injector GUI A responsive packaged Select → Configure → Inject workflow backed by the verified engine, useful diagnostics and clean-PC acceptance.

Each gate has an overview and five focused tasks. Dependencies describe what is needed to accept a task; independent fixtures and shared runtime development can continue while a prerequisite awaits evidence.

Status and review¶

  • Planned: scoped work has not started.
  • In progress: implementation exists but its criteria remain open.
  • Awaiting evidence: an artifact is ready for the named console/input/Windows check.
  • Blocked: a recorded prerequisite prevents completion.
  • Ready for review: the scoped change and required checks are ready to assess.
  • Done: a task's criteria have been reviewed; this never accepts its parent gate.
  • Closed: work was closed without representing accepted delivery.
  • Accepted: reserved for a full milestone overview after all its criteria and physical/title/Windows evidence have been reviewed. A merged PR cannot set it.

Keep the gate open until that review is complete. Archive only after preserving the acceptance record; archived managed items make synchronization fail visibly. Use Priority for ordering, and Waiting resource for the concrete next handoff. Record evidence in issue comments or maintainer notes; retain failures and verbal reports separately from versioned logs.

Console handoff¶

Named TXT collection, fresh-launch Save, embedded/SD RomFS and GX/configuration results are already reviewed. Physical testing is currently deferred; the focused Audio Check/GX Transfers 0.20.0 launch-music regression remains pending. The new compiled Guest Draws guide supplies a separate renderer route when testing resumes. Original diagnostics require no ROM or key. Retain named TXT reports and observed exit/pictures privately, with exact source and environment versions when available. Do not publish game dumps, decryption keys, retail saves or commercial traces.

v0.23.0 increment¶

An original compiled ARM/libctru program now submits two standard GSP PICA command lists, retains shader/state across them, draws to owned tiled targets, checks the pixels, transfers both framebuffers and exits with its own PASS. The route executes 17,420,794 ARM instructions, zero fallback, four triangles and 97,200 covered fragments. Independent displayed-image checks cover all 172,800 top/bottom pixels. See the guest draw contract.

Guest physical ownership, permission splits, resource/command/relay aliases, retained uploads, late failure and queue completion have 83,108 assertions. The raw VFP push/pop guard now preserves exact bits under CRT FPSCR controls; unsupported arithmetic still fails. Required coverage is 384 Python tests and 22 normal/ASan/UBSan C suites; the report guide has 636 assertions. v0.22.0 increment (historical)¶

The first original scenes now execute PICA-format loader/index state, fixed attributes, packed/float32 uniforms and actual MOV/NOP/END shader instructions through a checked software triangle renderer. Byte and short index formats produce the same two-triangle square, with exact independent pixel comparison and whole-list rollback on late failure. A separate pinned nihstro comparison checks 12,290 shader/descriptor encodings, without copying or linking an emulator into the runtime.

The original shader/scene suite passes 1,393,972 assertions. Required tests now include 372 Python tests and 21 normal/ASan/UBSan C suites; the report guide has 624 assertions. Native and focused Scene Check RPX/WUHB cross-builds pass. Receipts and actual desktop PNG output are retained in artifacts/reports/first-scenes-v022/; CI also publishes a focused wiiu-scene-check-<source commit> artifact with provenance and notices.

See the scene contract and console guide. The focused check runs CPU rasterization and existing GX2 surface copies; physical picture, timing and exit evidence remains pending. Standard guest GSP still rejects draws. Arithmetic, textures/TEV, depth/blend, clipping, GPU shader translation and commercial profiles remain unsupported. This is a significant M4 foundation, not full M4 acceptance. The Audio Check/GX Transfers 0.20.0 launch-music regression remains open. Completed PR branches are deleted after merge.

v0.21.0 increment (historical)¶

The original vertex decoder now gathers resolved array attributes through unsigned 8-bit or little-endian unsigned 16-bit index buffers. It preserves duplicates and index order, handles unaligned buffers and explicit byte offsets, and checks every referenced range/value before changing any output. Unreferenced nonfinite vertex data does not reject a valid gather; 0xff/0xffff remain ordinary indices. Index buffers and descriptors receive the existing full-span alias protection. No heap allocation, PICA register interpretation, primitive assembly, shader execution or draw submission is introduced.

The focused vertex suite passes 364,123 original assertions, including every 8-bit/16-bit index, reordered multi-stream/interleaved records, late rejection, aliasing, truncation and overflow. Required full-source and native-build receipts are retained in artifacts/reports/indexed-vertex-v021/. This advances M4's vertex/shader task without accepting the task or any full milestone.

The focused Audio Check/GX Transfers 0.20.0 console handoff remains current; this independent vertex increment requires no repeated completed console guide. GitHub management continues through its verified Actions credential. Completed PR branches are deleted after merge.

Failure states and lifecycle counters enter the small result and full trace. A two-step Audio Check asks whether launch music stopped and whether the original native tone played on both outputs. This is not 3DS guest audio, and physical silence remains pending.

The private physical round now verifies named TXT read-back, original fresh-launch save reload, embedded/SD RomFS and GX transfer/configuration. Correct pictures and Minus return have user confirmation; the launch-music defect remains separate. Validation: 371 Python tests and 20 normal/ASan/UBSan C suites pass; report/guide has 603 assertions and AX lifecycle has 149. Current Audio Check and compiled GX RPX/WUHB cross-builds passed. It checks complete layouts, ranges, capacity and aliases before publishing output. Lane defaults are explicit; fixed/index attributes, PICA loader-state adaptation, float24 arithmetic, shaders and draws remain unsupported. See the vertex contract. GCC and Clang normal/ASan/UBSan and emulated big-endian PowerPC Linux pass 166,547 original assertions each. The devkitPPC module compiles; its measured decoder stack frame is 64 bytes. This is not physical Espresso execution or a rendered scene.

v0.17.0 increment¶

The standard GSP command ring now executes DMA, triggered memory fills, unscaled equal-size display conversion, raw strided texture copy and cache-region checks. It commits commands in order and preserves a failing entry/later work; all ranges/permissions/allocations are checked before that command changes output. Staging handles guest overlap, relay aliases are protected, and bulk memory writes invalidate exclusive/code snapshots including executable storage aliases. PSC/PPF/DMA completions follow bounded ring behavior. PICA lists remain unsupported.

The original stock-libctru application uses 16 GX operations and verifies exact green/red/green TV images with blue GamePad pixels. Its sanitized AOT run exits after 1,487,784 instructions, zero fallback, with three deliberately unavailable startup services still recorded. Desktop PASS does not imply all services or Wii U rendering are implemented. The GX contract records alignment/size/format/layout limits and the optional two-step console guide.

Independent review found and corrected missing fill-trigger validation and signaling for dropped interrupts. Required make check passes 291 Python tests and 17 C suites, normal and ASan/UBSan. GX has 1,102 assertions; report/guide has 587. The transfer matrix has 400 original C/Python/reference color/layout comparisons and matching emulated big-endian PowerPC hashes. Reference color/ Morton primitives were tested; the external full transfer engine was not. Fresh Native and GX Transfers RPX/WUHB cross-builds carry v0.17.0.

Reports: artifacts/reports/gx-transfers-v017/. Delivery: artifacts/delivery/gx-github-0.17.0/, containing source/wheel, original Native/GX diagnostics, reference images, guides and checksum/manifest. The release record and CI provide repository-visible delivery/evidence; consult the actual Actions run status.

CTR2WUP product development plan¶

Updated October 13, 2026 for v0.23.0. The requirements remain the original brief, the reviewed GUI workflow and the user amendment allowing optional local encrypted-input preparation. Upstream feasibility scores and compatibility percentages are research context, not verified results. Current status records actual evidence.

Product and completion target¶

Deliver CTR2WUP.exe, a Windows GUI with an UWUVCI-style 3DS Inject page:

Select owned ROM → configure title/artwork/settings → choose WUP output → Inject → install on Wii U → launch from the Wii U Menu.

The shared engine must identify one supported Old 3DS revision, validate its executable/assets, translate ARM/Thumb/VFP ahead of time, compile the reusable Wii U runtime and shader/state data, stage stable per-game metadata/identity and produce a validated installable WUP. Decrypted .3ds, .cxi and supported base-application .cia inputs are supported intake routes; encrypted input uses user-managed local keys. No key or commercial asset is bundled or uploaded.

The first MVP supports one exact commercial revision. Super Mario 3D Land is the selected candidate, but the received small intake has no executable and no profile is enabled. Completion requires clean-Windows GUI conversion, physical WUP installation/Menu launch, repeatable gameplay, TV/GamePad mapping, required input/touch, audio, save/reload, rebuild/save continuity and useful measured performance. Record the revision, environment and remaining limits. The CLI is the shared engine; the final user workflow requires no manual shell commands.

Current checkpoint¶

Original normal-CRT/libctru applications now exercise boot, threads, input, framebuffer display, interactive execution, save/reload, RomFS, GX transfers and bounded guest PICA draws. The new draw route executes compiled ARM code through standard GSP commands and compares exact original two-screen scenes. It uses CPU software rendering and existing presentation; native GX2 shader translation, broader PICA state and physical renderer acceptance remain open.

Checked generated-code integration, owned guest memory, scoped services, original synthetic packaging and hosted Windows foundations exist. Reviewed physical Save/results/assets/GX evidence is retained. The launch-music regression, installed-WUP acceptance, commercial conversion and final GUI are still pending. 0/7 full milestones are accepted. Completed scoped features do not change that.

Now, next and later¶

Next independent renderer work Add checked shader arithmetic, then bounded TEV and texture sampling using original reference scenes M4 #28/#29; separate scoped features with numeric/pixel and rollback criteria

The user is available for focused physical tests with Windows 11 and Aroma. October 13 audio/GX/draw results are reviewed; the draw trace exposes a 57.33-second scene delay and premature visual confirmation. Continue original renderer/runtime and host development alongside targeted hardware follow-ups. Keep exact blocked acceptance visible; do not treat a host pass as a console result or ask for repeated tests of unchanged completed routes. Priorities may change when actual title requirements or measured bottlenecks become available.

Execution order and gates¶

  1. P1 — Translation and packaging foundations: extend exercised CPU/ABI/ fallback correctness and measure PowerPC costs. Resolve original-homebrew WUP installer/metadata/certificate prerequisites and prove Menu launch/exit. Host/emulated proofs already exist; physical acceptance remains open.
  2. P2 — Exact title analysis: identify the owned revision, code/assets/modules, observed service/GPU demands and a repeatable gameplay/save route. Enable a profile only after actual executable identity and support evidence are recorded.
  3. P3 — Shared runtime and renderer: implement the required reusable interfaces, shader/fragment/resource paths, modules, guest audio and retail saves. Advance the selected game through boot, menu, gameplay and save/reload; measure accuracy, timing, memory and stability on Wii U. Independent original probes can proceed now.
  4. P4 — Automated Windows engine: connect optional decryption, profile, extraction, translation, compile, shader/cache setup, stage, metadata/title ID, pack, validate and publish. Prove cancellation, trace import, revision-aware rebuild, save continuity and clean-Windows conversion without hand edits. Complete M6 gates.
  5. P5 — Windows injector: implement the specified WPF workflow over P4, provision actual prerequisites and prove a normal user can Inject, install and play the supported title. Complete M7 and the first MVP; broader compatibility follows.

Packaging and translator portability are independent streams. Resolve installation prerequisites early while runtime work continues. The GUI contract is specified now; GUI implementation follows the demonstrated conversion pipeline.

Tracking and visible development¶

Project #2 owns live status. Each change belongs to a scoped feature issue with native parent task, M1–M7 repository milestone and linked PR. The 35 gate tasks have native milestone parents. The reviewed private feature catalog backfills historical PRs and defines renderer follow-ups; development tracking explains the workflow.

Use focused branches, attributable commits and early pushed draft PRs. Required CI and documentation checks precede merge; completed remote/local branches are removed and verified. Trusted-main Actions perform management with the Actions credential and retain live audit receipts. A merge completes only its scoped feature criteria, never a full hardware/title/Windows gate by inference.

Reuse, privacy and feasibility¶

The pinned external host lifter has a checked portable ABI and original runtime adapter. Complete redistribution notices before bundling the external tool or ABI support. Adopt CPU/HLE components only after license and Wii U suitability review; a wholesale Rust/Vulkan desktop emulator port is outside the selected architecture. External emulator/reference code remains in separate test checkouts.

The original scoped packer/decrypting verifier exists. Earlier nuspacker/ CNUS_Packer candidates lacked an identified license grant and established certificate-template provenance; no such implementation or material is bundled. Synthetic package correctness does not establish authenticity, installer policy or physical Menu launch. Local packaging requirements remain explicit.

ROMs, keys, assets, translated commercial code, saves, traces and per-game packages stay private, with redacted diagnostics and user-managed local setup. Optimize measured bottlenecks and document failed performance gates before widening scope. Test counts and upstream desktop compatibility cannot replace product acceptance.

See milestones for full exit criteria, repository tour for source layout and development history for dated checkpoints.

Maintained source: docs/development-plan.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Windows GUI and injection workflow¶

Final product specification, finalized 2026-10-07. The user explicitly selected UWUVCI AIO as the workflow reference. The shipped product is a Windows GUI named CTR2WUP.exe. The CLI is the internal/testable build engine; the standard user flow runs entirely through the GUI.

Reviewed reference¶

Reviewed UWUVCI AIO WPF at commit d03b559bbac53642506557519108e5967d5fbd25. The source shows:

  • System navigation in UI/Windows/MenuWindow.xaml.
  • Three groups in UI/Frames/InjectFrame.xaml: BaseRom, Configuration, Packing; packing exposes a “WUP Installable” button after injection.
  • ROM selection, game name, icon/TV/GamePad artwork, image creation and an “Inject” action in UI/Frames/InjectFrames/Configurations/OtherConfigs.xaml.
  • Configuration import/export, output-path settings, asynchronous injection and packing, progress and completion UI.

This was a source/workflow review, not a Windows execution test. Its README lists DS/GBA/N64/SNES/NES/TurboGrafx/MSX/Wii/GCN, without 3DS support. Its source is GPLv3; no code, artwork or binary was imported into CTR2WUP. It supplies the interaction reference. CTR2WUP supplies the new 3DS AOT/HLE/GX2 runtime and uses its own original Wii U title template instead of a commercial VC donor base.

Main user flow¶

  1. Open CTR2WUP → 3DS Inject. The injector page is the default destination; navigation also provides saved projects, settings and help.
  2. Choose the game. Browse for a .3ds/.cxi or supported base-application .cia. Accept already-decrypted input, or offer Import keys from your 3DS when the detected encryption needs local material. The setup wizard explains supported console-export steps, imports selected files and shows missing requirements. Decrypt locally and validate before continuing. Display detected title, icon, region/revision and supported/experimental/unsupported status. Provide an actionable reason when the input cannot be injected. Automatically select the supported compatibility preset for the exact revision.
  3. Configure the injection. Edit the Wii U Menu title; preview or choose icon, TV boot and GamePad boot artwork. Use original default artwork when needed. Default to top screen on TV, bottom screen/touch on GamePad, native resolution and aspect-preserving scaling. Offer only implemented display options.
  4. Choose WUP output. Select a destination and review readiness. The app detects/provisions documented prerequisites through a first-run setup flow. Routine injection requires no manual compiler commands or shell session.
  5. Click Inject. One primary action runs game preparation, translation, compilation, runtime/assets staging, metadata creation, WUP packing and validation. Display the actual stage, elapsed time, progress and Cancel. Detailed logs and technical settings live in expandable diagnostics/settings. Decryption runs as a preparation stage; it does not require a separate game conversion action. Console key values never enter reports or WUP output.
  6. Receive the finished WUP. Show the completed package directory, title information, build report and installation guide. Offer Open output folder and an explicit Copy to SD action if implemented. Publish success only after the real package pipeline passes its checks.
  7. Install and launch on Wii U. The user installs with the documented compatible homebrew installer/environment and launches the new per-game title from the Wii U Menu. The Windows app creates the WUP; installation remains a console step with recorded test evidence.

The visible term Inject includes all of CTR2WUP's conversion work. A user selecting a supported game should receive its installable per-game WUP through that action. RPX diagnostics, generated C and intermediate staging are development artifacts, distinct from the completed user output.

State and persistence¶

The injector follows: Choose game → Configure → Ready → Injecting → Complete, with actionable Failed/Cancelled states. Unsupported games remain visibly unsupported; experimental status requires an actual available conversion path. Readiness includes the input/profile, toolchain, output space and required local packaging configuration. Progress and cancellation come from the shared engine.

Save/import/export an injection project containing selected input identity, settings, artwork paths and destination, keeping user-derived data private. Rebuild uses the same per-game identity and preserves existing saves. Auto-assigned title identities are stable and collision-checked; advanced overrides are validated. Installation instructions identify the supported console environment.

Implement the GUI in C# WPF on a supported .NET LTS release. Its engine interface uses versioned requests, stage events and reports. Document/provision all real Windows dependencies, and keep keys in local settings with redacted diagnostics.

GUI acceptance¶

A clean Windows machine can open CTR2WUP.exe, select the supported commercial revision, configure title/artwork/output, click Inject, cancel/retry when needed, and obtain a validated WUP without undocumented tools or hand-editing files. That WUP installs and launches from the Wii U Menu and passes the agreed gameplay, input/touch/audio/save/reload/rebuild test. The app stays responsive during the entire conversion. Its completion message distinguishes validated package output from previously recorded physical-console compatibility evidence.

October 12 encrypted-input amendment¶

The user asks for both decrypted input and local decryption using their own 3DS material. The shared engine now implements the scoped preparation/import path in encrypted-input.md. The eventual GUI includes import/remove/reselect key storage and guided export help, with optional on-console companion integration only after hardware/tool validation. The Windows app cannot read keys directly from a stock console over USB. An imported key set may need title keys/seeds; one console-specific SD key is not a universal standalone-ROM key.

Acceptance adds real locally exported keys plus an owned encrypted fixture, recovered-code/asset integrity, missing/wrong-key messages, cancellation/cleanup, protected Windows storage and redacted diagnostics. No key is bundled, fetched, uploaded, included in exported projects or shipped in WUPs. Preparation success does not override a game's unsupported profile or physical compatibility gate. The GUI remains unimplemented until the underlying commercial pipeline works.

Maintained source: docs/gui-workflow.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

These are general startup interfaces, not requirements observed in the inaccessible Mario executable or a Mario patch.

Selected by the user on 2026-10-08 UTC. A CIA attachment was subsequently supplied on October 7 (America/New_York), but exceeds the workspace's 32 MiB transfer limit. Its filename suggests USA title 0004000000054000, CTR-P-AREE, v0.3.0; these are unverified filename claims. Its bytes/encryption/hash/revision have not been inspected. cia-intake.md supplies a small local executable export helper for this transfer constraint. No commercial profile is enabled, and there is no reproduced CTR2WUP boot/gameplay/save or Wii U performance result. The MVP is still the Windows GUI's Inject → per-game WUP → Wii U Menu workflow.

Evidence and requirements to investigate¶

The pinned Zakuro source provides concrete leads rather than a compatibility guarantee. At commit 76f17bf76cd9ea238a94f37d389fe701ab05a673:

  • zakuro-core/src/services/mod.rs and services/mii_selector.rs include tests explaining that the game's saved Mii must agree with its CFG console identity. APT/Mii selection, CFG identity and save/reload must therefore be consistent; an empty generic applet response is unsuitable.
  • zakuro-gpu/src/raster/hardware.rs notes small color-buffer CPU readbacks while loading a course. GPU completion and guest-visible memory coherence need real handling, beyond presenting a final framebuffer.

These are source observations that have not been reproduced using the user's revision. The desktop Vulkan implementation is not a GX2 implementation. No upstream executable/assets/caches or implementation from these locations was copied into CTR2WUP.

Area Intake/implementation evidence needed
Revision NCCH/NCSD identity, region/version, input/executable hashes and module inventory
CPU ARM/Thumb/VFP coverage, CP15/TLS, exceptions, exact generated-code/RSS/memory costs; measured fallback
Kernel/IPC Required SVCs, threads/synchronization, srv: routing and traced command shapes
Declared services Read the private dump's extended-header allowlist; confirm actual use by tracing

Private intake¶

Keep a fully decrypted .cia, .3ds or .cxi under ignored private/; no keys are needed for already decrypted input. Avoid update/DLC merging for the first exact revision. The following produces metadata and declared capabilities, not a supported or playable conversion:

python3 -m host analyze-title private/game.cxi --lifter --output private/first-title-intake

The report preserves exact hashes, segment/stack/BSS layout, declared service access and section inventory. The external lifter analysis remains private. Do not infer implemented/observed IPC from the service allowlist. Later extraction and module/shader analysis will use this revision to prioritize concrete runtime work. The synthetic intake commands and CIA/executable-export fixtures are verified; actual commercial intake remains pending the smaller private export.

Proposed manual route, to agree after identifying the dump: cold boot, title menu, start a new save, enter/play the first course, test movement/jump/touch as used, finish/save, exit and relaunch, then rebuild the same title ID and confirm save continuity. Record firmware, homebrew environment, installer, screen/audio behavior, frame/instruction timings and fault/fallback logs. This route is not claimed to have been executed.

While private executable intake is pending, v0.7.0 adds shared heap/linear CPU allocation, free/protection/query and an original three-backend ARM probe. These are reusable startup interfaces, not an inferred Mario patch/profile. Its actual memory needs, thread/shared-memory behavior and GPU/DSP mappings still require the supplied revision and runtime evidence. See guest-memory.md.
