CTR2WUP — Work Mode development brief¶

Paste this entire brief into Work Mode, or attach it and say: “Use this brief as the project requirements. Begin implementation and continue through the milestones that can be verified in this environment.”

Objective¶

Develop CTR2WUP, a Windows desktop application that accepts a user-provided, decrypted Nintendo 3DS game dump and produces a per-game Wii U installation package.

The intended user experience is:

  1. Select a supported decrypted ROM.
  2. Review detected title information and compatibility.
  3. Choose an output folder and press Build.
  4. Receive an installable WUP directory.
  5. Install it on a suitably configured homebrew Wii U.
  6. Launch the game from the Wii U Menu, with the 3DS top screen on the TV and bottom screen and touch input on the GamePad.

Internally, use ahead-of-time ARM11-to-portable-C/C++ translation, devkitPPC compilation, a reusable Wii U 3DS HLE runtime, and a PICA200-to-GX2 graphics backend. The Windows PC performs analysis and compilation; the console executes the resulting PowerPC application and supplies compatibility services.

This is a staged research and development project. Producing a WUP is one milestone; proving a game runs correctly and at useful speed is another. Do not promise broad compatibility or full-speed gameplay before measurements exist.

Scope and development rules¶

  • Begin with Old 3DS application software, 2D output, native guest resolution, offline play, and one validated game revision.
  • Accept decrypted NCSD .3ds and NCCH .cxi when the parser actually supports them. Reject encrypted input with a clear explanation. Add other formats later.
  • Use open-source homebrew toolchains. Do not depend on Nintendo's proprietary SDK, a donor Virtual Console title, or bundled commercial assets.
  • Keep user ROMs, extracted assets, translated game code, saves, generated packages, and credentials out of source control and public artifacts.
  • Start with a CLI and a working Wii U prototype. Add the polished Windows GUI after the execution pipeline is demonstrated.
  • Reuse suitable existing implementations after checking licenses and architecture. A publicly visible repository without a license is not automatically reusable.
  • Proceed through routine implementation decisions without asking for repeated confirmation. Ask for a game dump, console results, or a material product decision only when needed.
  • Continue useful independent work while external testing is pending. Keep externally dependent milestones marked unverified.
  • Never replace missing core functionality with mock success. Unsupported instructions, services, and GPU states must be observable.
  • Do not report console success from a desktop test, cross-compilation, emulator screenshot, or package validation alone.
  • Maintain a Git repository, reproducible build instructions, dependency versions, a milestone checklist, and a concise STATUS.md so later Work Mode sessions can continue reliably.
  • On a blocked dependency, record the actual error, implement a supported alternative where feasible, and identify the smallest user action needed. Do not repeatedly retry unavailable infrastructure.

First actions: establish the actual foundation¶

Inspect any existing workspace and its instructions before creating a new project. Inventory the available compiler, Windows build tools, devkitPPC/WUT, network access, test fixtures, and console access.

Audit these candidate projects from their current source, recording commit hashes:

  • https://github.com/fearkov/3dsrecomp
  • https://github.com/fearkov/zakuro
  • https://github.com/mtrdrgz/3ds_recompiler
  • https://github.com/devkitPro/wut
  • https://github.com/devkitPro/wut/tree/master/samples
  • https://github.com/azahar-emu/azahar
  • https://github.com/ihaveamac/nuspacker
  • https://github.com/NicoAICP/CNUS_Packer

Treat this list as candidates, not approved dependencies. Verify each repository's existence, license, build process, CPU semantics, runtime dependencies, actual compatibility evidence, and suitability for 32-bit big-endian PowerPC.

The checked README of mtrdrgz/3ds_recompiler describes ARM-to-C++ lifting with HLE, interpreter fallback, and desktop/Web targets. It also lists gaps including dynamic CRO loading and some services. Its success claims are author reports, not Wii U benchmarks. Source access to the fearkov repositories was not fully verified when this brief was prepared; inspect them directly before relying on their claimed coverage.

Select the smallest viable foundation based on a reproduced build and code inspection. Prefer adapting a proven lifter and HLE implementation over writing every subsystem from scratch. Do not assume desktop-specific SIMD, Rust runtime dependencies, floating-point control, pointer casts, or host threads will port unchanged.

Deliver docs/foundation-audit.md with a reuse/license matrix, the chosen architecture, measured build results, and unresolved risks. Then immediately begin the first executable prototype.

Proposed architecture¶

Keep these components independently buildable:

Component Responsibility
Windows host CLI Input inspection, build orchestration, manifests, logging, profiles, packaging
ROM/container tools NCSD/NCCH parsing, ExeFS/code decompression, RomFS access, metadata
Translator adapter ARM/Thumb/VFP lifting, block discovery, code generation, missing-code feedback
Portable runtime core Guest memory, registers, dispatcher, interpreter, kernel/HLE interfaces
Wii U platform backend GX2 output, VPAD input, audio, timing, threads, files, saves
PICA backend Commands, state, shader processing, textures, rendering, synchronization
Packaging adapter Wii U executable metadata and validated WUP generation
Windows GUI A usable front end over the same build pipeline

Suggested source layout: host/, translator/, runtime/core/, runtime/wiiu/, renderer/, packaging/, profiles/, tests/, docs/.

Initially link translated code and the runtime into a single RPX. Consider RPL splitting only if executable size or loading measurements justify it. CRO relocation and guest module behavior still need their own implementation; making an RPL does not solve them.

Use a versioned manifest with input identity, executable/module hashes, runtime version, translator version, profile, display settings, toolchain versions, package identity, and stage results. Separate stable title/save identity from executable hashes used to invalidate compilation caches.

Milestone 1 — Native Wii U platform and packaging smoke test¶

Build a minimal WUT/devkitPPC application before involving 3DS execution.

Implement:

  • Separate TV and GamePad output with different diagnostic images.
  • GamePad buttons, sticks, calibrated touch, and an orderly exit.
  • A simple audio sample.
  • Timing, logging, and allocation diagnostics.
  • A minimal host build command and dependency doctor command.
  • A packaging investigation using an original homebrew title and metadata.

Target top-screen presentation at 400 × 240 and bottom-screen presentation at 320 × 240. At a 1280 × 720 TV output, a centered 1200 × 720 top image is a useful default. For the GamePad, compute the viewport from the actual rendering buffer and calibrated input coordinates; do not assume physical panel pixels are identical to GX2 surface coordinates. Ignore touches in borders.

Acceptance: produce a valid RPX and record successful physical-console dual-screen, input, audio, and exit tests. Independently attempt a WUP smoke package, documenting the installer and homebrew environment required. WUP installation is not supported on an unmodified retail console merely because packaging succeeds.

Milestone 2 — Portable CPU translation correctness and benchmarks¶

Translate small, reproducible ARMv6K/Thumb/VFP test programs and compile the generated code for desktop and Wii U.

Implement explicit guest little-endian memory access. Guest addresses remain 32-bit guest addresses; translate them through validated mappings rather than casting them into host pointers. Handle permissions, aliases, shared memory, overflow, alignment, and access failures deliberately.

Verify:

  • ARM condition flags, conditional execution, shifts, carry/overflow, ARM/Thumb transitions, indirect branches, and exception behavior.
  • Load/store ordering, exclusive access semantics where required, and code invalidation.
  • VFP register layout, rounding modes, denormals, NaNs, signed zero, and integer conversion.
  • Defined C/C++ behavior without unsafe aliasing or signed-overflow assumptions.
  • A dispatcher and interpreter fallback that preserve guest state across calls, waits, interrupts, and yields.
  • Missing-block logs keyed by executable/module hash and ARM/Thumb state.

Use trusted instruction-reference behavior and differential tests where available. Include tests running on PowerPC; a little-endian desktop pass cannot establish big-endian correctness.

Measure arithmetic, memory, branch-heavy, VFP, and dispatch workloads on real hardware, including generated-code size, link time, peak memory, and elapsed time. Measure fallback execution time as well as instruction counts. A tiny fraction of interpreted instructions can still consume substantial runtime.

Acceptance: documented correctness results and actual PowerPC timings. These benchmarks inform feasibility; they do not establish game performance. Fix semantic failures before expanding game coverage.

Milestone 3 — Boot a recompiled 3DS test application¶

Create or reuse a redistributable 3DS homebrew fixture in the executable/container format this pipeline supports. A .3dsx fixture may require its own loader; do not silently treat it as NCCH.

Implement the minimum guest environment the fixture actually exercises:

  • Executable layout, initial register state, stack, TLS, and memory regions.
  • SVC handling and a deterministic guest scheduler.
  • Handles, events, waits, timers, synchronization, and IPC transport.
  • Required service discovery and minimal APT, FS, HID, CFG, and GSP behavior.
  • Guest framebuffer presentation, frame timing, and touch/button state.

Start with controlled guest scheduling instead of assuming one Wii U core per 3DS subsystem or mapping every guest thread to a host thread. Preserve observable synchronization behavior.

Acceptance: the recompiled fixture executes guest code on Wii U, updates both framebuffers, responds to GamePad input and touch, and exits or runs a documented stability session. Distinguish a framebuffer demo from a working PICA renderer.

Milestone 4 — PICA200-to-GX2 renderer¶

Implement and test incrementally:

  1. PICA command/state decoding and draw submission.
  2. Guest framebuffer formats, rotation, display transfers, and clears.
  3. Vertex formats, attributes, uniforms, and vertex shader execution.
  4. Texture formats, ETC decoding, swizzling, samplers, and caching.
  5. Fragment combiner/TEV behavior, blend, depth, stencil, and alpha tests.
  6. Render-to-texture, aliasing, readback, and CPU/GPU coherency.
  7. Lighting LUTs, geometry processing, procedural textures, and other features required by the selected title.

Use software rendering or software vertex processing as temporary correctness references where useful. Do not assume a full software renderer is fast enough for the final Wii U build.

Investigate available Latte shader assembly/compilation tooling and its licenses before choosing a shader pipeline. Offline compilation may require runtime traces and title profiles. Scanning ROM assets cannot guarantee discovery of every shader or dynamic PICA state.

Build a versioned shader/state cache and explicit handling for misses: supported generic translation, slower correctness fallback, or a diagnostic failure. Never silently render unsupported states incorrectly.

Acceptance: fixture scenes compare against a trusted reference using defined tolerances and visual inspection; console timing includes CPU submission, GPU work, transfers, and synchronization. Expand features according to observed requirements.

Milestone 5 — One commercial Old 3DS title¶

Request one user-owned decrypted dump when needed. Choose the initial title using reproduced upstream compatibility, required services, renderer features, module complexity, and a short repeatable gameplay test. Do not select a demanding title solely because it is popular.

For the exact revision supplied:

  • Complete required services and kernel behaviors.
  • Add CRO loading/relocations and translated-module dispatch if exercised.
  • Implement required DSP/audio behavior and Wii U output.
  • Implement RomFS reads and correct save/extdata archive behavior.
  • Preserve saves across application exit and later rebuilds.
  • Add missing-block trace import and cache invalidation.
  • Use narrowly scoped, documented profiles matched by hashes.
  • Log unimplemented IPC with enough context to implement it accurately; generic success responses are acceptable only when their behavior is explicitly justified.

Acceptance: boot, menu, interactive gameplay, working input and required touch, synchronized audio, a successful save/reload, and a repeatable stability run on physical Wii U.

Record game revision, settings, console environment, test route, frame-time distribution, memory peak, fallback time, graphical/audio issues, and logs. Use the title's intended frame rate: a 30 fps title has a 33.3 ms frame budget; a 60 fps title has 16.7 ms. Preserve guest timing rather than speeding up the game to improve a counter.

If useful performance remains out of reach, identify measured bottlenecks and attempt targeted optimizations. Document the resulting limits before broadening scope.

Milestone 6 — Automated ROM-to-WUP CLI¶

Expose real commands such as doctor, inspect, build, rebuild, import-trace, and validate-package. Use machine-readable results and nonzero exit codes for failures.

The build pipeline must:

  1. Validate the container and detect executable revision.
  2. Resolve a supported profile or report experimental status.
  3. Extract/analyze code and required modules.
  4. Generate and compile PowerPC code.
  5. Prepare available shader/state caches.
  6. Link the runtime and stage content.
  7. Generate original Wii U metadata and title artwork.
  8. Package the staged title through the validated packer.
  9. Validate identities, sizes, metadata consistency, and content integrity.
  10. Emit the package, build report, and installation requirements.

WUP generally means a directory of installable files, not a single executable. Distinguish it from RPX launch and WUHB/menu shortcuts.

Assign application title IDs using a documented homebrew policy, collision registry, and override validation. Hashing a ROM is insufficient to guarantee a safe, collision-free Wii U title ID. Keep IDs stable for rebuilds and saves; reject system/reserved identities and mismatches among metadata and package files.

Where packaging needs user-supplied credentials or keys, use a documented local configuration and redact them from logs. Do not bundle them. Validate signature/installer requirements separately from package structure.

Support cancellation, paths with spaces and Unicode, sufficient-disk-space checks, interrupted builds, and cached stages keyed by all relevant inputs. Publish final output atomically so incomplete builds cannot be mistaken for finished packages.

Acceptance: a clean Windows machine with documented prerequisites builds and validates the test title through the CLI. Confirm installation, Wii U Menu launch, separate saves, rebuild behavior, and uninstall behavior on the supported console environment. Never automate installation or overwriting console titles without the user's direction.

Milestone 7 — Windows desktop application¶

Build a native Windows desktop front end, preferably C# with a supported .NET LTS release and WPF unless the repository provides a better justified choice.

Include:

  • ROM picker and detected title, region, revision, and platform information.
  • Compatibility state: untested, unsupported, experimental, or console verified.
  • Output folder, artwork preview, and simple display settings.
  • Dependency checks and actionable errors.
  • Real per-stage progress, elapsed time, cancellation, and exportable diagnostics.
  • Build reports and an Open output folder action.
  • Rebuild from runtime updates and imported fallback logs.
  • Advanced settings behind an expandable panel.

Default to top on TV, bottom on GamePad, native rendering, and aspect-preserving scaling. Add screen swapping if straightforward. Mark unsupported features unavailable rather than letting users select ineffective options.

Acceptance: the GUI invokes the same proven pipeline, remains responsive during builds, and completes the supported-title conversion on Windows. Package the application's actual dependencies; do not advertise a self-contained one-click converter while requiring undocumented shells or tools.

Completion and continuation¶

The first MVP is complete only when one supported Old 3DS revision is converted by the Windows app, installed as a Wii U title, and verified through the agreed gameplay/save test. Broad compatibility is a later release.

For every milestone, provide:

  • Source changes and reproducible build commands.
  • Automated test results where they establish correctness.
  • Physical-console checks with actual evidence, or a precise “awaiting console test” status.
  • Current supported features, known failures, and the next concrete task.

Maintain docs/architecture.md, docs/build-windows.md, docs/console-testing.md, docs/compatibility.md, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES, STATUS.md, and versioned build reports.

When console access is unavailable, provide a ready-to-run diagnostic RPX or package plus a short test checklist and requested logs. Continue other verifiable tasks, but leave dependent feasibility and performance gates open. Do not end after generating a skeleton or a GUI mockup.

Defer New 3DS exclusives, stereoscopic TV output, online services, cameras, microphone support, DLC/update merging, save import/export, and broad arbitrary-ROM claims until the initial title works. Track each deferred feature so titles requiring it are identified accurately.

Begin now with the source/license audit and executable Wii U platform prototype.

Maintained source: docs/project-brief.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Foundation audit¶

Audited 2026-10-07 against source obtained through the existing authenticated Git transport. Every requested repository was reachable. Sources are outside this project at /workspace/ctr2wup-deps; none of the seven candidate source trees was vendored into CTR2WUP. Hashes below identify the inspected source, rather than an unpinned README or release claim.

Decision¶

Use fearkov/3dsrecomp as a pinned, external host lifter candidate, with Zakuro's MIT CPU and HLE interfaces as the primary runtime port candidate. The lifter and small CPU package both reproduce successfully on this machine. Keep generated code, guest memory and scheduling behind a C/C++ runtime boundary; compile Wii U code with WUT/devkitPPC. The first prototype must remain independently executable without a game dump.

This selection establishes a host-side foundation. It does not establish that Zakuro's Rust desktop runtime can run on Wii U, that its entire CPU/VFP behavior is correct, or that a game can be lifted into a working Wii U program. A full HLE runtime and PICA-to-GX2 backend are still implementation work. Before distributing 3dsrecomp code or its emitted ABI header, resolve the missing copyright/license-notice file described below. An external local tool invocation avoids silently vendoring an incomplete notice.

Do not adopt mtrdrgz/3ds_recompiler, nuspacker, or CNUS_Packer implementation until an applicable license grant is identified. Public source access is not permission to redistribute it. Azahar is a useful semantic reference and an explicitly GPL-covered alternative; incorporating its implementation would require a compatible project licensing/distribution decision.

Source and reuse matrix¶

The v0.22.0 scene increment additionally checks shader encodings against the unmodified nihstro headers at f4d8659decbfe5d234f04134b5002b82dc515a44, BSD-3-Clause, copyright 2014 Tony Wasserka. The original test adapter runs in a separate test executable; external headers/code are never copied, linked into the runtime or bundled with artifacts. Azahar's pinned PICA register/loader descriptions remain a semantic reference only. Its approximate float24 arithmetic does not establish hardware accuracy; the new runtime subset uses exact MOV values and explicitly rejects arithmetic/rounding. See software scenes.

These are x86-64 Linux results. They are neither PowerPC timings nor proof of Windows or physical-console execution.

CPU and 32-bit big-endian portability¶

3dsrecomp¶

The host lifter has real ARM, Thumb and VFP emitters, interpreter callbacks for unsupported instructions, an instruction budget and SVC exits, module-aware discovery/relocation analysis, and generated code identity checks. src/lib.rs exposes generation separately from compilation; build::Options can select a compiler. src/compile.rs defaults to desktop shared-library flags and must be adapted for RPX/static linking rather than assuming port outputs a Wii U project.

Guest registers/addresses are fixed-width uint32_t. The C ABI uses host page pointers rather than casting guest addresses directly to host pointers; this is a useful boundary, but its current fast path is not big-endian-safe. In abi/recomp.h:168–206, 16/32-bit guest loads and stores use native-endian memcpy. The generated multiple-transfer span path in src/codegen/mod.rs:81–120 calls load32/store32, which also use native-endian memory representation. Merely fixing fallback callbacks will leave mapped fast-page and span accesses wrong. A Wii U adapter must replace the guest-word helpers or disable those fast paths and provide explicit little-endian callbacks.

VFP double bit assembly in the C header numerically joins low/high words before bit copying, which avoids one common big-endian lane mistake. Floating arithmetic still uses host float/double. The emitter explicitly leaves some conversions to the interpreter, and compiler flags disable FMA contraction, but exhaustive FPSCR rounding, NaN/denormal, signed-zero, exception and conversion correctness is unproven. Exclusive monitors, invalidation, aliases, page permissions, ABI offsets and 32-bit pointer sizes need targeted checks. The existing C/Rust layout test only ran on this host.

Zakuro¶

zakuro-cpu is a small, useful interpreter candidate with a Bus interface and u32 guest state. zakuro-core/src/memory/mod.rs:627 uses explicit little-endian guest reads/writes. zakuro-cpu/src/vfp.rs:49 constructs double bit patterns from numeric low/high words. Those design choices fit a portable runtime better than a flat host address reservation.

The whole runtime is substantially less portable: zakuro-core/Cargo.toml:14 unconditionally enables Vulkan; GPU code uses wide::f32x8, Rayon, native worker threads and desktop window/GPU loading. Raw page pointers and unsafe Send appear in memory/mod.rs:78; recompiled.rs:260 passes pointer-backed page tables through the C ABI. Wii U thread, allocator, filesystem, timing, renderer and static-link boundaries must be supplied. No devkitPPC-compatible Rust target/runtime has been validated.

VFP operations use native f32/f64; upstream explicitly leaves strict default-NaN propagation for future work in vfp.rs:639. Ninety-two passing host instruction tests are useful regression evidence, not a complete ARMv6K/VFP oracle. Reuse the test ideas and compare against trusted references, then run on actual big-endian PowerPC before allowing broad instruction support.

mtrdrgz/3ds_recompiler¶

The source contains a Python ARM/Thumb/VFP lifter, C++ HLE runtime, interpreter fallback, software PICA renderer and desktop/WebGPU paths. Important source hazards are independent of its license blocker: rt/mem.h reserves 4 GiB on native hosts and maps guest VA by g_base + va; read/write helpers use native-endian memcpy. A 32-bit Wii U address space cannot provide that native 4 GiB reservation. WASM selects a different 512 MiB path, which does not establish PPC support.

The shader fast path uses __m128/SSE2; ARM64 builds use sse2neon, not PowerPC. Host FP control mappings target x86/ARM64, with an alternate software path requiring separate validation. Kernel scheduling creates a host thread per guest thread. README acknowledges missing dynamic CRO loading, services, and generic-success service stubs. Its author reports a playable retail title on desktop/Web; that result was not reproduced here.

Azahar¶

Azahar is a broad emulator and useful HLE/PICA semantic reference, but its documented host platforms are 64-bit x86-64/ARM64 desktop/mobile systems. src/core/CMakeLists.txt:552 selects Dynarmic only for x86-64/ARM64; GCC/Clang generic builds choose computed-goto FastInterp. This does not establish PowerPC correctness.

Concrete big-endian hazards remain in FastInterp: fastinterp.cpp:642–695 copies guest bytes directly to native 16/32-bit words and back; handlers/arm_vfp.inc:18–26 copies adjacent low/high u32 words into a native double. Azahar's normal memory access and endian wrappers do not fix these bypass paths. GPL-covered implementation reuse must preserve applicable notices and corresponding-source obligations; no such source has been copied into CTR2WUP.

WUT and sample suitability¶

WUT directly targets Espresso/PowerPC EABI with hard float (share/wut_rules: -mcpu=750 -meabi -mhard-float) and exposes OS, GX2, VPAD, audio and RPX/RPL support. This is the appropriate native console SDK candidate. The sample source demonstrates orderly WHB startup/shutdown and actual GX2 APIs rather than emulated desktop equivalents. samples/cmake/gx2_triangle expects pos_col_shader.gsh in SD content; its presence is a sample dependency, not evidence that arbitrary PICA shaders can be compiled for Latte.

SDK/library source review does not itself produce a valid RPX. The platform prototype's separate build report records the actual toolchain setup and compile result. Physical Wii U dual-display, touch, audio and exit acceptance remains awaiting console tests.

Packaging investigation¶

Both audited NUS packers require staged Wii U code/content/meta, package metadata and local key configuration. Neither is a direct .3ds converter. nuspacker/README.md:5 explicitly requires patched IOSU; packaging does not make a title installable on an unmodified retail console.

The implementations create TMD/ticket/content/hash structures, but do not generate authentic Nintendo signatures. Java/C# TMD code uses zero RSA signature fields; ticket code uses random signature bytes. Content and H3 hashes concern content integrity and do not establish authenticity or installer acceptance. Certificate templates also need independent provenance/license review.

Both upstream entry points echo supplied encryption keys (Starter.java:186–187, Program.cs:188–189); never pass secrets through logged commands. A tracked Java encryptKeyWith placeholder exists, but no credential value was printed, adopted or copied. Keep keys in user-managed local configuration, redact diagnostics, and obtain only credentials actually required by the selected future packer.

Actual source-only build errors: javac: command not found and dotnet: command not found, both exit 127. No packer executable was run, no WUP was produced or installed by this audit, and no source license gate was bypassed. Continue with an original structural validator and staging/reporting support; choose a licensed packer or obtain an explicit upstream grant before integration. A structural validation pass alone must not be reported as a successfully installed title.

Remaining gates¶

Resumed 2026-10-08: v0.3.0 integrates the pinned external generation driver with the original checked ABI; v0.4.0 implements original scoped encrypted packaging with independent decrypt/FST/file checks. cryptography 50.0.0 uses its BSD-3-Clause grant, preserved in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES/docs/licenses. Offline packaging uses original synthetic credentials only; the real WUT RPX passes staging/packing/decryption comparison. No packer candidate implementation or certificate template was adopted. See packaging.md for the current contract; installer acceptance, certificate provenance/trust and Menu launch remain gates.

  • Complete 3dsrecomp's redistribution notices; validate the original packaging profile on console.
  • Replace/verify little-endian fast helpers, 32-bit ABI layouts, guest permission/alias handling, exclusive access and invalidation.
  • Validate ARM/Thumb/VFP semantics differentially, including unsupported-instruction failure visibility and state at SVC/yield boundaries.
  • Establish a supported Wii U interpreter/HLE platform port; the desktop Zakuro runtime is not a ready-made RPX runtime.
  • Implement and measure PICA-to-GX2, guest services, CRO behavior, audio and save semantics required by a controlled fixture.
  • Run physical-console platform diagnostics and PowerPC benchmarks; use those measurements before choosing a commercial game revision or promising performance.
  • Validate Windows installation/builds and a complete original homebrew packaging path with a suitable installer; then request the smallest specific console evidence or user-owned dump needed for subsequent milestones.

v0.16 asset/texture layout review (October 12, 2026)¶

Read-only format references: GodMode9 f3b9ec7a50b8c8459f62b87bfd7b7214b6f6bda2 (arm9/source/game/romfs.c and romfs.h), the already pinned Azahar texture/color sources, and Zakuro 76f17bf76cd9ea238a94f37d389fe701ab05a673 texture/format modules. Canonical NCCH IVFC physical/logical layout and Level-3 metadata facts informed original bounded host verification. No GodMode9 code or bundled key database was adopted, read for key material or redistributed. Its GPL license remains in its separate read-only reference checkout; it is not a runtime/build dependency.

The original base texture decoder has independent golden vectors and a Python reference. A test-only Rust wrapper compiles the clean pinned Zakuro modules in that external checkout, compares original images, and remains outside source/ delivery binaries. The known HILO channel-layout difference is documented in renderer-status.md rather than hidden in a blanket comparison claim. No Azahar implementation is copied or linked. These checks do not prove GX2 rendering, real-game assets or Wii U performance.

Batch CIA 3DS Decryptor investigation¶

Reviewed October 12, 2026 for CTR2WUP 0.16.0. Its decryption methods can fit CTR2WUP's input-preparation stage. Retain the project's original checked engine; accept independently validated decrypted output as an additional input route. Decryption alone does not translate ARM, provide 3DS services, render PICA draws on GX2 or produce a playable Wii U WUP.

Sources reviewed¶

Project Pinned source Finding
matiffeder's original batch script af35b08a94f1662bd4ad23ccb35d2ab52f18712d MIT script; orchestrates external decrypt, ctrtool and makerom executables
Redux 2fb1ac549a4006dd5261b8545e943527960ceeff Rewritten batch wrapper; bundled binaries/seed DB; no license file in reviewed tree
Source-based ctrdecrypt fork 1763c5ebf04de24e8cdd672a391aae84dc45eef3 GPL-3.0; readable AES/container/seed implementation
ctrdecrypt upstream a0d170c2153fe4054bd241ef4605ea64c1f3cc99 GPL-3.0 source, referenced by later batch packages
rcyggdra rewrite 9984cd001bcd249af04d19eb9d015fd2ab742d76 MIT root label, but its two Rust decryption files match the GPL fork byte-for-byte

The original release thread is linked by the reviewed README; it was not used as executable provenance. Original decrypt.exe is opaque in the reviewed packages. Reading later Rust sources does not establish the exact behavior/source of those Windows binaries. Repository text and tree metadata were inspected; no bundled executable, seed/key database, downloader or actual game was downloaded or run. Source literals were redacted from the local audit excerpts. No external implementation or credential constant was incorporated into CTR2WUP.

What the software actually does¶

The original script loops over CIA/3DS inputs. ctrtool inspects CIA title/content metadata, a separate decrypt engine produces decrypted NCCH partitions, and makerom rebuilds decrypted CIA or NCSD/CCI output. Base-game CIA conversion commonly ends in a .cci, not a CXI. .cci and .3ds can both contain NCSD; the magic/layout, rather than renaming, identifies the format. makerom's -ignoresign does not create authentic Nintendo signatures or a Wii U package.

The readable later engine uses CIA AES-CBC ticket/title-key/content decryption, NCCH AES-CTR section counters, hardware-style KeyX/KeyY derivation, primary versus secondary ExeFS keys, and optional SHA-256 title-seed checks/derivation. It includes shared platform/common-key constants; it does not require a unique personal console key for every ordinary retail title. Some input types still need a particular title key or seed. The exact required material depends on the dump.

That engine also tries Nintendo seed endpoints when a seed is missing locally, logs derived key-related data, and supports forms outside CTR2WUP's current base-title scope. Those behaviors have not been adopted. CTR2WUP's current route uses explicitly local file imports, redacted diagnostics and no automatic key/ seed download. The final GUI will expose local preparation using that same engine.

Fit with CTR2WUP¶

Mechanism Current CTR2WUP position Decision
CIA outer AES-CBC and ticket/title key Implemented with local file references and full TMD plaintext-content hash checks Keep existing original implementation
NCCH section AES-CTR / mixed ExeFS keys Implemented for v2 and standard/1/0a/0b methods Same method; no need to launch the batch wrapper
Seed DB / seed-derived KeyY Local import, seed verification and derivation implemented Keep local/private source; GUI import remains planned
Decrypted NCSD/CCI output Accepted by magic; main NCCH preparation and asset checks available Useful optional handoff from a user-run decryptor
Rebuilding a CIA/CCI with makerom Unnecessary for the injector's main executable/asset preparation Do not add a dependency just to repack the source
Batch convenience Can inform eventual queued input/progress handling Use controlled engine jobs, structured results and isolated outputs
Fixed-key or NCCH v0/v1, updates/DLC/system/DSi titles Unsupported in CTR2WUP's current preparation profile Separate implementation/evidence needed; no new compatibility claim
Windows executables Not run in this Linux/cloud audit Native Windows/source-build/provenance tests remain pending

The original batch performs broad wildcard deletion of NCCH intermediates in its working directory. Redux documents non-ASCII filename limitations and cleanup of decrypted CIAs even when CCI conversion fails. These are concrete integration problems for a GUI that must preserve user inputs and support spaces/ Unicode. CTR2WUP already uses bounded parsing, isolated staging, immutable source checks and atomic non-overwriting publication.

Reuse and licensing decision¶

The original MIT wrapper can be adapted with its notice, but adds little to the existing orchestration. Bundling decrypt.exe as an unexplained binary would leave source/provenance and behavior unresolved. Redux's reviewed tree does not supply a blanket redistribution grant for its wrapper and bundled dependencies. The readable Rust engine is GPL-3.0; use would require the applicable source/ license obligations and an explicit integration design. A fork's MIT badge is not evidence that inherited GPL code was relicensed: the reviewed main.rs and ctrutils.rs match the GPL fork's full SHA-256 hashes. This audit does not make a legal conclusion about the publisher; it records why that label is insufficient for choosing a dependency.

Recommended adaptation: keep CTR2WUP's original decryption/preparation code, use published crypto/layout facts as cross-checks, and validate a user's already prepared CIA/CCI/CXI through the same inspection/full-IVFC/revision path. A future source-built external backend is technically possible, but no integration need or licensing/provenance resolution is established by this audit.

Verified handoff and remaining proof¶

An original synthetic decrypted .cci passed inspection -> prepare-rom -> full IVFC -> prepare-assets -> executable-only intake. The prepared blob matched its original bytes, the executable revision survived and the source stayed unchanged. This verifies the supported NCSD/CCI handoff, not execution of Batch Decryptor or its Windows binaries, and not an actual Mario dump.

A local handoff can use:

python -m host prepare-rom "MyGame-decrypted.cci" --output "private/prepared"
python -m host prepare-intake "private/prepared/game.cxi" --output "private/intake"
python -m host prepare-assets "private/prepared/game.cxi" --output "private/assets"

The standalone v0.5 Windows CIA helper expects CIA; a .cci is handled by the Python engine above, not by renaming it to CIA. When resources are available, the small private intake can be supplied without uploading keys or a full asset blob. Actual user-key exports, Mario input, Windows execution and console gameplay remain unverified. No further resource is needed from the user while away.

Audit receipts: artifacts/reports/assets-textures-v016/decryptor-audit/, including source pins, license/source comparisons and cci-interoperability.json. External source excerpts, binaries and key databases are excluded from delivery.

Maintained source: docs/batch-decryptor-audit.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

v0.14 adds CTR2WUP-libctru-filesystem-0.14.0.zip. Launch its WUHB/RPX with an accessible mounted SD card; the existing diagnostic creates its ctr2wup directory on the WUT-owned SD mount. A fresh diagnostic save must show/log CTR2WUP compiled filesystem SAVE 1 PASS and state=exited. Press Plus: it must reload, update and show RELOAD PASS. v0.13 adds GUEST THREAD SCHEDULER PASS CODE 0 in native mode and thread_selftest PASS result=0 in the SD log. Record its 345 instructions/eight switches, or the exact failure. This compares original interpreter/AOT contexts, per-thread TLS, owner-aware locks, waits and exit; no ROM is needed.

The separate CTR2WUP-libctru-interactive-0.13.0.zip supplies ctr2wup-libctru-interactive.rpx/.wuhb, a real original compiled libctru program. It starts in standard GSP display mode: top red, A held turns it green; bottom blue, a valid GamePad touch marks the corresponding white pixel. B is consumed by the ARM application itself and exits its graphics thread/process. Plus restarts; Y switches between its display and native diagnostics; Minus exits the host. Check all native self-test rows as well. This build has no custom ARM framebuffer mode and no guest PICA/audio/FS implementation.

Record CTR2WUP INTERACTIVE READY, increasing paired GSP swaps, zero fallback, touch corners/release, the guest CTR2WUP compiled interactive PASS and state=exited after B. Startup currently records eight failed IPC requests (six APT lookups, one FS lookup and one invalid session); preserve the IPC logs rather than interpret these as successful APT/FS initialization. Test ten-minute stability, repeated Plus restarts and clean host shutdown. Do not compare its diagnostic instruction budget with commercial performance or claim graphical-game compatibility from these frames.

Artifacts and launch¶

Use artifacts/wiiu/ctr2wup-prototype.rpx with an RPX-capable homebrew launcher, or the .wuhb with a compatible Aroma environment. For Aroma, place the WUHB under sd:/wiiu/apps/ following that environment's homebrew application guidance. Record the precise homebrew environment, firmware and launcher versions used. These are diagnostic applications, not WUP installation directories. Do not attempt to install the RPX/WUHB through a WUP installer.

Current v0.14.0 delivery is CTR2WUP-diagnostic-0.14.0.zip; it contains the native RPX/WUHB, the instructions and direct/shared display reference PPMs. The separate original/external-guest bundles contain the other starting modes.

Version 0.2.0 also includes ctr2wup-guest-fixture.rpx/.wuhb, which starts in controlled guest mode. All current builds contain three modes; Y cycles native, custom-I/O ARM guest, standard HID/GSP ARM guest, then native. Use the native mode for the original checks below, then run the guest checks.

Version 0.3.0 also supplies ctr2wup-upstream-guest.rpx/.wuhb. Run the same guest checks with this build: it executes the actual external lifter's code through the checked portable ABI. The embedded CPU self-test remains the original bounded test; collect the guest instruction/frame/fault logs for this adapter. Neither the RPX/WUHB nor the separate title-staging ZIP is an installable WUP.

Version 0.6.0 embeds the original TLS/SRV/CFG startup probe in all three builds. In native mode, record TLS/SRV/CFG STARTUP PASS CODE 0 and the corresponding service_selftest PASS result=0 SD log, or the exact FAIL code. This compares an 89-instruction ARM service sequence in interpreter/original AOT, without a ROM. The separate external-AOT service probe has desktop/emulated PPC evidence only.

Version 0.7.0 embeds the original ARM heap lifecycle probe in all builds. Record GUEST HEAP LIFECYCLE PASS CODE 0 in native mode and heap_selftest PASS result=0 in the SD log, or the exact FAIL code. It compares 151 instructions across interpreter/original AOT: heap/linear CPU allocation, queries, protection, partial free/hole reuse and complete release. No ROM is required. GPU/DSP physical memory and commercial startup remain untested.

v0.8.0 embeds the shared-memory/synchronization probe. Record SHARED/SYNC LIFECYCLE PASS CODE 0 and kernel_selftest PASS result=0, or FAIL codes. Its 148 ARM instructions/27 SVCs check aliases, mutex/semaphore/event waits and ownership release. v0.13 separately embeds the multi-thread test below.

v0.9.0 adds HID ARM CONSUMER PASS CODE 0 / hid_selftest PASS result=0. Native GamePad rows show ARM-read HID buttons, packed signed Circle Pad XY, packed touch XY/validity and an increasing snapshot count. Compare these with the native VPAD rows while holding/releasing A/B/L/R/directions, moving the left stick and touching corners/center/borders. Touch release must clear position and validity; border touches must remain invalid. SD HID live logs must show waiting between samples, increasing snapshots, fallback0 and no fault; shutdown should report exited. Motion and C-stick are absent. Host Y/Plus/Minus controls are reserved for diagnostics. No commercial dump is needed for this test.

v0.10.0 adds HID/GSP RGB565 DISPLAY PASS CODE 0 and gsp_selftest PASS result=0. This compares an original ARM standard-HID/GSP graphics program through interpreter/original AOT, including rotated RGB565 pixels and cleanup. The external translator comparison is separate desktop/ emulated-PPC evidence, even in the external-guest RPX/WUHB build.

Requested evidence¶

  1. Launch and photograph both independent screens together. TV shows moving colored bands at logical 400×240; GamePad shows a distinct checkerboard at 320×240. Verify centered aspect-preserving borders on your actual outputs.
  2. Both sticks and held buttons update on the GamePad. Confirm VPAD errors are absent under normal input. Press A several times: a short 440 Hz tone should play from TV and GamePad, with the tone counter increasing.
  3. Touch the guest image's corners and center; the crosshair should match. Touch the black side borders: no guest touch should be accepted. The code calibrates using VPAD's selected 1280×720 coordinate space then maps into the queried GX2 surface dimensions; logs record both dimensions and viewport.
  4. CPU ARM/Thumb AOT self-test should show PASS, result0. It executes an original 40-instruction fixture in interpreter and generated paths and compares their registers, flags, little-endian data, and counts. Report FAIL codes exactly.
  5. X repeats the 64 KiB aligned allocation/write/read diagnostic. Leave it running for ten minutes and record hangs, frame-time changes, glitches and logs.
  6. Minus should return cleanly to the launcher/homebrew environment. Also test normal HOME/lifecycle handling supported by your environment. Record any failure to exit or audio continuing after exit.

The older v0.14.0 builds write sd:/ctr2wup/native-*.log; v0.14.1 uses named native-*.txt, compiled-interactive-*.txt, and compiled-save-*.txt reports. Optional WHB UDP logging uses port4405 (UDP_LOG=1 developer build); it is off by default in v0.14.2. SD TXT logging remains enabled. Supply the log and the two-screen photo, test duration and console environment. The log includes CPU outcome/timing, pointer bits, output surface dimensions, allocation, input/touch/tone events, frame timing and shutdown. These timings include CPU software image scaling and GPU copy/synchronization; they are not game timings.

Controlled ARM guest checks¶

See guest-fixture.md for the contract and desktop reference images. No ROM is needed: this build embeds only the original generated ARM fixture.

  1. Enter guest mode with Y or launch the guest build. Confirm completed guest frame count increases; the TV background starts blue and GamePad starts teal. Record state/counters and any fault. Each guest frame needs multiple display iterations because execution is limited to 50,000 instructions per iteration.
  2. Hold A through the next complete guest frame: the top changes to green. Hold touch inside the bottom viewport: the next bottom frame changes to purple and draws a white guest pixel at the touch position. Check corners and center; touches in black borders must not enter guest input. Record latency.
  3. Press B: the guest should show exited after completing its current frame; the host remains responsive. Plus restarts it and its frame counter. Y cycles into the standard HID/GSP mode and then native diagnostics. Minus exits the entire application.
  4. Leave guest mode running for ten minutes. Collect both-screen images and SD logs with guest_open, debug/SVC events, increasing frames/instructions, fallback0 and no faults. Include your test duration and firmware/launcher.

Guest pixels are ARM-generated; the status overlay is host-generated. Success would establish this controlled fixture's PowerPC/input/presentation behavior, not stock 3DS graphics, PICA/HID/GSP or commercial compatibility.

v0.11.0 also adds GSP QUEUE/VBLANK PASS CODE 0 and gsp_relay_selftest PASS result=0, checking mapped queues, delayed presentation, IRQ delivery and cleanup through the original interpreter/AOT paths.

v0.12.0 adds ARM/THUMB STACK CALLS PASS CODE 0 and stack_selftest PASS result=0, checking 47 original instructions, nested ARM/Thumb calls, stack/register restoration and cleanup. Include that line in your SD log and native-screen results. This is original interpreter/AOT console evidence; external and Unicorn results remain separate developer checks.

Standard HID/GSP queued guest checks (0.11.0)¶

  1. Cycle Y until CTR2WUP HID / GSP QUEUE / VBLANK is shown. Completed frame count must increase without faults or fallback. This guest reads HID shared memory/events and submits both RGB565 screens through the GSP shared queue, then waits for VBlank and drains IRQs.
  2. Top should be blue, or green while holding A. Check the corner markers: white top-left, red top-right, green bottom-left, blue bottom-right. The host status overlay is in the middle so every corner remains visible.
  3. Bottom should be cyan; valid touch changes it to magenta with a white pixel at the guest touch coordinate. Check center/corners and border rejection. Release must restore cyan. ARM fills run over several bounded host slices, so record visible latency rather than assuming one display-frame response.
  4. B exits this guest; Plus restarts both graphics guests. Cycle all three modes, leave the GSP mode running for ten minutes and test Minus/HOME lifecycle. Return both-screen photos and SD gsp_selftest, gsp_relay_selftest, GSP relay, GSP guest, gsp_guest_shutdown logs, duration and firmware/launcher environment.

Compare with shared-display-reference/frame-{1,2}-{top,bottom}.ppm in the native diagnostic bundle: frame1 holds A/touch at 100,80, frame2 releases both. These reference images have no host status overlay. Success establishes the scoped CPU/shared framebuffer bridge and relay on the diagnostic clock. It does not establish stock libctru initialization, real 3DS timing or PICA. Check registered=1 while running, increasing VBlank and swap counters, no device faults or dropped IRQs, and registered=0/clean exit after B. Record coalesced intervals and input latency; slow CPU fills can legitimately coalesce host ticks.

Packaging prerequisite checks¶

Version 0.4.0 supplies an experimental original encrypted writer, not a packaged installable diagnostic. Its offline proof uses synthetic key/certificates and deletes that temporary package. Use the local-input contract in packaging.md only after establishing your own inputs and console/installer environment. Never try to install the synthetic test fixture.

A separately generated original homebrew package needs recorded installer acceptance, Wii U Menu icon/boot artwork, launch into both screens, input/audio, clean exit and removal. Record the title ID and installed-title inventory; confirm no existing title was replaced. FST/ownership/COS metadata and the nonhashed content/ticket policy may need corrections from those results. No console installation, replacement or removal is automated by these tools.

Not yet available for console validation¶

Proven installable WUP, broader commercial service boot, PICA renderer scenes, commercial gameplay, DSP, retail saves, CRO modules, and representative CPU/VFP/fallback performance benchmarks. No commercial ROM is needed for the native test above. One user-owned fully decrypted Old 3DS revision will be requested for the commercial milestone for the selected Super Mario 3D Land candidate's private revision analysis.

Maintained source: docs/console-testing.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

Covered 3DSX startup source¶

Unmodified files from https://github.com/devkitPro/devkitarm-crtls at 1c0c10257c44bbb5a433453bb6bba91582825492, supplied under MPL 2.0 for the startup code embedded in the optional compiled libctru interactive artifact. The full license is ../MPL-2.0.txt. These files and their MPL notices remain available as source without CTR2WUP's project license replacing their terms. No modification to these original source files was made.

On 2026-10-09, normal devkitARM arm-none-eabi-as -mcpu=mpcore -mfpu=vfp -mfloat-abi=hard 3dsx_crt0.s and objcopy of .crt0 produced the same section bytes as the installed armv6k/fpu/3dsx_crt0.o: 7d6c815f71e12fa284d444fc7d63924de75e63d4b9461b4f7eb2ed15f14e1afe. Their complete readelf -r output also matches. Whole-object SHA256 differs: source-built 997a20aad1f26a45d0c2b9ded8af0d6fe62ebc4c05e94279a51e3996fd25d3f9, SDK 6f1188b6f8b64d36818e95a4cc54a95c857e1a0bd0ca41906081efe6babda895. This establishes the linked section/relocations, not identical SDK package builds. The preserved 3dsx.ld and 3dsx.specs also match the installed SDK files byte for byte.

Preserved file SHA256:

  • 3dsx_crt0.s: 0cd0169dd5b711a2adf5efefe57134afff22a70b54e51cc4a87e608fd11071a8
  • 3dsx.ld: 930b274d551cb4b61718c09d8bfbc76212f5f574152f43937687984b916508aa
  • 3dsx.specs: 2663fca222c4a97bd51fae61a19f95bdabc068000685997ecbe7b11a6675f84b

The original application C, build flags, pinned library, checked translation driver/adapter/runtime and reproducible generation instructions are supplied in the CTR2WUP source bundle. No compiler/toolchain binary is redistributed.

Maintained source: docs/licenses/devkitarm-crtls/README.md · 0b5e01cd0abc. GitHub source and issue links may require repository access.

GitHub development¶

Project #2 is the authority for status, waiting resources and reviewed acceptance. GitHub also records source, reviewable changes, milestone issues, CI logs/artifacts and versioned original diagnostic releases. Keep the product goal and current limits visible; no badge establishes commercial gameplay or physical-console acceptance.

Roadmap and evidence¶

Each of the seven milestones has an overview tracker and five focused work issues. The 35 work issues carry acceptance criteria, prerequisite links and required evidence; the seven overviews coordinate full milestone acceptance. Area and waiting labels identify CPU/runtime/graphics/host work and console, input or Windows gates. Checked foundations and open full-acceptance items are separate. Add test evidence in comments; maintainers update the versioned source assessment after reviewing it.

docs/milestone-status.json supplies versioned requirements and evidence for managed milestone descriptions, overviews and work-issue sections. Its state fields seed empty values at initial setup; existing Project states and waiting resources always win. The Project tracking workflow updates evidence sections on trusted main without deleting comments or manual notes, duplicating trackers or automatically closing acceptance gates. To preview or explicitly apply a source update with normal gh authentication:

python3 tools/sync-github-progress.py
python3 tools/sync-github-progress.py --apply

Labels and branch status¶

Labels Meaning
status:planned Defined work awaiting implementation or triage.
status:in-progress Active/partial work, a draft PR or checks still pending.
status:blocked Missing prerequisites or current failed checks.
status:awaiting-evidence Scoped implementation awaits required evidence; also an explicit PR hold.
status:ready-for-review Current PR head has passing Required CI and Documentation checks; maintainer review remains.
status:done A Project task was reviewed as Done, an unmanaged task completed, or a PR merged.
status:accepted A Project overview was explicitly Accepted after full-gate evidence review; never a task or merged PR.
status:closed A task was abandoned or a PR closed without merging.
area:*, documentation, bug, enhancement Component and kind of work.
needs-console, needs-input, needs-windows Specific waiting resources.
p0 / p1 / p2 / p3 Emergency / urgent / normal / low triage priority.

Managed roadmap issues mirror Project Status and recognized Waiting resource categories. Edit the board fields to change them; an issue closure or manual label does not change canonical Project state. PRs and unmanaged issues retain their lifecycle/check-based status automation. Other labels, priorities and manual notes are preserved. Closing a task or merging code never accepts a full milestone. Use status:awaiting-evidence to hold a PR for missing validation; CI does not remove that hold. Record runtime and visual evidence in the issue/PR, using original reference frames, browser captures or guided console TXT observations.

The Work-item statuses workflow responds to issue/PR lifecycle, deliberate status-label changes and completed CI/Documentation runs. It skips Project-managed roadmap issues and leaves their mirrors to Project tracking. It executes trusted immutable main code and reads API data; it never executes a contributor's branch or downloads untrusted build artifacts. Status writes touch only managed status labels. Current merge checks must include the actual PR head, so old results cannot mark newly pushed code ready. The Actions summary/artifact reports branch names, associated PRs, states, checks and reasons.

python3 tools/sync-github-status.py --output artifacts/github-status.json
python3 tools/sync-github-status.py --number 12 --status awaiting_evidence --apply

GitHub labels apply to issues and PRs. Use focused names such as feature/m4-pica-commands, fix/m1-result-write or docs/testing-guide; their status is visible through the linked PR, CI checks and the workflow report. Release Git tags identify versions and remain separate from task status.

The structure adapts Odysseus's coordination milestone, label taxonomy and review template: focused tasks, explicit blockers/evidence, triage priority and reproducible review. CTR2WUP's acceptance criteria remain specific to Wii U execution and the planned injector; unavailable console/game tests stay pending. The user's GitHub Free, private-source and no-branch-protection choices remain in force.

Pull requests and CI¶

Use a topic branch and the PR template for reviewable problem/behavior/validation changes. CODEOWNERS identifies the maintainer. Required CI summarizes:

  • workflow lint with checksum-pinned actionlint;
  • Python 3.12 and 3.14 fixtures and installed-wheel smoke checks;
  • GCC and Clang portable tests with ASan/UBSan;
  • native Windows host and normal portable C fixtures;
  • Wii U RPX/WUHB build using the official immutable devkitPro SDK image.

Actions are pinned by commit, permissions are scoped per workflow/job, fork PRs receive no repository write access, logs survive failures and concurrency avoids unneeded duplicate PR runs. Test/build artifacts include the source commit; Wii U artifacts include checksums, provenance and notices. Dependency update PRs cover actions and Python dependencies. Review them; they are not auto-merged.

The separate manual Windows workflow validates real external AOT/Unicode execution and WPF tooling. CodeQL is manual-only and requires confirming existing scanning eligibility. This setup does not buy or enable Advanced Security. Read each run's actual result; workflow publication is not proof that hosted checks succeeded.

Releases¶

Development releases are prereleases with explicit diagnostic scope, source tag, notes, checksums and matching source/wheel/console artifacts. Do not call an RPX/ WUHB a WUP or label a diagnostic release playable. No real game, key, seed DB, save, private trace or extracted commercial asset belongs in an Actions artifact or release. Preserve older requested test kits and their versioned instructions.

The 0.17.0 kit contains Native and GX Transfers. Its release also includes a single offline HTML guide and generated public docs-only site bundle. The 0.14.6 kit remains the Save/RomFS follow-up. Getting started explains SD layout, Aroma/RPX launch and report delivery.

The Publish development diagnostics workflow is dispatched explicitly from main with the version in pyproject.toml. It runs required checks, verifies SDK/ lifter/libctru pins, executes the original GX application with sanitizers, builds RPX/WUHB and smoke-tests the wheel. The packager checks reported binaries/frames, source and wheel module equality, ZIP CRCs and SHA-256 sums. A separate job with contents: write publishes the matching prerelease with GitHub's native token; a failed upload leaves a draft. Published versions cannot be replaced by different artifacts. This route also avoids the cloud connection's rejected upload-host authentication. It does not close console or commercial acceptance gates.

Agent support¶

Shared instructions, Copilot guidance and four specialist profiles cover CPU/runtime, host conversion, graphics and read-only console-evidence review. Use supported Copilot clients when the account has access. Profile files do not activate a subscription or automatically assign work to an external service. Current development also used independent parallel runtime and workflow/documentation reviews; actual findings were corrected and checks rerun.

Codex work uses the repository's opt-in commit attribution hook. Its verified public bot author and standard Codex co-author trailer provide visible credit while normal Git/PR authentication continues through the connected account. It does not impersonate a bot login or rewrite earlier history.

Documentation and free hosting¶

The Markdown guides are readable directly in GitHub. Each Documentation workflow build also provides a searchable Material site and a single HTML guide that opens directly in a browser, with navigation, search and a print layout. The HTML guide requires no account, server or internet connection to read its content. Download it from a matching development release or Documentation run artifact.

The public CTR2WUP documentation site is deployed on free GitHub Pages while application source stays private. The export contains rendered guides, notices, provenance and a pinned Pages workflow; application code, ROMs, keys and private test data are excluded. Publication and Pages Actions succeeded, and HTTP checks verify the site, dashboard, JSON and chart. Private-source Pages is not used. The offline reader remains available.

python3 -m venv artifacts/docs-build-venv
artifacts/docs-build-venv/bin/python -m pip install -r docs-requirements.txt
artifacts/docs-build-venv/bin/python tools/build-docs-site.py
artifacts/docs-build-venv/bin/python tools/package-docs-site.py

The strict build validates rendered links, anchors, search and assets. The site is in artifacts/docs-site/site/; the HTML guide and public-repository bundle are in artifacts/docs-publication/. Their JSON receipts and checksums identify the source commit. Source links stay commit-specific and require repository access. The site and HTML guide preserve the product goal, progress and acceptance limits.

Maintenance on GitHub Free¶

The private source repository uses topic branches and reviewable pull requests. Check the latest Required CI and Documentation build on the PR before merging; keep source and evidence together. Preserve history and use a new version for changed published artifacts. Dependencies/actions are pinned, CODEOWNERS identifies the maintainer, and contributor/security/result guides provide the review context.

The user explicitly declined branch protection because the repository is private on GitHub Free. No protection rule or administration setup tool is incorporated. These maintenance practices are team conventions; GitHub does not enforce them.

Board setup¶

The owner-selected Project #2 is the tracking source of truth. Setup resolves that exact board and all 42 managed issues. It fails before writes on inaccessible/ambiguous data and never creates a replacement board. Existing title, notes, option IDs, manual items, nonempty field values and existing view filters/layouts are preserved.

The detailed overview explains the seven deliverables and acceptance boundaries, status policy, private evidence and next console handoff. Work type and Priority supplement Status, Milestone and Waiting resource. New filtered views cover Delivery gates, Implementation work, Evidence handoff and Pull requests. Source requirements initialize empty fields only; Accepted is reserved for reviewed full milestone overviews. See the overview template.

python3 tools/flesh-github-project.py --output artifacts/github-project-plan.json
python3 tools/flesh-github-project.py --apply --output artifacts/github-project-result.json

applied, live_project_verified, completed operations and partial-change flags state what actually happened. A denied read cannot establish a live board. As of this source increment the personal secret requirement is saved, but the environment reports has_saved_binding:false; its Project read still uses the limited integration and returns Resource not accessible by integration.

Project-backed progress¶

Project #2 is the authority for Status, Waiting resource and full-gate acceptance. Its seven overview issues and 35 focused tasks, detailed overview, work-type/priority fields and filtered views are populated and verified through the repository Actions secret. Existing owner values and notes are preserved. GitHub's default Todo option is shown as Planned in the aggregate without changing the option or its ID.

The tracking workflow reads Project fields hourly and on request, then publishes an allowlisted JSON snapshot, stacked milestone chart and interactive dashboard. Bars show five work items per milestone; completed children never automatically accept a full gate. Chart timestamps are visible; the dashboard marks snapshots older than two hours stale and preserves the previous update time on read errors. GitHub can cache README images; open the dashboard for current freshness.

The public docs publisher runs reviewed immutable main source, requires a strict searchable documentation build and verifies the docs-only archive before any write. It can explicitly create only Jdiller-86/CTR-2-WUP-docs as public, configure free workflow-based Pages and publish its generated site. Application visibility never changes. Both publishers preserve existing tree contents and update main without force. Progress lives in site/progress/, inside the Pages upload root. The documentation site and interactive dashboard are live. Their HTML, aggregate JSON and chart return HTTP 200; publication and Pages Actions succeeded. The README embeds the generated chart.

Only seven documented milestone names, aggregate counts, accepted-gate counts, fixed waiting-resource categories, freshness and the Project link enter the public snapshot. No private issue title/body/number, item ID, owner note, ROM, key or trace is exported. Unknown custom status/resource values remain in fixed Other buckets.

Configure CTR2WUP_GITHUB_MANAGEMENT_TOKEN in repository Actions secrets with Project and the required repository/Pages permissions. Cloud-environment secrets are separate; the Actions route is verified, while cloud Project calls still use a limited integration. Tokens are never requested in chat or copied into reports. Use trusted-main Project tracking for setup/export and Publish public documentation for docs-only publication. Neither runs untrusted PR code with the management token. Dry-run tools are available for reviewing mutations.

Repository settings and authentication¶

Normal Git, issues, milestones, PRs and Actions use the connected integration. Its direct cloud Project/administration calls remain limited. Trusted-main management workflows use the separate repository Actions secret CTR2WUP_GITHUB_MANAGEMENT_TOKEN; this route has populated Project #2 and published the docs-only Pages site. No further token entry is needed for those verified operations.

Repository features applies the requested About/documentation URL, topics, Discussions, merged-branch cleanup and free Dependabot alerts, then verifies the result through the API. Project tracking owns board setup, explicit reviewed task transitions and public aggregates. Publish public documentation verifies the docs-only export before publication. Each preserves operation receipts. The desired settings specify mutation scope; actual API receipts establish which operations succeeded.

Only trusted immutable main code executes with the management token. Values are consumed normally by the GitHub CLI and never copied into reports, source or chat. Existing owner board values remain authoritative; source synchronization cannot overwrite them. No branch protection or paid feature is enabled, and application repository visibility remains private.
